This page features the official bookplates of the library. Below the institution names is an illustrative woodcut of an armillary sphere <term>armillary sphere: a model of the celestial sphere with rings representing the great circles of the heavens</term> supported by a kneeling figure, likely Atlas. The sphere includes the following astronomical markers:

*   **Meridian** <note>original: "Meridianus"</note> — the vertical line at the top.
*   **Solstitial colure** <note>original: "Colurus solstitiorum"; the great circle passing through the celestial poles and the solstices.</note> — the vertical interior ring.
*   **Arctic Circle** <note>original: "Circulus Articus"</note> — the uppermost horizontal ring.
*   **Tropic of Cancer** <note>original: "Cancretropicus"</note> — the upper-middle horizontal ring.
*   **Equinoctial colure** <note>original: "Colurus Aequi"; the great circle passing through the celestial poles and the equinoxes.</note> — the diagonal ring.
*   **Zodiac** <note>original: "Zodiacus"</note> — the broad, central diagonal band showing the path of the sun.
*   **Oblique horizon** <note>original: "Horizon ob"</note> — the central horizontal ring.
*   **Tropic of Capricorn** <note>original: "Capricornus circulus"</note> — the lower-middle horizontal ring.
*   **Antarctic Circle** <note>original: "Antarctic circu"</note> — the lowest horizontal ring.

The figure supporting the heavens kneels upon a small patch of earth, signifying the terrestrial world beneath the celestial movements.

<meta>This is the title page for a work by the 10th-century astrologer Alchabitius. The original physical page shows signs of damage, specifically a large brown stain or burn mark on the upper right edge.</meta>

# Alchabitius with commentary.
<note>Alchabitius is the Latinized name of the Arab astrologer and mathematician al-Qabisi. His work was a fundamental textbook for students of astrology in European universities during the Middle Ages and the Renaissance.</note>

## With favor and privilege.
<note>original: "Cum gratia et privilegio." This legal statement indicates that the printer was granted exclusive rights by a ruler or authority to print this book, preventing others from copying it for a certain period of time—an early form of copyright.</note>

-><note>The center of the page features a detailed woodcut illustration of an armillary sphere. This device acts as a skeletal map of the heavens. The labels on the rings include the **horizon**, the **Arctic circle**, the **Tropic of Cancer**, the **Zenith** (the point directly above the observer), the **Equinoctial** (the celestial equator), the **Tropic of Capricorn**, and the **Antarctic circle**. A broad, diagonal band represents the **oblique** path of the sun (the ecliptic) and contains the symbols for the twelve signs of the zodiac: the Ram, the Bull, the Twins, the Crab, the Lion, the Virgin, the Scales, the Scorpion, the Archer, the Goat, the Water-Bearer, and the Fish.</note><-

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues from the title page, moving from the decorative frontispiece into the formal opening of the text. It introduces the author, the translator, and the medieval commentator, establishing the book's purpose as a foundational guide to astrology.</meta>

### The introductory treatise of Abd al-Aziz—that is, the glorious servant of God—who is called Alchabitius, on the mastery of the judgments of the stars. Translated by John of Seville and with the commentary on the same work edited by John of Saxony, connected in a useful sequence, now begins.
<note>Alchabitius is the Latinized name of the 10th-century Arab astrologer Al-Qabisi. "Judgments of the stars" is a historical term for judicial astrology—the practice of making specific predictions based on planetary positions.</note>

<image-desc>A large ornamental drop cap 'P' at the beginning of the text block. The letter is elaborately decorated with white-on-black floral and vine patterns. Within the lower curve of the 'P', there is a stylized bird or griffin creature with wings.</image-desc>

Having asked the Lord for long life for Sayf al-Dawla <note>original: "ceypha dala," meaning "Sword of the State," the Hamdanid Emir of Aleppo and patron of Al-Qabisi.</note>—that is, the Sword of the Realm—and for the endurance of his honor, the protection of his works and good deeds, and the extension of his empire: let us begin that which we wish to relate. When I saw that a collection of certain ancients among the authors of the <term>mastery of the judgments of the stars</term> <note>This refers to the professional skill of interpreting celestial omens.</note> had published books which they called "introductions" to this mastery—that is, to the judgments of the stars—I noticed that some of them had not diligently examined everything necessary for an introductory work. Others, however, presented the necessary material with excessive wordiness, and I saw that because of this, much of what is essential had been lost. I also observed that some had not followed a proper educational path in the organization of what they produced. Therefore, I began to publish this book; I have established it as an introduction and have collected within it from the sayings of the ancients whatever is necessary for this mastery in an introductory format. I have not included the complex reasonings or arguments necessary for the defense of these principles, since those can be found in the book of Ptolemy called the *Four Treatises* <note>original: "alarbamacalet," from the Arabic *Al-Arba' Maqalat*, known in Greek as the *Tetrabiblos*, the foundational text of Western astrology.</note> and in my own book which I published to confirm the mastery of the judgments of the stars, as well as in my refutation of the letter of Ali ibn Ridwan <note>original: "haiſſebenhali," referring to the Egyptian physician and astrologer.</note> where I nullified his arguments with sufficient reasoning. I have divided this work into five sections <note>original: "differentias," a term used here to mean distinct chapters or divisions of a subject.</note>:

1.  **The first section** concerns the essential and accidental nature of the circle of signs <note>the Zodiac</note>.
2.  **The second section** concerns the natures of the seven planets: what is unique to them and what they signify.
3.  **The third section** concerns what happens to the seven planets in themselves and what happens when they interact with one another.
4.  **The fourth section** concerns the explanation of the technical terms used by astrologers.
5.  **The fifth section** concerns the totality of the "Parts" <note>Arabic: *Sahm*; these are calculated mathematical points in a horoscope, like the "Part of Fortune."</note> and the explanation of their placement within the degrees of the signs.

->Section One.<-

<summary>This page contains the prologue to Alchabitius's influential astrological introduction, dedicated to his patron Sayf al-Dawla. It outlines the author's intent to provide a concise, well-organized textbook for students, divided into five clear sections.</summary>
<keywords>Alchabitius, Al-Qabisi, Sayf al-Dawla, John of Seville, John of Saxony, judicial astrology, Ptolemy, Tetrabiblos, zodiac, planets, astrological parts</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page follows the introductory greeting and justification for the book. It transitions from the author's preface to the actual technical instruction of astrology, beginning with the fundamental geometry of the heavens.</meta>

## On the division of the circle of signs

<image-desc>A large decorative drop cap 'N' within a square border, featuring intricate floral and vine patterns in a woodcut style.</image-desc>

The Zodiac <note>original: "Notach," a Latinized term for the zodiacal circle</note>, which is the circle of signs, is divided into twelve equal parts according to the division of the zodiacal circle. These twelve parts are called "signs" and refer to the constellations found beneath that same zodiacal circle, which are: Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces. Each of these signs is divided into thirty equal parts called "degrees." A degree is divided into sixty "minutes," a minute into sixty "seconds," and a second into sixty "thirds." Similarly, those that follow—namely fourths and fifths—continue upward to infinity.

Six of these signs are northern—that is, from the beginning of Aries to the end of Virgo—because they are north of the <term>equinoctial line</term> <note>The celestial equator, the imaginary line in the sky directly above the Earth's equator.</note>. Six are southern—that is, from the beginning of Libra to the end of Pisces. Furthermore, six of them are said to be "direct in ascension," from the beginning of Cancer to the end of Sagittarius. Six are called "tortuous in ascension" <note>This refers to the "Signs of Long and Short Ascension." Because of the tilt of the Earth's axis, some signs take longer to rise over the horizon than others.</note>—namely, from the beginning of Capricorn to the end of Gemini.

The "tortuous" signs are said to "obey" the "direct" signs. This means that two signs located at an equal distance from the "head" of Cancer obey one another, such as Gemini obeying Cancer, Taurus obeying Leo, Aries obeying Virgo, Pisces obeying Libra, Aquarius obeying Scorpio, and Capricorn obeying Sagittarius. 

Additionally, two signs located at an equal distance from the head of Aries are said to be "in agreement in their journey," such as Aries and Pisces, Taurus and Aquarius, Gemini and Capricorn, Cancer and Sagittarius, Leo and Scorpio, and Virgo and Libra.

The half of the circle from the beginning of Leo to the end of Capricorn is called the "Major" <note>original: "maria," likely a corruption of "maior"</note> half, and it is the half of the Sun, because the Sun has its primary influence throughout this half, just as planets have power within their own <term>terms</term> <note>Specific degrees of a sign where a planet is thought to have extra strength.</note>. The other half, from the beginning of Aquarius to the end of Cancer, is called the "Minor" <note>original: "minima"</note> half, and it is the half of the Moon, for the Moon similarly has primary influence in all of this half, just as the Sun does in the Major half. 

The half from the beginning of Aries to the end of Virgo is called the "warm half," and the other from the beginning of Libra to the end of Pisces is called the "cold half." 

The quarter of the circle from the beginning of Aries to the end of Gemini is called the warm and moist quarter, associated with spring, childhood, and the <term>sanguine</term> temperament. The quarter from the beginning of Cancer to the end of Virgo is called the warm and dry quarter, associated with summer, youth, and the <term>choleric</term> temperament. The quarter from the beginning of Libra to the end of Sagittarius is called the cold and dry quarter, associated with autumn and the <term>melancholic</term> temperament; it signifies the beginning of the decline of middle age. The quarter from the beginning of Capricorn to the end of Pisces is called the cold and moist quarter, associated with decline, old age, winter, and the <term>phlegmatic</term> <note>original: "flatica," likely a contraction or misspelling of "phlegmatica"</note> temperament. 

Regarding all the planets that move through these signs: it is not that they are literally *inside* the signs, but that they move *under* them—that is, in a direct line with them. Saturn is the highest and furthest from the circle of signs and the slowest in its course; then follows Jupiter, then Mars, then the Sun, then Venus, then Mercury, and finally the Moon, which is closest to the Earth and faster than all the others in its course. Certain meanings are also signified by the "Head of the Dragon" and the "Tail of the Dragon" <note>These are the North and South Lunar Nodes—the points where the Moon's path crosses the Sun's path.</note> alongside the planets, as we shall explain in what follows.

## On the houses of the planets.

<image-desc>A small decorative woodcut drop cap 'H' featuring two stylized columns or towers on either side of the letter.</image-desc>

The planets also possess powers within these signs: some by nature and some by accident. Those that are by nature are these: Houses, Exaltations...

<summary>This page defines the basic structure of the Zodiac, dividing it into signs, degrees, and minutes, while classifying the signs by their seasonal, temperamental, and planetary properties. It also establishes the hierarchical order of the seven traditional planets and the lunar nodes.</summary>
<keywords>Zodiac, signs, degrees, Northern signs, Southern signs, direct ascension, oblique ascension, four humors, planetary order, Head of the Dragon, Tail of the Dragon, Alchabitius</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the zodiac, moving from the physical measurements of the signs to their astrological properties and "dignities"—the systems used to determine which planets have the most influence in specific parts of the sky.</meta>

...tion, Term, Triplicity, and Face. <note>These are the five "Essential Dignities" used to calculate a planet's strength in a specific location.</note> Regarding those qualities which occur by accident, we shall discuss them in the appropriate place.

The **Houses** <note>Also called "domiciles," these are the signs where a planet is most "at home" and powerful.</note> are as follows: **Aries** and **Scorpio** are the houses of **Mars**. **Taurus** and **Libra** are the houses of **Venus**. **Gemini** and **Virgo** are the houses of **Mercury**. **Cancer** is the house of the **Moon**. **Leo** is the house of the **Sun**. **Sagittarius** and **Pisces** are the houses of **Jupiter**. **Capricorn** and **Aquarius** are the houses of **Saturn**. 

<margin>Planetary joys</margin>
The seventh sign from the house of any planet is said to be that same planet’s **detriment** <note>original: "detrimentum." A sign where the planet is weakened because it is opposite its home sign.</note>. And if two signs are the houses of one planet, they are said to be "concordant in the belt" <note>original: "almantica," from the Arabic "al-mantiqah," referring to the zodiacal belt or the celestial equator.</note>; that is, in the circle which is wide in the middle and narrow at the binding, which signifies the zodiacal circle.

Those signs which planets are said to **rejoice** in when they enter them—and their masters according to Dorotheus <note>Dorotheus of Sidon, a highly influential 1st-century astrologer.</note>—are these: **Saturn** is said to rejoice when he enters **Aquarius**. **Jupiter** in **Sagittarius**. **Mars** in **Scorpio**. **Venus** in **Taurus**, and **Mercury** in **Virgo**.

->### On the exaltations of the planets<-

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'H' featuring floral and scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>
These are the **exaltations** <note>A sign where a planet's influence is exaggerated or elevated to its highest honor.</note> of the planets. The **Sun** is exalted in **Aries**, specifically in its 18th degree. The **Moon** in the 3rd degree of **Taurus**. **Saturn** in the 21st degree of **Libra**. **Jupiter** in the 15th degree of **Cancer**. **Mars** in the 27th degree of **Capricorn**. **Venus** in the 27th degree of **Pisces**. **Mercury** in the 15th degree of **Virgo**.

<margin>Planetary falls</margin>
The **Dragon’s Head** <note>The North Node of the Moon.</note> is in the 4th degree of **Gemini**, and the **Dragon’s Tail** <note>The South Node of the Moon.</note> is in the 4th degree of **Sagittarius**. In the seventh sign from the exaltation of any planet, at the same degree, will be its **fall** <note>original: "descensio" or "casus." The point where a planet is most weakened or "shamed."</note>. For example: just as the Sun is exalted in the 18th degree of Aries, it falls in the 18th degree of Libra; and so it is for the others. However, Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century authority on astronomy.</note> posits that the entirety of Aries is the exaltation of the Sun, and the entirety of Taurus is the exaltation of the Moon, and so on for the rest.

->### On the triplicities<-

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'T' featuring floral and scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>
We distinguish the **triplicities** <note>Groups of three signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water).</note> in this way: because three signs which are seen to agree in one nature make a triplicity, they are called by that same name. 

Therefore, **Aries**, **Leo**, and **Sagittarius** make the **first triplicity** because each of these signs is fiery, masculine, diurnal, hot, and dry (choleric), and bitter in flavor. This triplicity is also Eastern. Its lords are the **Sun** by day, **Jupiter** by night, and their partner by day and night is **Saturn**.

The **second triplicity** consists of **Taurus**, **Virgo**, and **Capricorn**, because these are feminine, nocturnal, earthy, cold, and dry (melancholic), sharp in flavor, and Southern. The lords of this triplicity are **Venus** by day and the **Moon** by night, and their partner by day and night is **Mars**.

The **third triplicity** consists of **Gemini**, **Libra**, and **Aquarius**, because these signs are masculine, diurnal, sanguine, hot, moist, airy, sweet in flavor, and Western. The lords of this triplicity are **Saturn** by day and **Mercury** by night; their partner by day and night is **Jupiter**.

The **fourth triplicity** is made of **Cancer**, **Scorpio**, and **Pisces**, because these are feminine, nocturnal, northern, aquatic, phlegmatic <note>original: "flatica," likely a corruption of "fleuatica" or "pituita."</note>, cold, moist, and salty in flavor. The lords of this triplicity are **Venus** by day and **Mars** by night, and their partner by day and night is the **Moon**.

<summary>This page defines the fundamental strengths (dignities) of the planets, listing their home signs, exaltations, and elemental groupings known as triplicities. It establishes the basic "rules" for how planets interact with specific signs in a birth chart.</summary>

## On the movable, fixed, and common signs.

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'Q' featuring a stylized flower with leaves inside a square frame.</image-desc>
Four of these signs are also said to be **movable** <note>Also known as "cardinal" signs, they mark the start of the seasons.</note>, namely: Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn. And four are **fixed**: namely Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius. The remaining four—Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces—are **common** <note>Now usually called "mutable" signs, they occur as one season transitions to the next.</note>. They are called movable, fixed, or common because when the Sun enters one of them, the time is either "moved" (that is, the season changes), or it is "fixed" (that is, it persists in the same state), or it becomes "common" (that is, half of that season will be like half of the next). For example: when the Sun enters the sign of **Aries**, the time is changed, meaning winter turns into spring. When it enters **Taurus**, that same spring-time is fixed and established. When, however, the Sun enters **Gemini**, the time becomes common—that is, half spring and half summer—and so it goes for the others.

## On the aspects of the planets.

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'D' featuring floral flourishes within a square frame.</image-desc>
<margin>
fourth
fourth
</margin>
The signs are also said to "look at" one another <note>original: "aspicere," the root of the word "aspect." This refers to geometric relationships between signs.</note>: that is, every sign looks at the third sign before it and the third sign after it (which is the eleventh from itself). This aspect is called **sextile**, and it is an aspect of affection and moderate friendship. It is called sextile because it holds a sixth part of the circle (that is, 60 degrees). For example: a planet that is at the beginning of Aries looks at one that is in Gemini before it and one in Aquarius after it, which is an aspect of friendship.

It also looks at the fourth sign before it and the fourth after it (the tenth sign); this aspect is called **tetragonus** <note>The "square" aspect.</note> because it holds a fourth part of the heaven (that is, 90 degrees). This is an aspect of discord and moderate enmity. It also looks at the fifth sign before it and the fifth after it (the ninth sign); this aspect is called **trigon** <note>The "trine" aspect.</note> or a "three-angled radiation," because it holds a third part of the heaven (that is, 120 degrees). This is an aspect of perfect concord and love.

Finally, it looks at the seventh sign by **opposition**, and this is an aspect of perfect enmity. If planets are in signs that "look at" each other in this way, they are said to "be in aspect." If two planets are in a single sign, they are said to be **conjoined**. When a planet is in any sign, its rays will be in those signs that look at the sign itself, at the same degree and minute. For example: if Mars is in the first degree of Aries and in the first minute of that degree, his rays will be in the first degree of the sign of Libra and in the first minute of that same degree; and understand the other aspects in this same way, as is most clearly shown in this figure.

### On the Terms of the Planets.

**T**here are also <term>terms</term> <note>original: *termini* or *fines*; these are specific sub-divisions of each zodiac sign that grant a planet extra influence or "dignity" when it is located within those degrees.</note> or boundaries of the planets within the signs. For in every sign, five planets hold terms arranged through various <term>degrees</term> <note>Each zodiac sign consists of exactly 30 degrees.</note>. For instance, from the beginning of Aries up to its sixth degree is the term of Jupiter; from the sixth to the twelfth is the term of Venus; from the twelfth to the twentieth is the term of Mercury; from the twentieth to the twenty-fifth is the term of Mars; and the remaining five degrees belong to Saturn. Because of the variety of these degrees and the difficulty of memorizing them, we have recorded them in a table to make the work easier. These are known as the **Egyptian Terms** <note>original: *Termini egyptiorum*. This was the most common system of terms used in the Middle Ages, often attributed to the mythical sage Hermes Trismegistus.</note> and are said to belong to **Hermes**.

| Sign | 1st Planet | Deg. | 2nd Planet | Deg. | 3rd Planet | Deg. | 4th Planet | Deg. | 5th Planet | Deg. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Aries** | Jupiter | 6 | Venus | 6 | Mercury | 8 | Mars | 5 | Saturn | 5 |
| **Taurus** | Venus | 8 | Mercury | 6 | Jupiter | 8 | Saturn | 5 | Mars | 3 |
| **Gemini** | Mercury | 6 | Jupiter | 6 | Venus | 5 | Mars | 7 | Saturn | 6 |
| **Cancer** | Mars | 7 | Venus | 6 | Mercury | 6 | Jupiter | 7 | Saturn | 4 |
| **Leo** | Jupiter | 6 | Venus | 5 | Saturn | 7 | Mercury | 6 | Mars | 6 |
| **Virgo** | Mercury | 7 | Venus | 10 | Jupiter | 4 | Mars | 7 | Saturn | 2 |
| **Libra** | Saturn | 6 | Mercury | 8 | Jupiter | 7 | Venus | 7 | Mars | 2 |
| **Scorpio** | Mars | 7 | Venus | 4 | Mercury | 8 | Jupiter | 5 | Saturn | 6 |
| **Sagittarius** | Jupiter | 12 | Venus | 5 | Mercury | 4 | Saturn | 5 | Mars | 4 |
| **Capricorn** | Mercury | 7 | Jupiter | 7 | Venus | 8 | Saturn | 4 | Mars | 4 |

<summary>This page introduces the "Egyptian Terms," a system that divides each 30-degree zodiac sign into five unequal segments assigned to the five classical planets (excluding the Sun and Moon). It includes a detailed reference table to help the practitioner determine which planet governs a specific degree of a sign.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary "dignities"—the systems by which astrologers determine how much influence a planet has based on its exact position in the zodiac. It follows the previous discussion of "terms" and introduces "faces" (decans) and the scoring system for planetary strength.</meta>

->Where triplicities are placed before terms.<-

<note>There is a significant dark stain or discoloration on the right edge of the page, though it does not obscure any text.</note>

**S**ome, however, place the <term>triplicities</term> <note>Groups of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element: Fire, Earth, Air, or Water.</note> after the <term>terms</term> <note>Specific subdivisions of a sign where a planet gains power.</note> because the lords of the triplicities are stronger in "nutrition" <note>original: *nutritione*; in a medical or horary context, this refers to the planet's ability to "feed" or sustain the matter at hand.</note>: because it signifies nourishment: and because there is no discord in the triplicity, as there is in the terms when they are placed. And some place the terms before the triplicities, because the lords of the terms are stronger in <term>direction</term> <note>original: *directione*; a predictive technique where degrees are "moved" forward in time to determine when events will occur.</note>.

### On the faces of the signs and to which planet each is attributed.

The <term>faces</term> <note>original: *facies*; also known as decans, these are 10-degree segments of a sign.</note> of the signs are these: every sign is divided into three equal parts; each part consists of 10 degrees and is called a "face," the beginning of which is at the first degree of Aries. Therefore, the first face is from the first degree of Aries up to the tenth, and it is given to **Mars**. The second is up to the twentieth degree, and it is given to the **Sun**, who follows him in the order of the spheres. The third is up to the end of the aforementioned sign, and it belongs to **Venus**. Similarly, the first face of Taurus belongs to **Mercury**, who follows Venus; and so on until the end of the signs.

Therefore, when you have a degree in any sign and you wish to know which planet's face it belongs to: take the whole signs from the beginning of Aries up to the sign you wish to know, and triple them, and to this result add the faces that have passed within that sign for which you wish to know the face, including the face whose lord you wish to know. Then count <note>original: *proiice*; literally "throw out" or calculate by casting.</note> from the planet who is the lord of the face of the sign of Aries, which is Mars, following the succession of the circles—7, 7, 7—and whatever remains below seven, count from Mars through the succession of the planets. Wherever the number ends, that will be the lord of the first face of that sign. From 1 to 10 of that same sign belongs to the planet where the count ended; from 10 to 20 belongs to the next planet that follows it; and from 20 to the end of the sign belongs to the next planet after that. The planet in whose ten-degree section the degree of the sign falls will be the lord of that face. These are also called "lords of the faces" and by some <term>decans</term> <note>original: *decani*; from the Greek word for "ten."</note>, for which the table follows.

| In | Sign | 1st Face (10°) | | 2nd Face (10°) | | 3rd Face (10°) | |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| In | Aries | Mars | 10 | Sun | 10 | Venus | 10 |
| In | Taurus | Mercury | 10 | Moon | 10 | Saturn | 10 |
| In | Gemini | Jupiter | 10 | Mars | 10 | Sun | 10 |
| In | Cancer | Venus | 10 | Mercury | 10 | Moon | 10 |
| In | Leo | Saturn | 10 | Jupiter | 10 | Mars | 10 |
| In | Virgo | Sun | 10 | Venus | 10 | Mercury | 10 |
| In | Libra | Moon | 10 | Saturn | 10 | Jupiter | 10 |
| In | Scorpio | Mars | 10 | Sun | 10 | Venus | 10 |
| In | Sagittarius | Mercury | 10 | Moon | 10 | Saturn | 10 |
| In | Capricorn | Jupiter | 10 | Mars | 10 | Sun | 10 |
| In | Aquarius | Venus | 10 | Mercury | 10 | Moon | 10 |
| In | Pisces | Saturn | 10 | Jupiter | 10 | Mars | 10 |

### On the virtues of the planets.

And since we have now, by God's grace, treated the powers of the planets in the signs—which are House, Exaltation, Triplicity, Term, and Face—let us now treat their "virtues" or "fortitudes" <note>This refers to the weighted scoring system used to calculate a planet's total "Essential Dignity."</note> within them. For the lord of the **House** has 5 fortitudes; the lord of the **Exaltation** has 4; the lord of the **Triplicity** has 3; the lord of the **Term** has 2; and the lord of the **Face** has 1. Understand and count from this...

...the strengths of the planets. For the more they abound in number <note>Referring to the numerical "points" or "scores" a planet earns for being in a favorable position.</note>, the more they abound in strength. And some place the <term>term</term> before the <term>triplicity</term>—that is, they wish for the lord of the term to be a companion to the lord of the triplicity. However, we have already explained above the value of each one.

<margin><image-desc>A manicule (pointing hand) points to the start of this paragraph.</image-desc> Some have provided a comparison for this matter, saying that when a planet is in its own **house** <note>The specific sign a planet naturally rules.</note>, it is like a man in his own home and lordship. When it is in its **exaltation** <note>A specific sign where a planet's influence is amplified to its highest degree.</note>, it is like a man in his kingdom and glory. When it is in its **term**, it is like a man among his parents, relatives, and kin. When it is in its **triplicity**, it is like a man in his honor, surrounded by his helpers and ministers. And when it is in its **face** <note>Also called a decan; a 10-degree segment of a sign.</note>, it is like a man in his craft or mastery. These are all the powers of the planets which are essential within the signs, and the figures of the signs follow these.</margin>

->On the essences of the signs and what they signify in themselves.<-

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'V' (for Via) decorated with scrolling acanthus leaves and a central rose window motif.</image-desc>**A**mong the signs, there are certain ones called "rational" <note>original: *rationalia*; signs representing human figures, believed to grant intelligence or speech.</note>: namely, **Virgo**, **Gemini**, **Libra**, **Aquarius**, and the first half of **Sagittarius**. Because their images in the circle are shaped like the images of humans, these are also said to be signs possessing "beautiful voices." These also thrive when they are in the East. And some are said to "possess wings": namely, Gemini, Virgo, and Pisces. And some are "four-footed": such as Leo and Sagittarius. Some of them are "domestic": such as Aries, Taurus, and Capricorn; and these thrive when they are in the South. However, Virgo, Capricorn, and Aquarius thrive when they are in the North. And among the signs, some are "tortuous" <note>original: *tortuosa*; signs that rise slowly or at an angle on the horizon.</note> and "faulty": namely, Aries, Taurus, Cancer, Scorpio, and Capricorn. And some of them are said to have "many offspring": namely, Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces; and these thrive when they are in the West. And some of them are "sterile": such as Gemini, Leo, and Virgo. And some are those having "few children": such as Aries, Taurus, Libra, Sagittarius, Capricorn, and Aquarius. And some are said to be "greatly lustful": namely, Aries, Taurus, Leo, and Capricorn. And among the signs, some are said to have a "medium voice"—those which are formed in the images of animals that bleat, low, or roar: such as Aries, Taurus, Leo, Capricorn, and the last part of Sagittarius. And some are "voiceless": namely, those which are formed in the images of animals lacking a voice: such as Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.

->On the significations of the signs.<-

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'I' (for In) featuring a standing figure in long robes, possibly a scholar, holding an object or gesturing.</image-desc>**E**ach and every sign has its own proper signification regarding what they signify from the creation of the limbs, the character of men, regions, seeds, trees, and so forth. **Aries** governs the head and face of the human body. From the regions, it governs Babylon and Persia <note>original: *feriz*</note>, that is, Babylonia, Persia, Arabia, and Palestine. **Taurus** governs trees that are planted; and from the human body, it governs the neck and the knot of the throat; and from the regions, it governs Ezetimet, Almechim, Sande, and Araterad <note>Transliterations of Arabic geographical terms common in medieval Latin astrology.</note>. **Gemini** is a sign of a generous and good soul, and from the human body it governs the shoulders, arms, and hands; and from the regions, it governs Vigeti, Armenia, Nuthrabigen, and Elenimistet—that is, Egypt and Bartha. **Cancer** governs trees of equal length; and from the human body, the chest, heart, stomach, ribs, spleen, and lungs; and from the regions, Lesser Armenia and the Eastern region, Buthabe, Masses, and Acin; and it has a share in Barach and Atrabicen. **Leo** governs tall trees—that is, long ones. It is hot and cunning, and of much distress and sadness. From the human body, it governs the stomach, heart, back, and side; and from the regions, Artritri unto the end of the habitable world. **Virgo** governs whatever is sown from seeds and is a sign of a generous and good soul. From the human body, it governs the belly and the internal parts, namely the membrane—that is, the diaphragm—and the intestines; and from the regions, Argunienta and...

...Assem <note>Likely a transliteration of an Arabic geographical term.</note>, which is a certain region near Jerusalem, and Alforas, which is the Euphrates, and the island that is Spain, and Festir or Aseum. **Libra** has tall trees and is generous and of a good spirit; from the human body it governs the loins and the lower parts of the belly, the navel, the pubic area, the genitals, the hips, the flanks, and the buttocks. From the regions of the world, it governs the land of the Romans or the Greeks and those neighboring lands that follow them as far as Africa; it also governs Arasse or Azim as far as the end of Ethiopia, and Barthan; it also governs Carmen and Fegestem, and Thebes or Thebil, and Tabrasten Barah and Ambarath.

**Scorpio** has trees of equal height and is generous and of a good spirit; from the human body it governs the genitals, the testicles, the bladder, the anus, and the thighs. From the regions of the world, it governs Helebiget, the rural lands of the Arabs, and its borders as far as Allegem; it also has a share in Alchach.

**Sagittarius** is ingenious and hot; from the human body it governs the thighs. From the regions of the world, it governs Ethiopia, Maharahen, and Achiut (also called Acenit or Achichit), which is India.

**Capricorn** is of a good life but is irritable, cautious, and hot, having much sadness; from the human body it governs the knees. From the regions of the world, it governs Ethiopia, Annaban Alcon, Acuith, and Mahamen as far as the two seas of Etabant, and Achuit, which is India.

**Aquarius** governs from the human body the shins down to the lower parts of the ankles <note>original: *cauillarum idest tallorum*</note>. From the regions of the world, it governs Azenes—which means "blackness" <note>likely referring to Sub-Saharan Africa</note>—and Althupha and its parts, the land of the Elfies, part of the land of Egypt, the western region, and the land of Achiut.

**Pisces** is hot and cautious, of a mixed and many-colored nature; from the human body it governs the feet. From the regions of the world, it governs Carabiarem, the northern region of the land of Iurgem, and has a share in the Roman lands as far as Effen; it also governs the island <note>perhaps Britain or Sicily</note>, Egypt, Alexandria, and the Lycian Sea.

### On the significance of the planets in the signs according to the members of man.

->On the bright, dark, smoky, and empty degrees.<-

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'E' featuring scrolling foliage within a square frame.</image-desc>
And in each of these signs there are degrees which are called bright <note>original: *lucidi*</note>; and degrees which are called dark <note>original: *tenebrosi*</note>; and degrees which are called smoky <note>original: *fumosi*</note>; and degrees which are called empty <note>original: *vacui*</note>. ->On the *azemena* degrees, that is, of the body's infirmity.<-

And there are in the signs certain degrees called *azemena* <note>An Arabic-derived term (from *al-zamāna*) referring to degrees that indicate chronic illness or physical disability.</note> degrees, that is, degrees of bodily infirmity. For *azemena* is a certain lifelong <note>original: *tpalis*, likely *perpetualis* or *temporalis* meaning "during the course of one's life."</note> weakness of the body, such as deafness, blindness, loss of a limb, and other such things that a person will always have for as long as they live, unless they are healed miraculously. Therefore, when the Moon is in these degrees at the birth <note>original: *natiuitate*</note> of any child, the aforementioned infirmity will happen to them according to the significance of the [astrological] place and the aspect or the positions of the planets. This infirmity is also signified by the stars in various ways, just as you will find in the books of nativities <note>Horoscopes or birth records.</note>. Moreover, the *azemena* degrees are those described here in the table.

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of zodiacal degrees. Having previously discussed "pit" degrees (special degrees of weakness), the author now shifts to "corresponding" degrees—those that share equal power based on their distance from the cardinal points. This page also transitions from the "essential" qualities of the zodiac signs themselves to the "accidental" qualities of the houses, which are determined by the specific time and location of an observation.</meta>

### On corresponding degrees.

Two degrees that are of the same distance from the start of the cardinal <note>original: *signorum mobilium*, literally "movable signs," which are Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn.</note> signs are called **corresponding** <note>original: *competentes*, meaning they match in strength</note>, that is, of one strength and **partners** or **sharers** in power. For example: the 20th degree of Capricorn or Cancer with the 10th degree of Sagittarius or Gemini; and the 20th degree of Aries or Libra with the 10th degree of Pisces or Virgo. <note>This refers to the concept of "antiscia"—degrees that are reflections of one another across the solstice or equinox axes, resulting in equal day or night lengths.</note>

->On the accidental nature of the circle.<-

But since, with God’s help, we have already presented the **essential** nature of the circle of signs, let us now present the **accidental**. For the circle is shaped at every hour in such a figure that it is divided into four parts: these are divided by the circle of the hemisphere <note>the horizon</note> and the circle of the meridian, which is the circle of the "middle of the sky" <term>Midheaven|The highest point in the sky that the sun or a planet reaches during its daily journey; the cusp of the 10th house.</term>, which makes the middle of the

<note>The original manuscript uses a square diagram where the "houses" are triangular segments. The cardinal points (1, 4, 7, and 10) are the strongest "angles."</note>

day. Each of these parts is divided into three unequal parts according to the ascensions of the rising sign, and in this way the circle is divided into twelve parts which are called **houses**; they are also named **cusps** and **towers**. The use of these is explained in the *Book of Ezibi* <note>original: *libro Ezibi*. This refers to the *Zij*, a type of Arabic astronomical treatise or set of tables, likely those of Al-Khwarizmi or Al-Battani.</note>, that is, in the *Book of the Course of the Stars*. 

The beginning of this division is the **horoscope** <term>Horoscope|In medieval astrology, this term does not mean the whole chart, but specifically the Ascendant—the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment.</term>. This is the Ascendant, whose beginning is upon the circle of the eastern hemisphere; then follows the second house and the third, and the rest of the houses up to the twelfth. 

The first quarter, which is from the Ascendant up to the Midheaven—which contains the twelfth, eleventh, and tenth houses—is called the **eastern masculine quarter**. Its arrival signifies the beginning of life, and it is called **childhood**, **sanguine**, and **vernal** <note>This quarter is associated with the qualities of spring: heat, moisture, and the element of Air.</note>. And the next part, which is from the Midheaven up to the degree...

<meta>This page continues the technical division of the heavens into houses. Having established the "corresponding degrees" on the previous page, the author now explains how the circle is divided into four quadrants (the "accidental" nature of the sky) and begins a house-by-house analysis of human life.</meta>

...while [the part] of the western sign, that is the seventh, which is above the circle of the western hemisphere, contains the ninth, eighth, and seventh houses: this is the southern feminine receding part. It signifies middle age and is called the **perfection of summer youth**, being choleric <note>One of the four humors, associated with fire, heat, and a quick-tempered nature.</note> in nature. ¶ The third part also, which is from the west up to the degree of the fourth house, which is above the circle of the meridian below the earth—containing the sixth, fifth, and fourth houses—is western masculine approaching. It signifies the end of life and is called **autumnal**, melancholic, elderly, and corresponds to a moderate age. ¶ The fourth part, which is from the fourth house up to the Ascendant—containing the third and second houses as well as the Ascendant—is northern feminine receding. It signifies what happens to a person after their death and what becomes of their body or the disposition of the property left behind; or what will be said about them, namely whether they are praised or blamed. This part is called **elderly, phlegmatic, failing, and wintery**. And those two parts which are from the middle of the sky <note>The Midheaven, or the highest point of the sun's path.</note> to the Ascendant, and from the Ascendant to the fourth house: this half is called the **ascending half**; and the remaining parts which are from the fourth house to the seventh, and from there to the middle of the sky, are called the **descending half**. And whatever is above the earth from the circle—that is, the upper part of the circle—is called the **right**; and that which is under the earth—that is, the lower part—is called the **left**. And the Ascendant, the fourth, the seventh, and the tenth [houses] are called *alamed* <note>original: *alamed*, a Latinization of an Arabic term meaning "pillars."</note>, which we call **angles** <term>Angles|The four most powerful points in a horoscope: the Ascendant, Midheaven, Descendant, and Imum Coeli. Planets here are considered very strong.</term> to make it sound more beautiful. The second, eighth, fifth, and eleventh houses are called **succedent** [following] the angles. The third, sixth, eighth <note>Likely a scribal error for the ninth house.</note>, and twelfth are called **cadent** [falling away] from the angles. When, therefore, a planet is in the angles or their succedents, it is said to **prosper**; and if it is in the cadents from the angles, it is said to **fail**; and each of these houses signifies something about the state of human beings.

->On the houses and their meanings.<-

**P**rima <note>The letter P is a woodcut decorative initial featuring foliage designs.</note> The **first house**, the beginning of which rises in the circle of the eastern hemisphere, is called *athale* <note>original: *athale*, from the Arabic *at-tāli‘*, meaning "the rising one."</note>, that is, the **Ascendant**. This signifies the body and life, the beginnings of undertakings in questions, speech, talking, and rumors; and whatever the asker is thinking in their mind: and it signifies the beginnings of life. And Alendezgod <note>likely Al-Andarzaghar, a famous 7th-century Persian astrologer whose works were translated into Latin.</note> said regarding the birth: the first **lord of the triplicity** <term>Lord of the Triplicity|A planet that has a special relationship with one of the four elements (Fire, Earth, Air, Water). In this system, three planets share the rulership of each element, governing different stages of a person's life.</term> of the Ascendant signifies the life and nature of the newborn or the asker, their delight and will, what they love and what they hate, and what good or evil comes to them in the beginning of their life. The second lord of the triplicity signifies the life, the body, strength or fortitude, and the middle of life. The third lord of the triplicity signifies what his partners signified, and signifies the end of life in death.

**S**ecunda <note>The letter S is a woodcut decorative initial featuring foliage designs.</note> The **second house** is the house of substance <note>Wealth, possessions, and material resources.</note>, livelihood, servants, and helpers; it signifies the end of the years of life, that is, the end of youth. And Alendezgod said regarding the lords of the triplicity of the house of substance: concerning the first, second, and third, see which of them is stronger in its position; you should rightfully make that one the author of substance and the significator of acquisitions. For if it is in the middle of the sky, the person will find this [wealth] from a king; and if it is in the house of faith (otherwise called "trust"), it is better. Similarly, the first lord of the triplicity gives substance in the beginning of life, the second in the middle of life, and the third at the end of it.

**T**ertia <note>The letter T is a woodcut decorative initial featuring foliage designs.</note> The **third house** is that of brothers and sisters, relatives, friends of the faith and religious commands, messengers, changes, and minor journeys; and it signifies the state of life before death. Alendezgod said the first lord of the triplicity of the house of brothers signifies the older brothers; the second, the middle ones; and the third, the younger ones; and their dignity and state will be according to their positions.

**T**he **Fourth House** is of fathers, inheritances, the end of things, treasures, and all hidden and secret things; it signifies the conclusion of human life. Alendezgod <note>Original: "alendezgod." This refers to Al-Andarzaghar, a 7th-century Persian astrologer whose work was influential in the medieval Arabic and Latin traditions.</note> said that regarding the lord of the triplicity <note>In astrology, each zodiac sign belongs to one of four elements (triplicities). Each triplicity is ruled by three specific planets which govern different aspects of the houses they occupy.</note> of the house of fathers: the first signifies fathers; the second, cities and lands; the third, the ends of things and prisons.

**T**he **Fifth House** is of children, affections, messengers, and gifts; it signifies what shall come after death, namely regarding praise or blame. Alendezgod said that regarding the lord of the triplicity of the house of children: the first signifies children and life; the second, affection; the third indeed, messengers.

**T**he **Sixth House** is of illnesses and servants; it signifies the end of life and whatever shall occur before old age. Alendezgod said the lord of the triplicity of the house of illnesses: the first signifies illnesses and health conditions <note>Original: "valitudines."</note> arising from illnesses, evils, and deteriorations; the second signifies household slaves and servants; the third signifies what shall come of them, their utility, and their works. It is also the significator of beasts and cattle, and all four-footed animals: their strength, their abundance or scarcity, their behavior under one's hand <note>Presumably the hand of the owner or caretaker.</note> or their departure from it; it also signifies prison and detention.

**T**he **Seventh House** is of women, marriages, contentions, as well as partnerships and opposites; it signifies the middle of the end of life toward old age. Alendezgod said that regarding the lord of the triplicity of the seventh house: the first signifies women; the second, contentions; the third, unions and partnerships.

**T**he **Eighth House** is of death; it signifies fear and death and *almauerith* <note>Original: "almauerith." A Latinization of the Arabic *al-mawarith*, meaning "inheritances."</note>—that is, the property or inheritances of the dead which heirs ought to possess after their passing—and it signifies the end of the years of life after old age. Alendezgod said that regarding the lord of the triplicity of the house of death: the first signifies death; the second, decrees or ancient things; the third, *almauerith*, that is, those things which are to be inherited from the dead.

**T**he **Ninth House** is of pilgrimages, journeys, faith and religion, wisdom, philosophy, and books, as well as letters, messengers, narratives or rumors, and dreams; it signifies the beginning of the middle of life. Alendezgod said that regarding the lord of the triplicity of the house of pilgrimage: the first signifies pilgrimage and everything that happens within it; the second signifies faith and religion, their health, and their manner; the third is the significator of wisdom, dreams, the stars, and auguries, as well as the truth or falsehood of those who practice them.

**T**he **Tenth House** is the royal <note>Original: "regina," literally "queen," used here to denote the supreme or most powerful house.</note> house, of works and advancement or exaltation; also of the kingdom, memory, and the "voice"—namely of command—and of masteries and mothers; it signifies the middle years of life. Alendezgod said that regarding the lord of the triplicity of the royal house: the first signifies work and exaltation—namely the advancement of the seat and the highest dwelling; the second therefore signifies the voice of command and boldness in the same; the third signifies its stability and durability.

**T**he **Eleventh House** is of trust, praise, and fortune; also of friends, ministers, and helpers; it signifies the end of the years of the middle of life. Alendezgod said that regarding the lord of the triplicity of the house of trust: the first signifies trust; the second, friends; the third, their utility or profit.

**T**he **Twelfth House** is of enemies, labor, sadness, envy, and whispering <note>Likely referring to backbiting or secret plots.</note>, of the cunning of cleverness and of beasts; it signifies the end of life and...

These are the things that the twelve houses signify.

## ->On the colors of the twelve houses.<-

The twelve houses also signify colors: and these are they: for the ascending house (the first) and the seventh are **white**. The second and twelfth are **green**. The third and eleventh are **yellow** <note>Original: "crocee," meaning saffron-colored.</note>. The fourth and tenth are **red**. The fifth and ninth are **honey-colored**, that is, they have the color of honey. The sixth and eighth are **black**.

## ->On the joys of the planets in the houses.<-

Each planet has in each of these houses a certain power from among its accidental powers, which are called "joy": for **Mercury** joys in the ascendant (1st house). The **Moon** in the third house. **Venus** also in the fifth, and **Mars** in the sixth. The **Sun** in the ninth. **Jupiter** in the eleventh. **Saturn** in the twelfth.

## ->On the strength of the angles.<-

It is said regarding the significance of the houses that the **angles** <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which represent the four corners of the sky: the East, the West, the Midheaven, and the Bottom of the Sky.</note> signify strength and perfection. Those houses **cadent** <note>Falling away</note> from the angles signify weakness and loss; except that the ninth and third signify a thing that is fit and revealed with fame. The twelfth and sixth signify concealment, withholding, and the worthlessness of things. But the angles and the lords of the angles signify the greatness of honors, value, and fortune; they signify strength, practice, and distance from a fall. Their presence in cadent houses is contrary to fortune, that is, it signifies disgrace and a fall. 

Regarding the **succedent** houses <note>The houses that follow the angles: the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th.</note> of the angles: the house that follows the tenth—the **eleventh**—signifies strength and moderate fortune among friends, and from that part where trust was placed. And that which follows the fourth—the **fifth**—signifies moderate fortune through gifts and honors, and because of children, with honor, happiness, and joy. That which follows the ascendant—which is the **second**—similarly signifies moderate fortune from the cause of substance <note>Wealth or possessions</note> and servants. That which follows the seventh—the **eighth**—similarly signifies moderate fortune from *al-mawarith* <note>Original: "almauerith." An Arabic term for inheritance.</note>, that is, inheritance which is inherited from the dead and from hidden things.

## ->On the meanings of the lords of the angles.<-

There are also meanings for the lords of the angles when they are present in the angles. 

The presence of the **lord of the ascendant** in the ascendant signifies one's fortune through oneself and through one's own acquisition. 
Its presence in the **tenth** signifies fortune through the king and higher offices. 
When it is in the **seventh**, it signifies fortune through agreements, agents, and wives. 
By its presence also in the **fourth**, it signifies fortune through inheritances and the affairs of fathers, and through the production of waters and populations or plantations—that is, buildings—and from ancient and fundamental things. 

The **lord of the tenth** signifies, through its presence in that same tenth, fortune either through the king or through a great kingdom and higher offices. 
It signifies, through its presence in the **seventh**, a kingdom through victory in contentions and from the cause of wives. 
Through its presence in the **fourth**, it signifies a kingdom through the friendship of nobles and of its king, and through the causes of tribunes <note>Original: "tribunorum," referring to local officials or leaders.</note>, and through the stewards of kings, the cultivation of lands, the building of cities, the dividing of rivers, the guarding of cities, and from ancient things. 
And by its presence in the **ascendant**, it signifies a kingdom through ingenuity and through the closeness of the king to the affairs of the common people. 

The presence of the **lord of the seventh** in the seventh signifies fortune through trade and through agreements, and also through nurturings and women, and through agents. 
It signifies, through its presence in the **fourth**, fortune through agreements with women, trade, the affairs of fathers, inheritances, and the cultivation of the land. 
Its presence in the **ascendant** signifies fortune through agreements and trade...

### How it may be known which planet is the Ruler.

<image-desc>Large decorative initial 'E' featuring ornate leaf and scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>
And when you wish to know the ruling planet of a matter, look at which of the planets has more authority in the house of that matter, and the planet which signifies the nature of that matter, as we shall say in [the section on] the natures of the planets. Look, therefore, at which of the planets is stronger in the house of the matter and in its "part" <note>The "Part" or "Lot" is a mathematical point in a chart calculated from the positions of other planets.</note> based on the strengths we mentioned before—that is, from the number we spoke of earlier when we treated the strength and power of the planets. Whichever is strongest of all in the place of the matter shall be its ruler. 

For example: if the inquiry is about substance <note>Wealth or personal possessions.</note> and you wish to know who is its ruler, and it is the second house which signifies substance (namely the fifth degree of the sign of Aries): because it is the house of **Mars**, **Mars** has in this place five strengths; the exaltation <note>A sign where a planet's influence is particularly high.</note> also belongs to the **Sun**, and it has four strengths in it. It is also the **Sun's** triplicity <note>A group of three signs belonging to the same element.</note>, and it has three strengths in it. Therefore, the **Sun** has seven strengths there. And it is the "term" <note>A specific division of a sign assigned to a planet.</note> of **Jupiter**, and he has two strengths there; and it is the "face" <note>A 10-degree division of a sign.</note> of **Mars**, and he has one strength there. Therefore **Mars** has six strengths there (five from the house and one from the face), and the **Sun** has seven. Such a planet is called the <term>Almubtez</term> <note>Original: "almubta," from the Arabic "al-mubtazz," meaning the "victor" or the planet with the most combined dignity at a specific point.</note>, that is, the "victor." The **Sun**, therefore, takes the leadership there, because it has seven strengths there, and it rules the power in the house of substance. Similarly, you will calculate this in the place of the "Part of Substance" and the "Part of Fortune," and you will make **Jupiter** a participant in them, who is the natural significator of substance, and you shall combine the testimonies of the parts and the planets; and you shall do this same thing for all the houses and you will know their ruler.

### On the accidental powers of the planets.

<image-desc>Large decorative initial 'E' containing an illustration of a seated figure or youth amidst foliage.</image-desc>
Among the accidental powers of the planets is also <term>Al-Hayz</term> <note>Original: "alhais." An accidental dignity where a planet's condition matches its natural preference.</note>, that is, "similarity." This occurs when a diurnal planet <note>Planets naturally suited to the day, like the Sun or Jupiter.</note> is above the earth by day and below the earth by night, and a nocturnal planet <note>Planets naturally suited to the night, like the Moon or Venus.</note> is above the earth by night and below the earth by day; and if this should be a masculine planet in a masculine sign, and a feminine planet in a feminine sign, it is said to be in its "similarity," that is, in its <term>Al-Hayz</term>. And its strength will be like the strength of a man in his place of success, that is, of acquisition and fortune.

It also introduces the concept of Al-Hayz, a condition where a planet gains power by being in a placement that matches its inherent nature (day/night and gender).</summary>

# The second distinction concerning the natures of the seven planets, what is proper to them, and what they signify regarding the essence of things.

<image-desc>Large woodcut ornamental initial 'Et' featuring a large 'E' with white interlaced scrollwork on a dark square background.</image-desc>And because, with God’s help, we have now completed what we proposed to treat regarding the circle of signs <note>The Zodiac.</note> and its accidental properties, let us now pursue our intention concerning the seven planets: their natures, their being, and what they signify.

<image-desc>Small woodcut decorative initial 'S' with foliate patterns.</image-desc>Saturn is masculine, malefic <note>original: "malus"; in astrology, Saturn is traditionally the "Great Malefic," associated with hardship.</note>, and diurnal <note>A "day" planet.</note>. He is the signifier of fathers if the birth of a child occurs at night. He signifies the final stage of old age if he is occidental <note>Setting after the Sun.</note>, and the beginning of old age if he is oriental <note>Rising before the Sun.</note>. He signifies the gravity of cold and dryness, and the melancholic <term>complexion</term> <note>The "complexion" refers to the balance of the four humors in the body; melancholy was associated with black bile, earth, and cold/dry qualities.</note> of bodies, as well as its increase and its distillation. And perhaps his complexion will sometimes be cold, moist, and heavy; he is of a foul odor, given to much eating, and capable of true love. 

He signifies depth of counsel and much silence. Regarding <term>magisteries</term> <note>Occupations or professional skills.</note>, he signifies ancient and precious things. He signifies the cultivation of fields and the populating of lands and rivers if he is fortunate <note>In a favorable position in the birth chart.</note>; but he signifies vile and laborious things if he is malefic, such as the rubbers in the baths, fullers <note>Workers who shrink and thicken cloth.</note>, and sailors. If he is fortunate, he will be of true love, spacious <note>Perhaps meaning broad-minded or generous in spirit.</note>, and patient. If he is malefic, he will be indiscreet, stubborn, sad, and mourning; of bad suspicion, suspecting much, and moving men to whispering and gossip. 

If he is fortunate, he signifies among substances things that are ancient and durable, inheritances, and the cultivation of the earth. If he is malefic, he signifies dirty waters of bad taste, old and subject to change. Among infirmities, he signifies phlegmatic, melancholic, viscous, and frozen sharp diseases, such as leprosy, morphea <note>A skin disease causing discolored patches.</note>, gout, cancers, and other such things. He signifies long pilgrimages, prisons, chains, labor, slowness, and affliction, and <term>almauerith</term> <gloss>that is, the estates of the dead</gloss>. Also fathers, grandfathers, elder brothers, eunuchs, servants, and lowly men. 

Regarding crafts, he signifies the work of leathers if he is the sole signifier without the combination of any other planets. But if Jupiter is joined with him, he signifies the work of parchment in which divine books are written. If Mars is joined to him, he signifies the soles of shoes and their preparation. If the Sun is joined to him, he signifies the work of cobblers. If Venus is joined to him, he signifies the work of leathers from which drums <note>original: "timpana"</note> and other instruments are made which we use in games. If Mercury is joined to him, he signifies the work of leathers in which testaments and the accounts of stipends <note>Payroll or tax records.</note> are written. If the Moon is joined to him, he signifies the preparation of the hides of wild beasts and mountain animals and other similar things. 

Regarding religious sects, he signifies that which confesses unity <note>Monotheism.</note> if he is fortunate; and if he is malefic, he signifies the belief in unity, yet with much hesitation <gloss>that is, doubt</gloss>. And **Messehala** <gloss>that is, "he whom God willed to be exalted"</gloss> <note>A reference to Masha'allah ibn Athari (c. 740–815 AD), a prominent Persian Jewish astrologer and astronomer.</note>, who was an astrologer in the science...

<summary>This page begins a detailed catalog of the astrological properties of Saturn, describing its influence on personality, physical health, social status, and various crafts involving leather based on its conjunction with other planets.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, astrology, melancholy, Masha'allah (Messehala), leatherworking, monotheism, medical astrology, planetary natures</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the description of Saturn's astrological significations, specifically his physical appearance and his governance over faiths and regions, before transitioning into a detailed profile of Jupiter, the "Greater Fortune."</meta>

...[clever]ness, clear in judgments; for which reason it is said that he signifies the Jewish faith, because it is among the oldest; and all faiths acknowledge it, while it acknowledges no other. Similarly, Saturn is the one to whom all the other planetary spheres are joined, while he is joined to none <note>In the ancient geocentric model, Saturn was the outermost planet, encompassing all others within its orbit.</note>. He also signifies black garments. And some others have said that Saturn signifies the inner parts of the ear, the spleen, and the stomach; and among colors, he has blackness; and among tastes, those that are astringent and sour. And of the days, Saturday; and of the nights, the one which precedes Wednesday. And the size of his orb is nine degrees. And his <term>firdaria</term> <note>original: "fridarie"; a system of planetary periods or "ruling years" in a person's life.</note> years are 11; his greatest years are 465; his major years 57; his middle years 43 and a half; and his minor years 30. His strength among the quarters of the circle is in the right of the North. And Masha'allah <note>original: "messehala"; an influential 8th-century Persian Jewish astrologer and astronomer.</note> said that regarding the forms of men, he signifies a man between black and saffron-colored, who, when he walks, casts his eyes down toward the earth, heavy in his gait, keeping his feet close together. He is lean and hunched, having small eyes and dry, veiny skin, with a thin beard on his jaws; he is clever, ingenious, a deceiver, and a killer. And Dorotheus <note>Dorotheus of Sidon, a 1st-century astrological poet.</note> said that he signifies a man with a very hairy body and joined eyebrows; and from the regions, he has Al-Sind <note>original: "Alcinc"; referring to the region of the Indus Valley.</note> and India and all the land of the blacks. And among the "parts" <note>Referring to the "Arabic Parts" or "Lots," specific mathematical points in a horoscope.</note>, he has the part of strength and stability; and he signifies lands or the things of the earth and inheritances, and those who are in charge of works and audacity and labor, inventions, and the causes of death; and he has from the parts of the world Achiud and Alchiut and their borders, and the lands of the blacks and their mountains.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'I' (or 'J') featuring a stylized floral or geometric pattern within a square frame.</image-desc>

Jupiter is the [Greater] Fortune, masculine and diurnal; and he is the signifier of substance <note>Wealth or possessions.</note> and produces heat and a tempered moisture, being of an airy and sanguine nature. And regarding ages, he signifies youth up until the perfection of age <note>Middle age or maturity.</note>; and regarding the <term>magisteries</term> <note>Professions or skills.</note> that pertain to the law, such as judging just judgments, and bringing peace between men, and studying good things. Regarding substance, he signifies abundance; and of business, those things which are done without deception. He signifies the soul and life, joy, truth, religion, patience, and every beautiful and precious precept, and an abundance of pleasures. Regarding illnesses, he signifies those that arise from the blood through an increase in quantity (provided it is not an unnatural excess) nor from corrupted or changeable blood. 

He is the planet of wisdom, intellect, and practice. If Saturn is joined with him, he signifies necromancy, incantations, and exorcisms, etc. And if Mars is joined with him, he signifies the science of medicine. And if the Sun is joined with him, he signifies the knowledge of sects <note>Schools of thought or religious groups.</note> and prudence in contentions and disputes. And if Venus is joined with him, he signifies the composition of sounds <note>Music.</note> and of other delightful sciences. And if Mercury is joined with him, he signifies the science of arithmetic and writing, as well as astronomy, philosophy, and geometry. And if the Moon is joined with him, he signifies the science of the management of waters and their measurement, as well as of lands. Regarding the quality of the mind, he signifies generosity, modesty, and justice; and regarding sects, plurality and simulation. 

However, some have said that he signifies the liver, the stomach, the left ear, the arms, and the belly; also the lower parts of the pubic region and the intestines. Among colors, he has the color of ash and green and those similar to them; and among tastes, he has the sweet. The size of his orb is 9 degrees. Of the days, he has the day of Jupiter <note>Thursday.</note>; and of the nights, the night of the day of the Moon <note>The night preceding Monday.</note>. His firdaria years—that is, his period of dominion—are 12; his greatest years are 428; his major years 79; his middle years 45 and a half; and his minor years 12. His strength among the quarters of the circle is in the West. And Masha'allah said that among men, he signifies a white man having redness in his face, with eyes that are not entirely black, and nostrils that are not...

<sig>b ij</sig>

...even and short [teeth], and bald; having a blackness in some of his teeth; of beautiful stature, of good spirit and good morals, and of a beautiful body. And Dorotheus <note>Dorotheus of Sidon, a 1st-century astrological poet.</note> said that he signifies a man having large eyes with a broad pupil and a curly beard. And among regions, he possesses Al-Khayrat <note>Likely referring to fertile regions in the East or modern-day Iraq/Iran area</note>, Babylonia, Syria <note>original: "asen"; likely referring to Ash-Sham</note>, Persia, the land of the Alans <note>original: "alaormes"; refers to the Alani, a nomadic Iranian people of antiquity</note>, and Arcadia. From the parts of the body, he governs the state of perfect health. He signifies faith and the desire for good things; and among works, he signifies health, security, and partnership.

### Mars

**Mars**, masculine, nocturnal, and malefic, produces heat and dryness. He is the significator of brothers and of travels. Among the ages of man, he governs youth until its very end. His nature is choleric <note>One of the four humors, associated with a quick-tempered and ambitious personality</note> and of a bitter taste. Among professions, he governs every fiery craft performed with iron and fire, such as the forging of swords with hammers. 

When Saturn is joined with him, it signifies the striking of iron. If Jupiter is joined, it signifies the striking of bronze. If the Sun, it signifies the striking of gold coins. If Venus, it signifies the craft of ornaments. If Mercury, the striking of needles. If the Moon, it signifies the striking of spears and books; he also signifies the work of medicine when he alone takes up the signification. 

If no planet is joined with him, he signifies bloodletting <term>minutionem</term> <note>original: "minutionem"; specifically phlebotomy, a common medieval medical treatment</note>, wounding, the binding of wounds, and similar things. And if Saturn is joined with him, it signifies the work of medicine and of wounds. If Jupiter, the work of natural things. If the Sun, the care of the eyes. If Venus, the work of ornamentation, such as the cutting of hair and beards and the clipping of nails. If Mercury, the cutting of veins. If the Moon, it signifies the pulling of teeth and the cleaning of ears. 

By himself, he signifies the injuries of the wretched, the shedding of blood, oppression by force, highway robbery <note>original: "abscissiones viarum"; literally 'the cutting of the ways'</note>, wrath, the leadership of an army, haste, shamelessness, travel outside one's homeland, the foulness of intercourse, the loss of children—that is, miscarriages—and middle brothers and sisters, and the knowledge and skill of caring for beasts. 

Among infirmities, he governs hot and bloody fevers, and bloody pustules called <term>al-abzaha</term> <note>original: "alabzaha"; an Arabic-derived term for a type of skin eruption or rash</note>, which is a redness of the body with roughness and foulness and the eating away of flesh with rot; also pain in half the head <note>migraine</note>, "holy fire" <term>ignem sacrum</term> <note>original: "ignem sacrum"; likely referring to Erysipelas or shingles</note>, fear, and horrible thoughts that disturb people, agitating and hindering them and rendering them empty; and whatever comes with the inflammation of heat. 

From the quality of the soul, he signifies agitation and disturbance. If Saturn should look upon him <note>Form an astrological aspect with him</note>, it signifies hatred and the greatest envy. Of religious sects, he signifies those in which there is war, and unity, and a quickness to change one’s faith, a multitude of hesitation, and changing from one testament to another—yet all these will be under a certain unity. 

Some have said that among the limbs, he governs the gallbladder, kidneys, veins, the flow of seed, and the back. Among colors, he has redness; and among tastes, the bitter. The size of his orb is 8 degrees. Among the days, he has the day of Mars <note>Tuesday</note>; and of the nights, the night of the Sabbath <note>Saturday night</note>. His <term>firdaria</term> <note>A system of planetary periods governing different stages of life</note> years are seven. His greatest years are 264; his major years are 66; his middle years are 40 and a half; and his minor years are 15. His strength in the quarters of the circle is in the South. 

And Masha'allah <note>original: "mesceala"; an influential 8th-century Persian Jewish astrologer</note> said that regarding the forms of men, he signifies a reddish man having red hair and a round face, who slightly dishonors men; having saffron-colored eyes, a horrible appearance, a bold and sharp mind, and having a mark or spot on his foot.
