<meta>This page concludes the profile of Mars before beginning an extensive description of the Sun (Sol), detailing its physical, social, and medical influences, as well as its interactions with other planets.</meta>

And Dorotheus <note>Dorotheus of Sidon, a 1st-century astrological poet often cited in medieval texts.</note> said that he [Mars] signifies one having a sharp gaze; and from the parts of character, a portion of audacity; and he signifies perseverance, craftiness, pride, levity, mobility, boldness, and sharpness in business, and haste in all matters. From the regions of the world, he governs Al-Ahwaz <note>original: "Alce"; likely referring to the region in modern-day Iran</note> and the lands of the Romans as far as the West, and the lands of the Tuscans.

### The Sun

The **Sun** is fortunate by its aspect <note>A "fortunate aspect" means the Sun’s influence is positive when it is positioned at specific angles (like a trine or sextile) to other planets.</note>, but harmful through conjunction <note>A "conjunction" occurs when two celestial bodies appear in the same sign; the Sun "burns" planets that get too close to it.</note> in a single sign. He is masculine and diurnal <note>Active during the day.</note>; he produces heat and dryness. He is the significator of fathers, and if the birth occurs during the day, he signifies the greatest kingdom, the vital soul, light, splendor, intellect, beauty, cleanliness, and faith. From the ages of man, he governs the end of youth. He participates with all the planets in the arrangement of the years. 

Among roles of mastery, he signifies rule and leadership; he signifies the throwing of javelins, hunting, and purging with every kind of cleansing by which bodies are purified internally and externally. From infirmities, he signifies hot and dry diseases appearing in the body. From substance, he signifies much gold and all types of wealth. From the quality of the mind itself, he signifies sublimity, prudence, and those things which follow honesty—namely, generosity, glory, and a broadness of mind. From religious sects, he signifies good worship and similar things. He signifies the command of the voice and the strength of speed. 

If **Saturn** joins him, it signifies the office of a steward <note>original: "villicationem"; the management of an estate or villa</note> and similar leadership. If **Jupiter** joins him, it signifies leadership in faith and religion, and also a judge among men judging the works of the oppressed or of injuries, etc. If **Mars** joins him, it signifies the leadership of an army and the investigation of wars. If **Venus** joins him, it signifies a kingdom obtained through women or through the service of the powerful. If **Mercury** joins him, it signifies counselors of kings and the writing of books, inheritances, and great works. If the **Moon** joins him, it signifies the work of ambassadors and the uncovering of secrets and similar things. 

Certain authorities have said that the Sun signifies the image of the human face: specifically, the right eye in men and the left eye in women. Some have also said he governs the heart, the marrow, and the thighs. From infirmities, he signifies the eating away of the flesh in the mouth <note>Likely cankers or ulcers.</note>, damage to the mouth specifically, and the "descending of water into the eye" <note>A historical term for cataracts.</note>; his power resides in the head. 

The Indian astrologers said that when he is in the ascendant <note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>, he will be "burning" and the person will have a mark on their face. From colors, he governs whatever appears as a foreign or strange color; from tastes, the acidic. The size of his orbital sphere is 15 degrees. Of the days, he has Sunday; of the nights, the night of Thursday. 

His years of *Fidaria* <note>A system of planetary periods governing blocks of a person's life.</note> are ten. His "greater years" are 120; his "maximum years" are 1460 <note>This corresponds to the Sothic cycle of ancient Egypt.</note>; his "middle years" are 39 and a half (or according to some, 69 and a half); and his "lesser years" are 19. His strength among the quarters of the circle is in the East. 

Messahallah <note>original: "mesceala"; an 8th-century Persian-Jewish astrologer.</note> said that in human figures, he signifies one whose color is between yellow and black—that is, a dusky complexion touched with redness—of short stature, with curly hair, bald, and of a beautiful body; and he possesses a sharp taste. Dorotheus said that the appearance of the Sun and Moon is the appearance of the planets that are with them, or of the planet that is most dignified in their place. Therefore, if you wish to know the appearance of the Sun, know that it is yellow, having a portion of redness in the hair, and his eyes are somewhat yellow. From parts of the mind, he governs the "part of things to come"—that is, the power of divination—and he is the significator of natures, the spirit of wisdom, elevation, the perfection of faith, and also of sciences and praises. From the regions of the world, he governs Samarkand <note>original: "zamarach"</note>, Kura <note>The Cyrus River region</note>, Fergana <note>original: "ferit"; a region in Central Asia</note> and its borders, and the lands of the Romans.

### Venus

**Venus** is a feminine, nocturnal "fortune" <note>In traditional astrology, a "fortune" or benefic planet is one believed to have a positive, helpful influence.</note>. She is the significator of women, wives, and mothers if the birth occurs during the day. She produces temperate cold and moisture. Regarding the ages of man, she governs youth or adolescence. 

From the occupations of mastery, she signifies instruments of play and ornaments, as well as beautiful figures, games of dice and chess, dancing, leisure, and fornications—including both those who commit fornication and children born of such acts. She governs the multitude of sexual acts and all kinds of luxury, the making of wreaths <note>original: "compositiones coronarum"</note> and their use, beauty, and cleanliness. She also signifies clothing, ornaments, gold, silver, affection, games, laughter, and joy. She governs the use of ointments and various spices, as well as drinking and drunkenness. She is a planet that trusts everyone; she signifies generosity, affection, diligence, love, justice, and houses of prayer, and she maintains faith. She signifies mastery of all the artistic signs <note>original: "signorum"; likely meaning the arts or skills</note>, such as music and so forth.

**The Music of Venus**
If **Saturn** joins with her, it signifies the sound of songs with which the dead are sometimes mourned, or those which builders sing when they are constructing buildings. 

If **Jupiter** joins with her, it signifies the sound of readings or songs used by the leaders of religious sects at altars and places of prayer in praise of almighty God. 

If **Mars** joins with her, it signifies sounds used by secular people and the songs of the common folk which mention blasphemy or battles, or which mention prison or labor, and similar things—such as songs mentioning capture, binding, and the striking of whips. 

If the **Sun** joins with her, it signifies the sound of wood <note>This likely refers to wooden instruments, such as lutes or flutes.</note> played before kings and nobles. 

If **Mercury** joins with her, it signifies the sound used in the composition of verses <note>Poetry and rhythmic speech.</note>. 

And if the **Moon** joins with her, it signifies the songs of sailors on ships.

**Health and Substance**
From infirmities, she governs cold and moist diseases that happen specifically and frequently in the genital organs. From substance—those things acquired for their beauty—she governs women’s ornaments, their clothing, pearls, and paintings. From the quality of the soul, she governs sweetness, friendship, eating, and the like, as well as cravings for drink, food, and sex. From religious sects, she signifies the worship of idols, and those people who especially indulge in eating and drinking. Some have said she signifies the hips, the spine, and sperm; others have said she signifies fat, flesh, the kidneys, the vulva, the womb, the belly, the pubes <note>original: "pectinem"</note>, and the navel. 

From colors, she governs whites; from flavors, the oily or greasy. The size of her orb <note>The "orb" is the range of degrees within which a planet's influence is felt before a perfect alignment.</note> is 7 degrees. From the days, she has Friday; from the nights, the night of Mars <note>In the medieval system, the "night of Mars" refers to Tuesday night.</note>. Her **firdaria** <term>firdaria: a Persian system of planetary periods where each planet rules a block of years in a person's life</term> years are eight. Her greater years are 82, her greatest 151, her middle 45, and her lesser 8. Her strength in the quarters of the circle is in the right of the east.

**Physical Appearance**
**Messeala** <note>Masha'allah ibn Athari (c. 740–815 AD), a prominent Persian Jewish astrologer.</note> said that regarding human figures, she signifies a person of white skin tending toward darkness <note>Swarthy or olive-skinned.</note> with a beautiful body and hair, a round face, small jaws, and beautiful eyes; the darkness of the eyes is more pronounced than usual. 

And **Dorotheus** <note>Dorotheus of Sidon, a 1st-century astrological poet.</note> said she signifies a person having a beautiful face, beautiful eyes, much hair, and a very white complexion mixed with redness, being thick-set and showing benevolence. 

From the "parts," she governs the **Part of Desire** <term>Arabic Parts: mathematical points in a horoscope calculated by the distance between two planets and added to the Ascendant</term>; she signifies friendship, play, affection, patience, and the joining together of males. From the regions of the world, she governs Al-Hijaz <note>original: "albiget"</note>, Yemen <note>original: "alieme"</note>, and the lands of the Arabs.

It also lists her physical characteristics, medical associations with the reproductive system, and her planetary years.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the astrological descriptions of the planets, moving from Venus (concluded on the previous page) to a detailed profile of Mercury. The text maintains a consistent focus on how each planet influences human behavior, professions, and physical traits.</meta>

### Mercury

**Mercury** is a mixed <note>In astrology, Mercury is "mixed" or neutral because he takes on the nature (masculine/feminine, good/bad) of the planets he is near.</note>, masculine, and diurnal planet. By his very nature, he is inclined toward whichever planet or zodiac sign he is joined with. He is the significator of younger brothers; he also signifies servants and the love of concubines. Furthermore, he signifies divinity, the oracles of prophets, belief, labor, and honor or prayer. 

If he acts according to his own nature without being joined to any other planet, he signifies earthly matters and the increase of things through growth. In terms of human age, he signifies youth and progress within it. Among occupations, he governs those that produce knowledge, such as preaching, rhetoric, business, appraisal, geometry, the management of affairs, philosophy, augury <note>The practice of predicting the future through signs or omens.</note>, proverbs, writing, and the science of poetry—but especially the work of numbers <note>original: "opus numeri"; referring to arithmetic or mathematics.</note>.

**Mercury's Combinations with Other Planets**
If **Saturn** joins with him, he signifies, through the work of numbers, the measurement of lands and inheritances, and the numbering of buildings and of looms. 

If **Jupiter** joins with him, he signifies the numbering of psalms and the numbering of divine books. 

If **Mars** joins with him, he signifies the numbering used in the payments given to armies and in battles, as well as the counting of strikes from whips and clubs. 

If the **Sun** joins with him, he signifies being in charge of the accounts of kings and the assets of households. 

If **Venus** joins with him, he signifies the number of strings on wooden instruments or lyres, and the number of sounds and flutes. 

If the **Moon** joins with him, he signifies the number of dishes prepared for the meals of those traveling away from home. 

**Health and Character**
Regarding illnesses, he signifies spiritual ailments of the soul—that is, "horrible thoughts," restlessness of the mind, doubts, and other similar things. He also signifies the quality of the mind according to his temperament and conjunction. When he is "fortunate" <note>Positioned in a way that exerts a positive influence.</note>, there will be goodness according to the type of fortune that favors him and the place where he is situated. When he is "malus" <note>unfortunate or poorly positioned</note>, there will be wickedness according to the evil that afflicts him and the place where he was made harmful. 

Regarding religious sects, he signifies the "culture of unity" and similar things, though this is often done in secret with hypocrisy and pretense. Some have said that he signifies the thighs, the navel, the groin <note>original: "pectinem"; literally 'the comb', referring to the pubic area</note>, the legs, the nerves, and the veins. 

**Physical and Temporal Attributes**
In terms of colors, he possesses every mixed and variegated color, such as *minium* <note>original: "menium"; a reddish pigment, though here compared to a flower.</note>, which is the color of the wild lily flower. In terms of tastes, he signifies the acidic. The size of his "orb" <note>The area of influence surrounding a planet.</note> is 7 degrees. Among the days, he owns Wednesday <note>original: "diem mercurij"</note>, and among the nights, the night of Sunday. 

His *fridaria* <note>A system of planetary periods used to predict stages of a person's life.</note> years are 13. His "greater" years are 76, his "greatest" are 480, his "middle" are 48, and his "lesser" years are 20. His strength in the quarters of the circle is in the North. 

**Mercury’s Movements and the Ages of Man**
Some have said that Mercury, from the midpoint of his retrogradation <note>The period when a planet appears to move backward in the sky.</note> until his "second station," signifies childhood. From the second station until his conjunction with the Sun, he signifies youth. From the conjunction with the Sun until his "first station," he signifies middle age. From his first station until the midpoint of his retrogradation, he signifies old age and opposition. 

In his conjunction while moving forward until the first station, he signifies affection, friendship, and the imitation of love. Moving from his second station toward conjunction, he signifies the pursuit of affection and concord. Moving from his first station back toward retrogradation, he signifies the pursuit of opposition, the slowness of hypocrisy, a lack of shame, preoccupation, and a dazed state in affairs. In the middle of retrogradation, he signifies a moderate speed because of a weakness of wit. At the second station, he signifies a dazed state, a preoccupation of the wit, and slowness; but the departure from this state signifies speed and the opening of the wits.

<summary>This page describes the astrological nature of Mercury, detailing his influence on professions like mathematics and writing, his physical associations, and how his meaning changes based on his "stations" and relationship to other planets.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, astrology, retrogradation, fridaria, planetary conjunctions, arithmetic, medicine, spiritual ailments</keywords>

...and through his conjunction with the Sun by direction <note>A method of calculating the timing of future events in a birth chart.</note>, he signifies quickness and a passion of the intellect, as well as their breadth or increase. A similar effect occurs with Venus in these positions and with the higher planets <note>The "superior" planets: Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note>. 

And **Messeala** <note>Messahallah (c. 740–815 AD), a Persian Jewish astrologer whose works were foundational to Western medieval astrology.</note> said: Mercury, by his appearance, signifies a man who is neither very pale nor dark-skinned; he has a high and long forehead, a long face and nose, a thin beard on the jaws, and beautiful eyes that are not entirely black; he also has long fingers. 

Among roles, he governs the sphere of trade and negotiation. He also signifies fear, harassment, war, enmities, deceptions, and oppositions. Furthermore, he signifies a perfect mastery and subtlety in craft and investigation, and in all other human activities involving actions and disputes. Among lands, he governs *Adelech Alcuidem* <note>original: "adelech alcuidem"; likely a Latinized Arabic geographic term, possibly referring to a specific region of the East or the coast.</note> and especially the South, and the lands of the Indians.

<margin>The Moon</margin>
<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'L' decorated with floral and foliate patterns.</image-desc>**The Moon** is a feminine, nocturnal fortune <note>In astrology, a "fortune" or "benefic" is a planet believed to have a positive or helpful influence.</note> that produces coldness and moisture. She is the significator of mothers. If a birth occurs at night, she represents a moderate temperament <note>The balance of the four humors (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, black bile).</note>; she also signifies the age of childhood and the beginning of growth. 

Among occupations, she governs embassies and mandates, and works involving water and land, according to the degree of her good or bad fortune. For if she is well-placed, she signifies good, and vice-versa. She also signifies leadership—if she presides over or is the "lady" of matters pertaining to the king—provided she is favored by the Sun in a good aspect and in a favorable position. 

In terms of substance, she signifies silver and the cultivation of the land (if she has authority in the fourth house <note>The Fourth House in a zodiac chart governs land, ancestry, and the home.</note>), and in matters of faith, she signifies religion. Among illnesses, she governs epilepsy, paralysis, the "falling gout" <note>original: "guttam caducam"; a historical term for epilepsy.</note>, twisting of the face, the knotting of limbs, and tremors—and whatever else resembles coldness and moisture. She also governs the quality of the soul according to her conjunction with the other planets. 

If she is joined with a "fortune," and that fortune is **Venus**, she signifies courtliness, kindness, sweetness of soul, honesty of character, quickness of movement, and divine service. If it is **Jupiter**, she signifies honesty, caution, fellowship, kindness, and a beautiful life. 

If she is joined with "malifics" <note>Planets traditionally viewed as challenging: Mars and Saturn.</note>, and it is **Mars**, she signifies a passion for whispering <note>original: "susurrationis"; implying gossip, plotting, or secret rumors.</note> among men and a habit of such things. If it is **Saturn**, she signifies hatred, hypocrisy, and envy. 

If the **Sun** is in a praiseworthy aspect, she signifies a royal disposition. If it is **Mercury**, she signifies rhetoric (that is, eloquence and a good tongue) and writing. Some have said she signifies thought, novelty of mind, weakness of the senses, and a heavy tongue; as well as honest women, the nursing of small children, mothers, maternal aunts, and the preparation of food. Others have said she specifically signifies the brain and the lungs. 

Among colors, she has saffron-yellow; and among flavors, saltiness <note>original: "falsum"; likely a transcription error for "salsum" (salty), the flavor traditionally assigned to the Moon.</note>. The size of her orb is 12 degrees. Among men, she signifies the left eye, and among women, the right. Among days, she has Monday; and among nights, the night of Friday <note>original: "noctez veneris"; the night belonging to Venus in the system of planetary hours.</note>. Her years of the *Fridaria* <note>A medieval astrological system of planetary periods that rule over specific stages of a person's life.</note> are nine; her "greater" years are 108, her "greatest" 120, her "middle" 66 and a half (or according to some, 39 and a half), and her "lesser" years are 25. Her strength comes from the quarters of the circle on the right of the West. And Messeala said that she signifies...

<summary>This page concludes the description of Mercury’s physical and professional influences before introducing the Moon (Luna). It details the Moon's governance over mothers, childhood, silver, and various health conditions, as well as how her influence changes when paired with other planets.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Moon, Messeala, astrology, planetary conjunctions, epilepsy, childhood, silver, Fridaria, medical astrology</keywords>

--- Page 32 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the planets' physical characteristics and astrological significations, transitioning from Mercury's description to the specific features, cycles, and influences of the Moon (Luna), as well as the planetary rulers of the months of pregnancy.</meta>

In human form, she represents a person of white complexion tinged with redness, with eyebrows that meet; a benevolent person with eyes that are not entirely black, a round face, and a beautiful stature; and there is a mark on the face. Among lands, she governs *Arcoch*, *Timana*, and *Aldeila* <note>original: "arcoch 7 timanam 7 aldeilam"; these are Latinized Arabic geographic terms commonly found in medieval astrological treatises.</note>.

And some have said that she signifies childhood from the start of the month until 7 days; and from 7 days until 14 days, youth; and she signifies the age of perfection—that is, middle age—until 21 days and nights; and old age until the conjunction—that is, until the end of the month when she is joined to the Sun. 

And when she is under the Sun’s rays <term>combust: when a planet is so close to the Sun that its influence is "burned up" or hidden</term>, she signifies secrets and hidden things, and likewise for every planet. At the start of the month, when she increases <note>waxes</note>, she signifies all that is to be done; and in her fullness, what is to be destroyed, because she then decreases <note>wanes</note>. At the start of the month, she and the Sun signify accusers, and at the end of the month, the accused. She signifies profit and self-control at the start of the month, and at the end of the month, wages and dispersions. At the opposition <note>the full moon</note>, she signifies contrariety. 

At the start of the change toward opposition—that is, when there are 15 degrees between her and the opposition—she signifies the beginning of opposition and its cause. When she has separated from the opposition, she signifies the causes of release from that opposition. In her exit from under the Sun's rays, she signifies an exit from concealment and his daughter <note>original: "his filia"; this is an obscure reference, possibly a corruption of a technical Arabic term or a mythological allusion to the Moon as the "daughter" of the Sun's light</note>. 

In her entrance under the rays, she signifies a preparation for concealment; at the hour of division from the Sun, she signifies the preparation of her exit from concealment; and at the hour of her exit from under the rays, she signifies appearance and arrival from absence. While she is in the fourth aspect <term>square: an astrological aspect of 90 degrees, often considered a point of tension</term> of the Sun, she signifies the diminishing of a matter or a descent from a high place to a low one. This is the same in the second fourth aspect. However, in the first fourth aspect, she signifies the appearance of things, profit, and an increase in self-control; in the second fourth aspect of the Sun, she signifies the opposite of these things. 

And some have said that from the Moon's conjunction until her first half-light <note>the first quarter moon</note>, her nature is moist. From the half-light until her fullness, her nature is heat. From fullness until her second half-light <note>the last quarter moon</note>, her nature is dryness. From the second half-light until the conjunction, her nature is cold. 

The remaining planets, from their rising until their first station <term>stationary-retrograde: when a planet appears to stop in the sky before moving backward</term>, will be moist in nature and signify childhood. From their first station until their opposition to the Sun, their nature is heat, signifying youth. From this place until the second station <term>stationary-direct: when a planet stops before moving forward again</term>, their nature is dryness, signifying the perfect age <note>maturity</note>. From their second station until their first concealment <note>becoming hidden by the Sun's light</note>, their nature is cold, signifying a cold old age. 

And some have said that the **Sun** signifies the spirit—that is, the vital soul. The **Moon** signifies thought and sense. **Saturn** signifies sorrow, sadness, worthlessness, and evil. **Jupiter** signifies wisdom and reason. **Mars** signifies anger, fury, and speed. **Venus** signifies play and joy. **Mercury** signifies rationality, or dialectic <note>the art of logical argument</note> and discipline. And because we have spoken in this chapter about the significance of the planets when they embrace another planet, a similar consideration must be made for the other things that the planets signify.

### Concerning the order of the planets and their lordship in the conception of children, and for as long as they are in the mother's womb.

It is to be known that the first month from the hour of conception belongs to **Saturn**. The second to **Jupiter**. The third to **Mars**. The fourth to the **Sun**. The fifth to **Venus**. The sixth to **Mercury**. The seventh to the **Moon**.

<summary>This page details the Moon's physical appearance and her influence over the human lifecycle and psychological states, concluding with the planetary rulers assigned to each month of pregnancy.</summary>
<keywords>Moon, planetary phases, life stages, planetary nature, conception, pregnancy months, Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 33 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary influences on human life, moving from the specific phases of the Moon to the planetary rulers of the months of gestation and the various stages of a person's life, followed by the nature of the Lunar Nodes and the system of planetary hours.</meta>

The eighth month of pregnancy belongs to Saturn; for this reason, a child born in the eighth month does not survive, because they are born under the dominion of Saturn. The ninth month belongs to Jupiter. The lordship or ordering of the planets over human life—that is, how they govern the life of the newborn—is divided as follows: The Moon begins from the child’s entry into the world—that is, from birth—and governs for the duration of the nursing years, which are four years. Then Mercury governs for ten years. Then Venus for eight. Afterwards, the Sun for 19. Then Mars for fifteen. Then Jupiter for twelve. Finally, Saturn governs until the end of life.

### Concerning the nature of the Dragon’s Head and Tail.

The Dragon’s Head <note>original: "Caput draconis"; this refers to the North or Ascending Node of the Moon</note> is masculine. Likewise, it is a fortune <term>benefic: a point or planet in astrology believed to have a positive, helpful influence</term> and its nature is composed of the combined natures of Jupiter and Venus; this signifies kingship, good fortune, and material substance. Some have said that its nature is one of increase; for when it is placed with a "fortune" <note>a lucky planet</note>, it increases their good fortune, but when it is with "evils" <note>malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, it increases their malice. Its *firdaria* <term>firdaria: a Persian system of planetary periods used in medieval astrology to predict the stages of a person's life</term> years are three. ¶ The Dragon’s Tail <note>original: "Cauda"; the South or Descending Node of the Moon</note>, however, is evil. Its nature is composed of the natures of Saturn and Mars, and it signifies dejection, downfall, and poverty. Some have said that its nature signifies decrease; for when it is with the "fortunes," it diminishes their good fortune, and when it is with "evils," it diminishes their malice. Therefore, it is said that the Head is a fortune when with fortunes and evil when with evils; and the Tail is evil with the good and good with the evil <note>because the Tail acts as a "diminisher," reducing the impact of whichever planet it is near</note>. Its *firdaria* years are two.

### Concerning the hours of the day and night and which planets they belong to.

If a day or night belongs to a certain planet, the first hour will belong to that planet, and the second hour to the planet that succeeds it <note>following the "Chaldean Order" of the spheres: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon</note>. The succession of the others follows the same logic. For example: on Friday <note>original: "die ueneris"; the Day of Venus</note>, the first hour belongs to Venus itself, the second to Mercury, the third to the Moon, the fourth to Saturn, the fifth to Jupiter, the sixth to Mars, the seventh to the Sun, the eighth again to Venus, the ninth to Mercury, the tenth to the Moon, the eleventh to Saturn, and the twelfth to Jupiter. Behold, you have the 12 unequal hours of the day <term>unequal hours: also called "seasonal hours," these were calculated by dividing the period between sunrise and sunset into twelve equal parts, meaning the length of an "hour" changed depending on the season</term>. Likewise for the hours of night: The first hour of the following night belongs to Mars, who also rules the whole night. The second belongs to the Sun, the third to Venus, the fourth to Mercury, the fifth to the Moon, the sixth to Saturn, the seventh to Jupiter, the eighth again to Mars, the ninth to the Sun, the tenth to Venus, the eleventh to Mercury, and the twelfth to the Moon. Thus you also have 12 unequal hours of the night. After this begins the day of the Sabbath <note>Saturday</note>, whose first hour belongs to Saturn, and the whole day is his. By counting in this order, you will find the hours of the days and nights and their divisions among the individual planets, God willing.

### Concerning masculine and feminine hours.

The hours are also said to be masculine and feminine: the first hour of every day and night is masculine, the second feminine, the third masculine, the fourth feminine. Thus they succeed one another in order—one being feminine, the other masculine—until the end of the day and night.

<meta>This page continues from the discussion of the Dragon’s Head and Tail (the Lunar Nodes) and their influence on the human lifespan. ## The third distinction regarding those things which happen to the seven planets within themselves and what happens to them from one another.

<image-desc>A large square woodcut decorative initial 'E' featuring dense white-line interlacing strapwork and foliate scrolls on a dark background.</image-desc>
And because, by the grace of God, we have now completed our study of the state of the planets in themselves and what they signify, let us proceed by describing what happens to them internally and from one another—that is, the influence of certain planets upon others.

The significance of the planets within themselves is determined by whether a planet is ascending in the circle of its <term>apogee: the point in an orbit farthest from the Earth</term> <note>original: "augis"</note>, where it is smaller in light, size, and speed; this occurs when there are fewer than 90 degrees between the planet and its apogee, whether before or after it. If, however, there are exactly 90 degrees between the planet and the apogee, it will be average in light, size, and speed. But if it is outside these two positions—that is, more than 90 degrees—it will be descending from the circle of its apogee and will appear greater in light and size, and faster in its course. Furthermore, if its adjusted portion is less than 180 degrees, it is said to be "increasing in number." If it is more, it is "decreasing in number." If its adjusted portion is exactly 180 or 360 degrees, it is neither increasing nor decreasing. If the adjusted calculation is added to its mean motion, it is called "increasing in course or number," and if the calculation is subtracted, it is called "decreasing in number." When any of the higher planets—namely Saturn ♄, Jupiter ♃, and Mars ♂—travels faster than its mean motion, it is said to increase its course; if it travels slower, it is said to decrease its course; and if it travels at exactly that rate, it is said to be equal in course. When any of the lower planets <note>Mercury and Venus</note> travels faster than the mean motion of the Sun, it will be increasing its course; when it travels slower, it is called decreasing; and when it travels at that exact rate, it is equal in course. The motion of the <term>Luminaries: the Sun and the Moon</term> follows the same rules as the three higher planets.

A planet is also considered "northerly" when it has crossed its <term>node: the point where a planet's path crosses the ecliptic</term> <note>original: "genzahar," from the Persian term for the nodes</note>—that is, when it has crossed the path of the Sun moving from the south to the north. That crossing—the intersection of the orbital circles—is called the node. When a planet has passed that intersection and there are fewer than 90 degrees between the planet and the intersection, the planet is "northerly ascending." When it is between 90 and 180 degrees, it is "northerly descending." When it passes this number—that is, when it moves toward 270 degrees—it is "southerly descending." And when it passes that number moving toward 360 degrees, it is "southerly ascending." This describes the state of the planets in themselves.

<image-desc>A smaller decorative woodcut initial 'D' with a stylized foliate pattern inside a double-lined square frame.</image-desc>
Let us now treat the state of the planets in relation to one another—that is, what happens to them. Specifically, we will discuss what happens to the planets in relation to the Luminaries, and we shall speak of what Ptolemy said regarding the <term>almugea: a specific astrological relationship where planets "face" one another based on the positions of their zodiacal houses</term> <note>original: "almugea," from the Arabic "al-muwajaha," meaning "confrontation" or "face-to-face"</note>. This occurs when a planet and the Sun are positioned so that the planet is "western"—that is, when it follows the Sun by the same number of signs that exist between that planet's house and the Sun's house. Alternatively, it occurs between a planet and the Moon when the planet is "eastern" to the Moon—that is, when the Moon follows the planet by the same distance that exists between the planet's house and the Moon's house. In other words, it is when a planet is distant from the Sun by the same amount that its house is distant from the Sun's house.

<summary>This page explains how a planet's appearance and speed change based on its distance from its apogee and its orbital nodes. it also introduces the concept of "almugea," a relative positioning system between planets and the Sun or Moon based on their astrological houses.</summary>
<keywords>apogee, planetary motion, nodes, genzahar, almugea, Ptolemy, astronomy, medieval astrology, superior planets, inferior planets</keywords>

--- Page 35 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary relationships. The previous page concluded by defining how a planet's speed and light change based on its distance from its apogee (its furthest point from Earth). This page shifts focus to the specific geometric and solar relationships known as Almugea, Ductoria, and the various stages of a planet being "burnt" or hidden by the Sun's light.</meta>

...Similarly, it is said of the moon: that when a planet follows the sun and Saturn is, for example, in the 6th sign; and Jupiter in the 5th, Mars in the 4th, Venus in the 3rd, Mercury in the 2nd, then it is said to be in the <term>almugea: a specific planetary configuration where planets are arranged in a specific sequence of signs relative to the Sun or Moon</term> <note>original: "almugea"</note> of the sun. Similarly, when this same number of signs exists between each planet and the moon following it, each planet is said to be in the almugea of the moon.

### On the ductoria, that is, the security of a planet.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'E' featuring a scholar or astronomer seated at a desk with books.</image-desc>From this comes the <term>ductoria: the "leadership" or "security" of a planet</term> <note>original: "ductoria"</note>, which means that a planet is in its <term>haiz: a condition of planetary dignity where a planet is in its preferred "sect" (day or night) and side of the horizon</term> <note>original: "haiz"</note>—that is, in the part proper to itself—and in one of the angles of the <term>ascendant: the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</term>, and [near] one of the <term>luminaries: the sun or the moon</term>; similarly, in a location like it in the quadrant, namely in some angle. This occurs so that during the day the planet is oriental (rising before) the sun, and at night it is occidental (setting after) the moon. Every planet is said to be in its "ductoria" (according to some) when it is 60 degrees between the oriental planet and the sun. 

And every planet, from the time it is covered by the rays of the sun until it appears from under those rays, is called <term>combust: "burnt" or hidden by the sun's overwhelming light</term> <note>original: "combustus"</note>; and when it begins to enter the rays, it is said to have "begun to be burnt"; and while it is hidden under the rays and within 12 degrees of the sun, it is called <term>oppressed</term> <note>original: "oppreſſus"</note>. When it is with the sun in the same degree, such that there are 16 minutes or less between them—and similarly in its latitude—it is called <term>united</term> <note>original: "vnitus"; often called "cazimi" or being in the heart of the sun</note>; and when it has passed that union, seeking its exit until it can be seen, it is called <term>escaped</term> <note>original: "euaſus"</note>. 

And from the moment the three higher planets <note>Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars</note> appear from under the rays and begin to rise—that is, to appear in the morning before the sun—this is when they are nearer to the circle of the eastern hemisphere until they reach opposition; they are then called "right-hand orientals." From the time they pass opposition until they are joined again to the sun, they are called "left-hand occidentals." 

As for Venus and Mercury, from the time either of them separates from the degrees of the sun in the middle of its <term>retrogradation: the period when a planet appears to move backward in the sky</term> <note>original: "retrogradatiōis"</note>, and they appear until they are burnt by the sun in their direct motion, they are called "oriental"; and from the time they separate from the sun in the middle of their direct motion until they are burnt again in their retrogradation, they are called "occidental." When one is in its rising—that is, when they are oriental—they are called "right-hand" and are shown to be masculine; and in their setting—that is, when they are occidental—they are called "left-hand" and are shown to be feminine, and then they are stronger. 

The three higher planets, after they come out from under the sun's rays, are called "oriental" and are shown to increase in strength up to 30 degrees from the sun; after this, they are called "strong" or "stronger orientals" for another 30 degrees. When they have passed the sun by 60 degrees, they are called "oriental planets moving toward weakness" until they come to retrogradation; then they are named "oriental retrogrades" until they reach opposition. After this, they will be "occidental retrogrades" once they have passed opposition, until they arrive at direct motion. Then, from direct motion up to 60 degrees after direct motion—until their distance from the sun is 30 degrees—they are called "strong occidentals." Then, from a distance of 60 degrees after direct motion, they are called "occidentals moving toward weakness"; then they become "weak occidentals" until they enter under the rays of the sun. 

The lower planets <note>Venus and Mercury</note>, from the time they separate from the sun while retrograde, are called "weak orientals," and they do not cease to be so until they come to their direct motion; then they become "strong orientals" until their distance from the sun is the same as the sun's distance was from them when they began to weaken and move toward retrogradation. Then they become "weak orientals" until they are placed under the rays of the sun; after this, they are "conjoined," then "combust," moving toward their appearance until they are seen. From the time they separate from the sun in direct motion up to 60 degrees, they are "stronger"; and from 60 degrees until the hour of their retrogradation, they will be "strong occidentals"; and until they are with the sun, they will be "weak." When a planet has exited from under the sun's rays and has been joined to no other planet, it is said to be "in its own light." This is the state of the planets in relation to the <term>luminaries: the sun and moon</term>.

<summary>This page defines complex astrological conditions based on a planet's proximity to the Sun and Moon, including "combustion" (being hidden by solar light) and "retrogradation." it details how the strength and gender of a planet change depending on whether it rises before the Sun (oriental) or sets after it (occidental).</summary>
<keywords>almugea, ductoria, hayz, combust, oppressed, cazimi, retrograde, oriental, occidental, planetary strength, superior planets, inferior planets</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions on planetary movements. While the previous page dealt with the specific spatial positions of Almugea and Ductoria (the planetary "security" or "leadership" relative to the Sun and Moon), this page moves into the dynamics of how planets interact with each other through conjunctions, "void of course" states, and the various ways they "transfer" or "return" their light and nature.</meta>

# Concerning the Things That Happen to the Planets in Relation to One Another.

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'E' featuring a classical figure, possibly a scholar, seated within ornate scrolling foliage.</image-desc>

Their existence in relation to one another is the same thing as a <term>conjunction: the meeting of two planets in the same degree of the zodiac</term> <note>original: "coniunctio"</note>; that is, when two planets are in two signs that "behold" each other <note>i.e., they are in aspect</note> and the <term>lighter: a faster-moving planet, usually closer to the Earth like the Moon or Mercury</term> <note>original: "leuior"</note> is in its sign at a lower degree than the <term>heavier: a slower-moving planet, like Saturn or Jupiter</term> <note>original: "ponderosior"</note> in its own sign. If there are 6 degrees or fewer between them, then the lighter planet is said to go toward the conjunction of the heavier one. When their degrees become equal, their conjunction is perfected; and when it has passed beyond it, it is separated from it. This type is called a <term>conjunction of longitude: a meeting based on their position along the ecliptic path</term>. ¶ A conjunction of latitude, however, is when two planets are joined by <term>latitude: the distance a planet is north or south of the Sun’s path (the ecliptic)</term>. If there is an "application" <note>an approaching movement</note> of conjunction, it is necessary that their latitude be equal in one part. If it is a conjunction by <term>opposition: a relationship where planets are 180 degrees apart</term>, it is necessary that their latitudes be equal, so that the latitude of one is ascending in the north and the other is descending in the north, or one ascending in the south and the other descending in the south. Or, if there is an application from any of the other <term>aspects: the geometric angles planets form with each other, such as squares or trines</term>, it is necessary that the latitude of one be northern ascending and the other southern descending, and vice versa in the south; and this is the application of latitude which has been mentioned. ¶ And when one planet is separated from another and is joined to no other planet as long as it remains in that same sign, it is said to be <term>void of course: a state where a planet makes no further major connections before leaving its current sign</term> <note>original: "cursu vacuus"</note>. ¶ And when a planet is in some sign and no other planet "beholds" <note>aspects</note> that sign, the planet is said to be <term>feral or wild: isolated and acting without the influence of other planets</term> <note>original: "feralis vel agrestis"</note> for as long as it is in that sign. ¶ And when a light planet is separated from a heavier planet and is joined to another, it <term>transfers nature: the process of carrying the influence of a previous planet to a new one</term> from the first to the second. A planet also transfers nature in another way: that is, when a light planet is joined to a heavier planet, and that heavier one is itself joined to another heavier than itself; then it better transfers the nature of the lightest to the heaviest. ¶ If, however, one of these is not joined to the other, but each is joined to a third, then if that third planet beholds any place in the circle, it returns their light to that same place. This is called the <term>return of light: when a third planet acts as a bridge between two others</term> <note>original: "redditus luminis"</note>. Again, if one planet is not joined to another, but a third planet transfers light into both of them, then this conjunction is also called a return of light. ¶ Next follows <term>prohibition: an event where one planet prevents two others from completing a relationship</term> <note>original: "prohibitio"</note>, and it happens in two ways. One is from conjunction: this is when there are three planets in one sign but at different degrees; if the heavier one is at the highest degree, then the one in the middle "prohibits" the first one (the one at the lowest degree) from joining with the heaviest until it has passed through it. The second way is when two planets are in one sign and the lighter is joined to the heavier, but another is also joined to that same heavier one by an aspect; therefore, the one that is with it in the same sign prohibits the one that is aspecting from the conjunction of the heavier—provided, however, that the degrees of the one being joined and the one aspecting are equal (that is, of the same number). But if the one aspecting is closer to the degree of the heavier planet, the conjunction will belong to the one aspecting. ¶ And if a planet is joined to the lord of the sign in which it resides, or the lord of its <term>exaltation: a sign where a planet’s influence is most powerful</term>, or the lord of the other <term>dignities: the various signs and degrees where a planet has specific strengths</term> in which it resides, it is said to <term>pulsate: to "push" its power or nature toward the planet that rules it</term> <note>original: "pulsare"</note>—that is, to send the nature of that planet to its lord. ¶ And if a planet is in one of its own dignities and is joined to another planet that also has a share of dignity in that same place, it sends to it both of its natures (namely, its own and that of the one to which it is joined); and all of this "sending" is called <term>alcobol: an Arabic-derived term for "reception"</term> <note>original: "alcobol idest receptio"</note>.

<vocab>conjunction, latitude, longitude, application, aspect, void of course, feral, wild, transfer of nature, return of light, prohibition, pulsate, alcobol, reception</vocab>

<summary>This page explains the various ways planets interact geometrically, defining conjunctions of longitude and latitude and describing states such as "void of course" and "feral." It also introduces the complex rules for how planets "transfer" or "prohibit" influence and light, culminating in the concept of "alcobol" or planetary reception.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planetary conjunction, void of course, transfer of light, planetary dignity, alcobol, latitude, longitude, medieval astronomy</keywords>

--- Page 37 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary interactions. Having previously defined conjunctions and general planetary relationships, the text now details specific scenarios—often using Latinized Arabic terms—where a planetary connection is modified, redirected, or blocked by the movement of other celestial bodies.</meta>

Now follows the **return** <note>original: "redditus"</note>; that is, when a planet is joined to another planet that is **combust** <note>original: "conburitur"; a planet is "combust" when it is so close to the Sun that its power is "burnt up" or hidden by the Sun's light</note>—meaning it is under the sun’s rays—or is **retrograde** <note>appearing to move backward in the sky</note>. It returns its power to the other because of its own weakness, which it is not able to retain. In this case, if both planets are in an **angle** <term>Angle: The most powerful houses in an astrological chart (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th), representing the four corners of the sky</term> or in the **succeding** houses <note>original: "succedentibus"; the houses that "follow" the angles</note>, the return will be conspicuous. Similarly, it is significant if only the one being joined is in an angle and the one it joins receives it. However, if the planet that is joining is **falling from an angle** <note>original: "cadens"; located in a "cadent" house, which were considered the weakest positions</note> and the one it joins is in an angle or a succeeding house, or if both are falling, the return will be with detriment.

From this follows **refraining** <note>original: "almene" or "refrenatio"</note>, which happens when a planet intends to join another, but before it can join, it becomes retrograde, and thus its conjunction is destroyed. ¶ This is followed by **accidental contrariety** <note>original: "alichozad"</note>; this occurs when some light planet is at many degrees in a sign and another heavier planet is at fewer degrees. A third planet, even lighter than the first, wishes to join the heavier one; but before it can join him, that lighter planet which had more degrees becomes retrograde and joins or "applies" to the heavy one. Passing him, it then joins the other lighter planet, and thus the first one’s conjunction with the heavy planet is destroyed.

Next is **frustration** <note>original: "alfazim" or "frustratio"</note>. This also happens when some planet seeks a conjunction with another planet, but before it reaches him, that target planet moves into another sign. If there is some planet at a low degree "beholding" <note>in aspect to</note> that sign, and its rays are at the beginning of the sign, then as soon as the pursuing planet leaves the first sign, it joins with this "beholding" planet instead, and the conjunction it had with the first planet is annulled.

<margin>the cutting off of light</margin>
Next follows the **cutting off of light** <note>original: "abscisio luminis"</note>. This is when one planet seeks a conjunction with another, and there is a third planet in the sign immediately following the target’s sign; but before the first can join the target, the one in the second sign becomes retrograde. It joins the target first and "cuts off" its light from the planet that wanted to join it. Similarly, if a planet is going toward a conjunction with another, and that target planet itself seeks a conjunction with a planet heavier than itself; before the light planet can reach the degrees of the heavier one, the heavier one joins with an even heavier planet, cutting off the light of the first, lighter planet.

->**Concerning fortunate and unfortunate places.**<-

There are also places for these planets in which they are strengthened and in which they are weakened; places where they become fortunate and places where they become "malific" <note>unfortunate or harmful</note>. The places where they become fortunate are these: namely, when they are in **good aspects** <note>original: "aspectibus bonorum"</note>—that is, in a useful aspect such as a **sextile** <term>Sextile: An aspect where planets are 60 degrees apart, considered harmonious</term> or a **trine aspect** <term>Trine: An aspect where planets are 120 degrees apart, considered the most fortunate relationship</term>, which is called by some "hexagonal radiation" and "trigonal radiation." Also, they are fortunate when "malific" planets are falling away from them, and they are separated from misfortune and joined to a "fortune" <note>a lucky planet like Jupiter or Venus</note>. Or, they may be **besieged** <note>original: "obsessi"</note> by fortunate planets or their rays; that is, when they are between two fortunate planets or between the rays of fortunate planets. ¶ This is "besiegement": when a planet possesses a "fortune" or its rays on either side.

### On the misfortune of planets.

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'E' featuring a stylized floral or scrollwork design within a square frame.</image-desc>

From the misfortune indeed of planets and their destruction is that they are in **conjunction** with **malefics** <term>Malefics: Traditionally Saturn and Mars, planets associated with challenge or hardship</term>: or in **opposition** to them: or in their **square** <note>original: "tetragona"</note> or **trine** radiation: or if there is between them and the body of a malefic, or its rays, less than the **term** <note>original: "termino"; a specific degree-range within a sign</note> of the planet: or if they are in the terms of malefics: or in their houses: or if malefics are raised above them at the 1st or 11th from their places: and it is much worse if they do not **receive** them. Or if they are in conjunction with the sun: or in opposition to it: or in square radiation: or if it is at the heads of its <term>genzahar</term> <note>original: "genzahar"; an Arabic term for the nodes where the planetary orbits cross the ecliptic</note>: or with its tails: or with the <term>head of the dragon</term> <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> or the tail, and they are between them and between any of these places within 22 degrees or less: and especially if the moon is in any of them: similarly the sun is then more hindered by them, that is, by the head or tail when they are between it and one of them, even 4 degrees before or behind: or if a planet is **besieged** <note>original: "obsessus"</note> between two malefics: this is when a planet is in some sign and with it a malefic: or its rays before it: and a malefic or its rays behind it: or if it is separated from a malefic through conjunction: or through an aspect: and is joined in such a way to another malefic: or if a malefic, or its rays, is in the sign which is before it: that is, in the second from it: and in the sign which is behind it: that is, in the 12th from it, there is another malefic: or its rays. Similarly, it is said of signs that they are besieged. But if a **fortune** [a benefic planet] or the sun beholds that same besieged planet: or the besieged sign from a trine: or from a fertile aspect, and it is between it and the conjunction less than seven degrees, it dissolves that malice or siege. And from this [misfortune] is when a planet is retrogra-

a relationship where one planet is in a sign ruled by another</note>. ## On the friendship and hatred of the planets.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'E' featuring a floral and foliage pattern within a square border.</image-desc>

And it is said of the planets that some of them love one another and others hate each other: for concerning those that love, some of the ancients have said that **Jupiter** loves all the planets and is their friend, and they are his, except for **Mars**. The friends of **Venus** are all the planets, and all love her, except for **Saturn**. ¶ The friends of **Saturn**, however, are **Jupiter**, the **Sun**, and the **Moon**, and his enemies are **Mars** and **Venus**, and the latter hates him the most. ¶ And the friend of **Mars** is **Venus**, and the other planets hold him in hatred, especially **Jupiter** and the **Sun**. ¶ The friends of the **Sun** are truly **Jupiter** and **Venus**: and his enemies are **Mars**, **Mercury**, and the **Moon**. ¶ The friends of **Mercury** are **Jupiter**, **Venus**: and **Saturn** only: and his enemies are the **Sun**, the **Moon**, and **Mars**. The friends of the **Moon**, however, are **Jupiter**, **Venus**, and **Saturn**: and her enemies are **Mars** and **Mercury**. The friends of the **Dragon's Head** <term>Dragon's Head: The North Node of the Moon, generally considered auspicious</term> are **Jupiter** and **Venus**: and its enemies are **Saturn** and **Mars**. Truly, the friends of the **Dragon's Tail** <term>Dragon's Tail: The South Node of the Moon, generally considered inauspicious</term> are **Saturn** and **Mars**: and its enemies are the **Sun**, the **Moon**, **Jupiter**, and **Venus**. And there are other types of enmity: when there are two planets having opposite houses: such as **Mars** and **Venus**: and when they are joined, it is called the **opening of the gates** <note>original: "apertio portarum"; a term often used in weather forecasting or significant astrological changes</note>. A third type of enmity is when the **exaltations** <term>Exaltation: A specific sign where a planet's influence is heightened or "honored"</term> of two planets are opposite. However, the strongest friendship of the planets is: when planet agrees with planet in nature, quality, substance, and power, just as **Mars** agrees with the **Sun**: because both agree in heat and dryness and sharpness and speed: and he is the lord of his exaltation in which his strength appears: and as the **Moon** and **Venus** agree in cold and moisture: and she is the Mistress of his exaltation. When, moreover, two planets agree in nature and substance: as **Jupiter** and **Venus**, they are friends.

<meta>This page transitions from the general debilities of planets discussed on the previous page into a specific taxonomy of planetary conjunctions and a technical discussion on the "Animmodar" method—a technique used to rectify or find the exact degree of the eastern horizon (ascendant) at the moment of birth.</meta>

# Fourth Distinction on the explanation of astrological terms.

And let us now proceed with the explanation of astrological terms. The beginning of these is the **Conjunction** and the things signifying destructions or mutations that occur in this world from conjunctions. There are six types of these: the greatest of all conjunctions is the conjunction of **Saturn and Jupiter** at the beginning of Aries, which occurs every 960 years <note>original: "magna conjunctio"; these rare alignments were historically believed to signal the rise and fall of empires or religions</note>. 

The second type of conjunction is at the beginning of each **triplicity** <term>Triplicity: A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element—Fire, Earth, Air, or Water</term>, which occurs every 240 years. For they are joined twelve times in each triplicity, and perhaps they make 13 conjunctions in one triplicity; then their conjunction shifts to the triplicity that follows it. 

The third conjunction is that of **Saturn and Mars** at the beginning of Cancer, which occurs every 30 years. 

The fourth is the conjunction of **Jupiter and Saturn** in every sign, which occurs every 20 years. 

The fifth is the descent of the **greater light** <note>The Sun</note> into the point of the vernal equinox—namely the entry of the Sun into the "head" or beginning of Aries—which happens every year. 

The sixth is the conjunction of the **lights** <note>The Sun and Moon, creating a New Moon</note> and their opposition <note>A Full Moon</note>, which occurs in the middle of each lunar month. 

And when the **ascendant of the conjunction** is mentioned, it means the **ascendant of the world**; that is, the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the hour of the Sun’s entry into the aforementioned point of the vernal equinox at the beginning of that year in which the said conjunction is to occur.

### Concerning the Animmodar, that is, the investigation of the degree of the ascendant of births.

<margin>
f. 1. through... ... in three
f. 2. through... signs
f. through... ...
f. through... ...
27 from here...
f. degree d. or
through... ... through...
regarding the birth
</margin>
From this **Animmodar** <term>Animmodar: A technical method used to determine the exact degree of the Ascendant when the birth time is only approximately known</term>, which is the investigation of the degree of the ascendant of someone's birth: many astrologers think that the same degree of the ascendant of someone's birth can be found through it. But this is false, and I have already made this clear in my book which I wrote about the **Animmodar**. But there is found through it the most worthy of all degrees of the circle after the degree of the ascendant at that same hour according to the natural course; and it coincides many times—that is, it happens occasionally—that the degree <margin>through us</margin> of the ascendant is found through it. 

The knowledge of this matter is that you should consider the degree of the **conjunction** or the degree of the **prevention** <term>Prevention (preventionis): The New Moon or Full Moon immediately preceding a birth</term> which occurred before the birth itself. If the conjunction was closer to that birth, the birth is called **conjunctional**; and if the prevention was prior to it, it is called **preventional**. That is, in whichever of them the birth occurred—namely conjunction or prevention—the said birth is named in that same manner and name. 

Because the degree of conjunction is the same degree in which the lights are joined, there is no investigation needed, nor does it require explanation. In the prevention, however, because each of the lights <note>The Sun and Moon</note> is in its own degree—that is, in different degrees—it is necessary for us to know which of these degrees is to be understood as the degree of prevention. 

And **Ptolemy** <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century author of the 'Tetrabiblos', the most influential astrological text of the middle ages</note> has already said that the degree of that light which was above the earth is the degree of prevention. Some of the wise men have said that if it should happen in the prevention that one light is in the degree of the east and the other in the degree of the west, then the degree of the east shall be the degree of prevention. And **Vellisius** <note>Likely Vettius Valens, a prominent 2nd-century Greek astrologer</note> said that the degree of prevention is the degree in which the **completion** <note>The Full Moon</note> occurs, meaning the degree of the Moon; but because this is the work of Ptolemy, we must in this matter return to his opinion. 

Therefore, when the degree of conjunction or the degree of prevention has become most certainly clear, you shall establish the **ascending degree** by estimating the hour of birth, and the four angles, and the remaining houses. Then you shall look at the degree of the conjunction, or the degree [by]...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for rectifying a birth chart. It follows the previous discussion on planetary conjunctions by focusing on the "Animmodar" method—a system attributed to Ptolemy for finding the exact degree of the ascendant—and begins the vital calculation of the "Hyleg," or the planetary "Giver of Life."</meta>

...[the degree] which was present before the birth: and which planet was more worthy or stronger in it at that same hour of the **conjunction** or **prevention** <note>The "conjunction" refers to the New Moon preceding birth, and "prevention" refers to the preceding Full Moon</note> through a multitude of dignities or powers. That is, you will consider which planet has the greatest lordship in that same place. Therefore, you shall rectify this planet to the estimated hour of birth. Then you will see whether the degree of that planet is in a sign where it is closer to the degree of the **first house** <note>The Ascendant</note> or the degree of the midheaven; and to whichever of these it is closer, you shall set this angle <note>The house cusp</note> according to the degree and minute of that planet, and you will divide the twelve houses by it. 

For Ptolemy said: if several planets agree in the lordship of the same place and are equal in strength, you shall appoint as the lord of the place the one who is the **lord of the house** <note>original: "dominus haim"; this refers to the Almuten, the planet with the most essential dignity at a specific point</note>. But if they are also equal in this, you shall appoint the one who is most quickly changing from its current state to a better one. That is, the one about to leave a sign where it currently resides and enter another in which it will have more dignities; or, if they are **oriental** of the Sun <term>Oriental: A planet that rises before the Sun in the morning, considered a position of strength</term>, you shall choose the one closer to the Sun that is not **under the rays** <term>Under the rays: A planet so close to the Sun—usually within 15 degrees—that it is obscured by its light and weakened</term>. If, however, a planet is in an angle, that one is to be chosen which is closer to the degree of that same angle; and understand this well.

### On the Hyleg: the Giver of Life

From this comes the **Hyleg** <note>original: "hylech"; from the Persian term for the "Giver of Life" or "Releaser," used to determine the length of life and health</note>, which is the "place of life" in nativities. The knowledge of it is as follows: first, look at the hour of birth. If it was during the day, you take the calculation from the **Sun**. If the Sun is within five degrees before the degree of the ascendant or below it, or if it is in the 10th or 11th houses—whether the sign itself be masculine or feminine—it will be suitable to be the Hyleg. But if it is in these three places—namely in the 7th, 8th, or 9th houses—in a masculine sign, it will likewise be suitable to be the Hyleg. If, however, it is in these three places in a feminine sign, it will not be suitable to be the Hyleg. 

From here, look to the **Moon**. If she is in the ascendant, or in the 2nd or 3rd houses, or in the 7th or 8th, she will be a suitable Hyleg whether the sign is masculine or feminine. And if she is within five degrees before the degree of the ascendant or below it, or in the 10th or 11th, or in the 4th or 5th, or in the 7th house in a feminine sign, she is suited to be the Hyleg. But if she is in any of these in a masculine sign, she will not be a suitable Hyleg if it is a day birth.

If, however, the birth was at night, let us begin from the Moon; if she is in any of the aforesaid places according to what we stated before, she will be a suitable Hyleg. If the Moon is not in them, look after this to the Sun. If the Sun is within five degrees before the 7th house cusp or below it, or if it is in the 4th or 5th houses, it will be suitable to be the Hyleg whether the sign is masculine or feminine. If indeed it is within five degrees before the ascendant or below it, or in the ascendant, or in the 2nd house in a masculine sign, it is suited to be the Hyleg. If, however, it is in any of these places in a feminine sign, it will not be a suitable Hyleg.

But if neither **luminary** <note>The Sun or Moon</note> is suitable to be the Hyleg, look after this at the birth: whether it is **conjunctional** or **preventional** <note>meaning, did the birth follow a New Moon or a Full Moon</note>. If the birth was conjunctional and the degree of that conjunction is in any of the **angles** <term>Angles: The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, the strongest points in a chart</term>, or in a **succedent** angle <term>Succedent: The houses following the angles—the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th</term>, it will be in a place suitable for the Hyleg. If, however, the aforesaid degree is **cadent** <note>falling away from the angles</note> from these eight places—that is, from the angles and the succedent houses—it will not be the Hyleg. 

You shall then look at the degree of the **Part of Fortune** <note>A mathematical point representing prosperity and health</note>; if it is in any of the angles or in the succedent houses, it will be in a place suitable for the Hyleg. If it is cadent from these eight places, look after this at the degree of the ascendant and appoint it as the Hyleg. If the birth followed a Full Moon (preventional), you shall begin from the degree of that prevention. You shall examine it just as you did before with the degree of conjunction, then the degree of the Part of Fortune, and then the degree of the ascendant in the same order we described for the conjunction.

<summary>This page details the technical search for the Hyleg, or "Life-Giver," a central point in medieval astrology used to predict a person's lifespan. It provides a complex hierarchy of rules based on the time of day, the gender of zodiac signs, and the specific house positions of the Sun, Moon, and previous lunar phases.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining the "Hyleg" (the planetary Giver of Life) and begins the calculation for the "Alcochoden" (the Giver of Years), which medieval astrologers used to predict a person's lifespan.</meta>

[...dignities]. You shall not worry about the gender of the signs <note>original: "De sexu aut signorum nō curabis"</note>: that is, you shall not look at whether those signs are masculine or feminine, except only regarding the **luminaries** <note>The Sun and the Moon</note>, specifically at the degree of the **conjunction** or **prevention** <note>The New Moon or Full Moon preceding the birth</note>, the ascendant, and the **Part of Fortune**. You should not consider whether they are in masculine or feminine signs, whether in the **angles** <term>Angles: The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, the most powerful positions in a chart</term> or in the houses succeeding the angles. However, these four points will only be fit to be the **Hyleg** <term>Hyleg: From the Persian 'haylaj,' the planetary 'Giver of Life' used to determine vitality</term> when they are in these eight places: namely, in the angles or their succeeding houses. If, however, they are in the receding places—that is, the houses falling away from the angles <note>known as cadent houses</note>—they will not be fit to be the Hyleg.

It appears, therefore, that the **Sun** is fit to be the Hyleg by day and night, both above the earth and below the earth, in eleven places: six places above the earth and five below. The **Moon** likewise will be fit to be the Hyleg by day and night, above the earth and below the earth, similarly in eleven places: five above the earth and six below. Along with this, if the Moon is in these places but remains **under the rays** of the Sun <note>Within 15 degrees of the Sun, where its light is obscured</note>, it will not be fit to be the Hyleg. You shall look for the Hyleg in the angles and their succeeding houses according to how the twelve houses of the circle are equated by the degrees of the hours of the **ascendant**, just as its work is explained in the *Canon of Planets* (that is, in the book of the courses of the planets).

And when you have equated the houses in this way: every planet which is located within five degrees or less before the degree of the ascending sign or any other house cusp, its strength shall be considered valid in the house that follows it. Every place among the locations we have mentioned is fit to be the Hyleg if it is "aspected" <note>An 'aspect' is a specific geometric angle between planets, like a square or trine, allowing them to 'see' each other</note> by any of its lords: whether it be the **lord of the house**, the **lord of exaltation**, the **lord of the term**, the **lord of the triplicity**, or the **lord of the face** <note>These are the five 'essential dignities' or layers of planetary authority over a specific degree of the zodiac</note>. If not, it will not be fit to be the Hyleg.

### About the Alcochoden. <margin>of years</margin>

<margin>alcochoden</margin>
**A**nd from this is derived the **Alcochoden** <term>Alcochoden: From the Persian 'al-kadhkhudah,' the 'Giver of Years' which determines the length of life</term>, who is the significator of life—that is, the lord of the years or the giver of years. When you wish to know this, and you have already identified the Hyleg in the order I previously described, you shall look at the lord of the house of the Hyleg, or the lord of its exaltation, or the lord of its term, or the lord of its triplicity, or its face. You must see which of these is stronger and more authoritative in the position of the Hyleg. If that planet aspects the Hyleg, it shall be the most worthy Alcochoden. 

But if the one with the most authority does not aspect the Hyleg, you should look to the one with the next most authority until you find one among them who does aspect it. If none of them aspect the Hyleg, then 그 place cannot be the Hyleg; you must then seek another Hyleg, and again seek the Alcochoden from that second Hyleg in the same order we described. 

If two or three planets are equal in authority at the degree of the Hyleg, and both aspect it, the one that is stronger by its own position shall be the worthier Alcochoden. If they are truly equal in the strength of their positions (that is, where the planets themselves are located), then that planet which is closer in degree to the Hyleg shall be the Alcochoden.

To some, however, it seemed better to look first to the lord of the house; if it aspected the Hyleg, they appointed it as the Alcochoden and considered no other. If they did not find the lord of the house aspecting the Hyleg, they took the lord of exaltation; if they saw it aspecting the Hyleg, they appointed it as the Alcochoden and considered no other. They did the same with the lords of the triplicity, term, and face in order. **Dorotheus** <note>Dorotheus of Sidon, a highly influential 1st-century Greek astrologer</note>, however, placed the lord of the term before the lord of the house in this matter. 

And some have said that if two or three or more planets are equal in dignity and in the closeness of their aspect to the degree of the Hyleg, then the Alcochoden shall be the one in a "laudable place" from the Sun: that is, the one who is with the Sun in a single degree within 16 minutes or less <note>A position known as 'Cazimi' or 'in the heart of the Sun,' considered extremely powerful</note>, or the one closer to the degree of an angle, or...
