<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining the Alcochoden (the Giver of Years) and introduces the "Almuten" or ruler of the birth. It then moves into a new section regarding "Profections," a method used to predict themes for specific years of a person's life.</meta>

<page-type>text</page-type>
<sig>c iiij</sig>

at the beginning of its rising: that is, when it appears in the morning, or when it is in its second station <note>The point where a planet appears to stand still before changing direction</note>, or in some other praiseworthy state relative to the Sun as we said before, that planet will be the **Alcochoden** <term>Alcochoden: From the Persian 'shah-khuda,' the planet that determines the length of life based on its strength and position</term>. If, however, the degree of the Sun is the **Hyleg**, and it is in Aries or Leo, then the Sun will be both the Hyleg and the Alcochoden together, and no other Alcochoden besides it shall be considered. Similarly, if the degree of the Moon is the Hyleg and it is in Taurus or Cancer, take it as both the Hyleg and the Alcochoden together, and you shall not consider any other Alcochoden besides it.

The **Almuten** <term>Almuten: The planet with the most dignity or "power" over specific points in a birth chart</term> is the planet that presides over the birth. By this planet, the state of the native <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note> is signified after the Hyleg and Alcochoden. This planet holds more authority than the others over the **Ascendant**, the places of the **Luminaries** <note>The Sun and Moon</note>, the place of the **Part of Fortune**, and also the place of the **conjunction** or **prevention** <note>The New Moon or Full Moon</note> that occurred before the birth. If any planet presides over two, three, four, or more of these places through the magnitude of its power, that planet will be the Almuten—that is, the author and significator of life after the Hyleg and Alcochoden. Through it, the state of the native is signified, and some even consider it to be the Alcochoden for the giving of life.

->**On the Profection of the Signs**<-

<margin>Keep well in mind the counting</margin>
From this comes the "sign of profection" <term>Profection: An ancient timing technique where a point in the chart is moved forward by one zodiac sign for every year of life</term> in the years of births and of the world. For in the years of births, there is a science: you must look at how many completed solar years have passed for the native. For every year, you will look at one sign, starting from the Ascendant of the native and following the succession of the signs until the number is finished. The sign that follows is the sign of the profection from the Ascendant—specifically, the sign of the entering year, which you did not include in the count. This will be at the same degree as it was in the Ascendant of the birth. The lord of that sign is called the Alcochoden <note>original: "Alcochoden"</note>, whose interpretation is "Lord of the Year." Similarly, you shall count one sign for every year from the sign of the Sun, and through this, the sign of profection from the Sun will emerge. Likewise from the sign of the Moon, from the **Midheaven** <note>original: "medio celi," the highest point of the chart</note>, and from the Part of Fortune, in the same way as the degree of the **root** <note>The original positions at the moment of birth</note>. 

An example of this: someone was born with **Capricorn** rising at its 17th degree; the Sun was in **Pisces** at the 15th degree; the Midheaven was in **Scorpio** at the 8th degree; the Moon in **Libra** at the 15th degree; and the Part of Fortune in **Leo** at the 17th degree. Now, three full years have passed for the native. The fourth year from the Ascendant will reach **Aries**, which is the fourth sign from Capricorn, as far as the 17th degree of that same Aries. **Mars** will then be the Lord of the Year. The place of the Sun will have reached **Gemini** at its 15th degree; the Moon will have reached **Capricorn** at the 15th degree; the profection from the Midheaven will have reached the 8th degree of **Aquarius**; and the profection from the Part of Fortune will have reached the 17th degree of **Scorpio**. 

When the year reaches the degree of profection—as in the 17th degree of Aries in this example—and there is a planet or its rays between that degree and the similar degree of the following sign which the next year must reach in that same house, and you wish to know when the profection reaches that planet or its rays: you shall look at the distance between the degree the year reaches and the planet or rays in degrees and minutes. You shall multiply that by 12 and a sixth of that number which you are multiplying (or by 12 degrees and 1/6th of a degree), and the result will be the days of that year in which...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological calculations, specifically transitioning from the length of life (Alcochoden) to the methods of "Profections" and "Directions" used for timing events in history and individual lives. It references the works of the philosopher Al-Kindi.</meta>

<page-type>text</page-type>

...you will be in those [signs] where the profection reaches from the degree in which it begins up to the degree to which you have counted; after that, the profection of that planet itself occurs. ¶ The **profection** <term>Profection: A symbolic timing technique where a point in the chart is moved forward, usually by one zodiac sign per year</term> from the **years of the world** <note>The 'Annus Mundi' or years since the beginning of a specific era or the world's creation</note>, as **Al-Kindi** <note>Al-Kindi (c. 801–873 AD) was a famous Arab philosopher and astrologer</note> said, is that which exists between the year of the conjunction <note>The Great Conjunction of Jupiter and Saturn</note> that signified the **sect of the Saracens** <note>Islam; 'sect' was a common medieval term for a religion</note> and the year of the **Hegira** <note>original: "albegerat," referring to the Hijra, the migration of Muhammad to Medina in 622 AD</note> in which **Muhammad** first began to preach, which was the first of the years of the Arabs. This was 52 solar years and 57 days. The **ascendant** <term>Ascendant: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> of the year of that aforementioned conjunction was the sign of **Gemini**, and the profection of that same year reached **Virgo**. Between that first year of the Arabs and the first year of **Yazdegerd** <note>Yazdegerd III, the last Sasanian King of Persia; his era began in 632 AD</note>, King of the Persians, there were 3,624 completed days. Therefore, when you wish to have knowledge of this matter, take the years of Yazdegerd and convert them into days, just as has already been explained in the **Zij** <note>original: "azig," from the Arabic 'Zij,' meaning an astronomical handbook or table</note>, that is, in the book of the courses of the planets. Add to these the days which are between the first year of the Arabs and the era of Yazdegerd, and divide this by 365 days and a quarter part of a day [365.25]. However many divisions result, that many will be solar years; and what remains in months and days will be from the incomplete year. When these are thus collected from the years, they are the solar years from the beginning of the years of the Arabs. Therefore, cast away one sign for every year, beginning from Virgo, and to whatever sign the number leads you, that will be the sign to which the "year of the world" arrives from the rising of the conjunction of the aforementioned sect. ¶ Other authorities besides Al-Kindi thought that one should add to the completed years of Yazdegerd 61 years, 11 months, 12 days, and 16 hours from the **years of the unbelievers** <note>original: "annis perfidum." This likely refers to the Persian calendar years which did not use a leap day ("without the fourth part of a day")</note>, which are without a fraction—that is, without the quarter-day—and they would extend these years into days and convert the days into solar years as we said before, and they would begin to cast the signs from the beginning of **Libra**. But if you want the profection from the sign of the conjunction of the sect, let the casting be from **Scorpio**. If, however, you want the profection from the ascendant of the kingdom, subtract from the years of Yazdegerd those 317 "unbeliever" years and convert them into solar years as we said before, and begin casting from **Virgo**. ¶ Again, if you want the profection from the rising of the profection of the change of the conjunction from the **Water Triplicity** to the **Fire Triplicity** <note>The shifting of Jupiter-Saturn conjunctions between elements, a major historical timing tool</note>, subtract 176 years from the completed years of Yazdegerd and convert those that remain into solar years, and begin casting from **Leo**. At whatever sign the number arrives, in that same sign will be the profection of each of the beginnings we have mentioned.

### Concerning the direction of the significator.

And from this follows the **Atazir** <term>Atazir: From Arabic 'al-tasyir,' meaning 'direction' or 'prolongation.' A method of moving a planet forward at a rate of roughly one degree per year to predict life events</term>, that is, direction. This is so that you may direct some **significator** <term>Significator: A planet or point in a chart representing a specific area of life, like health or career</term> to a certain place among the signs, and you may know how many degrees of direction lie between them; you shall take one year for every one degree. Therefore, when you wish to know this, and that significator you wish to direct is in the **ascendant** [the First House] and you wish to direct it to any part of the circle, subtract the **ascensions** <note>The time or degrees it takes for a sign to rise over the horizon</note> of the degree in which the significator is from the ascensions of the degree to which you wish to direct it, using the ascensions of the specific region. What remains will be the degree of direction. But if the significator is in the degree of the **seventh house** [the descendant], you will subtract the ascensions of the **nadir** <note>original: "nadir," meaning the point exactly opposite</note>, that is, the opposite degree in which the significator is, from the ascensions of the nadir of that degree to which you wish to direct it in that region. If, however, the significator is in the **Midheaven** <note>The 10th house, the highest point</note> or in the **Angle of the Earth** <note>The 4th house, the lowest point</note>, you will subtract the ascensions of the significator's degree from the ascensions of that degree to which you wish to direct it using the ascensions of the **Right Circle** <note>Right Ascension, used for planets on the meridian</note>; and what remains will be the degree of direction. If that significator you wish to direct is in some place outside of the four **angles** <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>, you shall look at its distance from any of these angles which are...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating "directions," a method used to predict the timing of life events. The previous page ended mid-word; this page begins with the completion of "angle." It specifically describes how to calculate the distance of a planet (the significator) from the four main angles of the chart (Midheaven, Ascendant, etc.) to determine its "equated" position.</meta>

...angle of the **midheaven** <term>Midheaven: The highest point in the sky at the time of birth, also called the Medium Coeli</term> and the **angle of the earth** <term>Angle of the Earth: The lowest point in the sky, opposite the midheaven, also called the Imum Coeli</term>: that is, as you will observe the **significator** <term>Significator: The specific planet or point in a birth chart that represents a person or a specific area of their life</term>. If it is between the **ascendant** <term>Ascendant: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> and the midheaven: subtract the **ascensions** <note>original: "ascensiones"; here referring to Right Ascension, the distance measured along the celestial equator</note> of the degree of the midheaven by the **right circle** <note>original: "circulum directum"; the celestial equator or "Right Sphere"</note> from the ascensions of the degree of the significator. 

And if it is between the **seventh house** <note>The descendant, or western horizon</note> and the midheaven: subtract the ascensions of the degree of the significator from the ascensions of the degree of the midheaven by the right circle. Whatever remains from either of these places, you shall divide by the parts of the hours of that day for the degree in which the significator is found. What results will be the **hours of distance** <term>Hours of distance: A measurement of how far a planet is from a planetary "angle," used to calculate its movement over time</term> from the angle.

And if it is between the ascendant and the angle of the earth, subtract the ascensions of the degree of the significator by the right circle from the ascensions of the degree of the angle of the earth. Again, if the significator is between the angle of the earth and the seventh house, subtract the ascensions of the degree of the angle of the earth by the right circle from the ascensions of the degree of the significator. Whatever remains from either of these places, you shall divide by the hourly times of the day of the **nadir** <note>original: "nadir id est oppositi gradus"; the point directly opposite a given degree in the zodiac</note>—that is, of the opposite degree of the significator—and what results will be the hours of distance from the angle.

¶ Once you know the hours of distance from the angle and you wish to **direct** <term>Direct: To symbolically move a planet forward in a chart to predict future events</term> the significator to any place on the circle of signs, and the significator is in the middle of the eastern circle—which is from the midheaven down to the angle of the earth among those things which follow the ascendant—subtract the ascensions of that degree in which the significator is found from the ascensions of that degree to which you wish to direct it by the right circle; what remains will be the significator of the right circle; keep this. 

After this, subtract the ascensions of that degree in which the significator is found by the **ascensions of the region** <note>original: "ascensiones regionis"; Oblique Ascension, which accounts for the specific latitude of the observer</note> from the ascensions of that degree to which you wish to direct it. What remains will be the significator of the region. After this, look at the remainder that exists between the significator of the right circle and the significator of the region; you shall take a sixth part of it and multiply it by the hours of distance from the angle. What results will be the **equation** <term>Equation: A correction factor used to reconcile different mathematical coordinate systems</term>. 

If the significator of the right circle is less than the significator of the region, you shall add the equation to the significator of the right circle. But if the significator of the right circle is more, you shall subtract the equation from it; what remains will be the **degree of direction** <term>Degree of Direction: The final calculated position used to determine the timing of an event</term>.

¶ Likewise, if the significator you wish to direct is in the western half-circle—which is from the angle of the earth up to the midheaven from that which follows the seventh house—subtract the ascensions of the **nadir** <note>The opposite point</note> of the degree of the significator you wish to direct from the ascensions of the nadir of that degree to which you wish to direct it; what remains will be the significator of the right circle. 

After this, subtract the ascensions of the nadir of the degree of the significator by the ascensions of the region from the ascensions of the nadir of that degree to which you wish to direct it; what remains will be the significator of the region. Then take the remainder that exists between the significator of the right circle and the significator of the region; take a sixth part of it and multiply it by the hours of distance from the angle, and what results will be the equation. After this, look at the significator of the right circle: if it is less than the significator of the region, add the equation to the significator of the right circle; and if the significator of the right circle is more than the significator of the region, subtract the equation from the significator of the right circle; and what results from that will be the degree of direction.

¶ But if the significator is in one **quadrant** <note>One of the four quarters of the astrological chart</note> and the place to which you direct it is in another quadrant, you shall direct the significator to the angle that follows it...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating "directions," specifically how to determine where a planet or point (the significator) will move in the zodiac over a set period of years. It follows from the previous page's discussion on measuring distances from the primary angles of the chart.</meta>

...measure, I have shown you the **direction** <term>Direction: A predictive technique where a planet is "moved" forward in the chart—usually at a rate of one degree per year of life—to see when it encounters other planets.</term> from the angle, and you shall join both directions together. 

When you have identified the **significator** <term>Significator: The specific planet or point in a birth chart that represents a person or a specific area of their life, such as the Sun for honors or the Moon for the body.</term> and you wish to know where that direction will reach on the **circle of signs** <note>original: "circulo signorum"; the Zodiac</note> in any given year:

If the significator is in the **ascendant** <term>Ascendant: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</term>: add the number of years to the **ascensions** <note>original: "ascensiones"; the measurement of time it takes for a sign to rise over the horizon.</note> of the degree of the ascendant, and make that the arc in the **ascension of the region** <note>Oblique ascension, which is adjusted for the specific latitude of the birthplace.</note>. Wherever that arc of degrees ends in the circle of signs, that is where the direction will reach in that year.

If, however, the significator is in the **seventh house** <note>The Descendant, or western horizon.</note>: add the number of years to the ascensions of the **nadir** <term>Nadir: The point directly opposite a specific degree in the zodiac (180 degrees away).</term> of the degree of the significator, and calculate its arc also in the ascensions of the region. Wherever it ends in the circle of signs, the direction will reach its nadir in that same year.

But if the significator is in the **tenth** <note>The Midheaven or Medium Coeli.</note> or **fourth house** <note>The Angle of the Earth or Imum Coeli.</note>: add the number of years to the ascensions of the degree of the significator in the **direct circle** <note>Right Ascension, measured along the celestial equator rather than the horizon.</note>, and calculate its arc in the ascensions of the direct circle. Wherever it ends in the circle of signs, the direction will reach that point in the same year.

If the significator is outside these four places which are the angles, you should find the **hours of distance** from the angle as I said before. If the significator is in the eastern half: add the number of years you desire to the ascensions of the degree of the significator through the direct circle, and calculate this in the ascensions of the direct circle; what results will be the significator of the direct circle—keep this value. After this, add the number of years also to the ascensions of the degree of the significator in the region, and calculate this in the ascension of the region; what results will be the significator of the region. 

After this, take a sixth part of the difference between the significator of the direct circle and the significator of the region, and multiply it by the hours of distance from the angle; the result will be the **equation** <term>Equation: A mathematical correction or adjustment used to find a precise astronomical position.</term>. 

If the significator of the direct circle is less than the significator of the region, add the equation to the significator of the direct circle; if it is more, subtract it. What remains is where the direction will reach in the circle of signs for that year. 

If the significator is in the western half, perform the same operation with the nadir of the degree of the significator as you did with the degree of the significator in the work preceding this. Where it ends is where the direction will reach in the circle of signs in that year. The place where the direction reaches is called the **place of division** and the **lord of the term** <term>Lord of the Term: In ancient astrology, each zodiac sign is divided into five unequal "terms" or "bounds," each ruled by a specific planet.</term> of that place is called the **divisor**. If another planet is at the degree of division, or its rays (aspects) are near it, that planet or the lord of those rays is called the partner of the divisor in that division.

The significators which are directed are found in five places: 
1. The degree of the **ascendant**
2. The degree of the **Sun**
3. The degree of the **Moon**
4. The degree of the **Part of Fortune** <term>Part of Fortune: A mathematical point in a chart calculated from the positions of the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant, representing well-being and success.</term>
5. The degree of the **midheaven** 

The degree of the ascendant is directed to discern accidents that happen to the body. The degree of the Sun is for matters of dignity, advancement, honor, and authority. The Moon is for the state of the soul, the body, and marriage. The Part of Fortune is for acquisitions, profit, poverty, or abundance. The midheaven is for one’s profession, mastery, and other specific circumstances of life. 

If it is the direction of the **revolution** <note>A chart cast for the "birth" of a year, often when the Sun enters Aries.</note> of any year of the world or of births, its significators are directed according to this method: specifically, one day for every one degree. Some believe that the significators of the year should be directed for everyone in this way: namely, 59 minutes and 8 seconds for one day. For the direction of kings and princes is directed from the degree of the midheaven through the ascensions of the direct circle at 56 minutes and 8 seconds for one day, until it reaches...

...reaches to good or bad planets. The well-being of kings is directed—whether in health or illness—from the degree of the **ascendant** <term>Ascendant: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</term> to all good or bad planets, at a rate of 59 minutes and 8 seconds <note>The mean daily motion of the Sun, often used as a timing measure in astrology.</note> for one day through the **ascensions of the region** <note>original: "ascensiones regionis"; measurements of time adjusted for the local latitude.</note>. This direction is used in the **revolution of the years of the world** <term>Revolution of the world: A mundane astrological chart cast for the moment the Sun enters Aries, used to predict the year's events for nations.</term>. 

The fate of the common people or peasants is to be foreknown from the degree of the ascendant; similarly, the fate of the king is foreknown from the degree of the **midheaven** <note>original: "medij celi"; the highest point in the chart, representing status and authority.</note>. In the revolution of the years of births <note>The Solar Return chart.</note>, the fate of the person born is to be foreknown from the degree of the ascendant of the revolution, using 59 minutes and 8 seconds for one day through the ascensions of the region.

## Concerning the *Algebugthar*

From this follows the **algebugthar** <term>Algebugthar: A term derived from the Persian word for "distribution," referring to a method of dividing the years of a person's life among the various planets.</term> in births, which is the direction of the degree of the ascendant. This is done by looking at the degree of the ascendant and seeing which planet's **term** <term>Term: Also known as "bounds," these are specific degrees within a sign ruled by a particular planet.</term> it occupies. Subtract the ascensions of the ascendant from the ascensions found at the end of that planet's term, using the ascensions of the region. For whatever remains, assign one year to each degree, one month for every five minutes, and six days for every minute. The Lord of that term will be the disposer of those years, who is called the **divider** <term>Divider: The planet ruling the specific bound or term currently governing a period of the person's life.</term>. 

After this, take the degrees of the term that follows it and convert those also into degrees of ascension. For whatever results, assign one year to each degree, one month for every five minutes, and six days for every minute. The Lord of that term will then be the divider and disposer of those years. Similarly, you shall proceed through every term of the Lord in succession until the end of life. If a planet or its rays are found within the term of any planet, they will participate in the division; and this division is called *algebugthar*.

## Concerning the **Twelfth-Parts** of the Planets and Houses

From this come the **twelfth-parts** <term>Twelfth-parts: Also called Dodecatemoria; a technique where each 30-degree zodiac sign is divided into twelve 2.5-degree segments.</term> of the planets or houses. To find this, observe how far a planet has traveled in its sign, or look at the degrees and minutes of the house you wish to calculate. Multiply this by 12. After this, add the same degrees and minutes that you multiplied by 12 to the total. Cast away the resulting sum from the beginning of the degrees of that same sign, giving 30 degrees to each sign. Where the number ends, there will be the twelfth-part of the planets and houses.

## Concerning the **Ninth-Parts** of the Same

From this comes the **amanbabarat** <term>Amanbabarat: A corruption of the Sanskrit term "Navamsha," a division of a sign into nine equal parts common in Indian and early Persian astrology.</term>, which are the **ninth-parts**. The knowledge of this is to know how far a planet has traveled in its sign in degrees and minutes, or the degree of the house for which you wish to know the ninth-part. 

After this, divide the sign into nine divisions, and each division consists of three degrees and one-third of a degree <note>3 degrees and 20 minutes.</note>. Then, observe into which of the ninth-parts the degree of the planet or house falls. After this, give the first ninth-part of the signs to the lord of the **movable sign** <term>Movable sign: Also called Cardinal signs (Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn), which mark the start of the seasons.</term> of that same **triplicity** <term>Triplicity: A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element: Fire, Earth, Air, or Water.</term>, and the second ninth-part to the lord of the sign which [follows]...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for divisional astrology, specifically explaining how to calculate sub-divisions of the zodiac signs (ninth-parts and decans) and how these determine the ruling planets for specific years of a person's life.</meta>

...which continues until you come to whichever **ninth part** <term>Ninth part: Also known as a "navamsha," this is a 3°20' division of a zodiac sign used to add detail to a prediction.</term> the degree occupies; the planet ruling that ninth part will be the ruler of that division. 

For example: if the sign containing the degree belongs to the **triplicity** <term>Triplicity: A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water).</term> of **Aries** <note>The Fire triplicity: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius.</note>, then its first ninth part belongs to **Mars** (the ruler of Aries), the second ninth part belongs to **Venus** (the ruler of Taurus), and the third ninth part belongs to **Mercury** (the ruler of Gemini). This continues in the same way through all the signs until the ninth ninth part. 

If the sign belongs to the triplicity of **Cancer** <note>The Water triplicity: Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.</note>, the first ninth part belongs to the **Moon** (ruler of Cancer), the second to the **Sun** (ruler of Leo), and the third to **Mercury** (ruler of Virgo). 

If the sign belongs to the triplicity of **Libra** <note>The Air triplicity: Libra, Aquarius, and Gemini.</note>, the first ninth part belongs to **Venus** (ruler of Libra), the second to **Mars** (ruler of Scorpio), and the third to **Jupiter** (ruler of Sagittarius). 

Similarly, in the triplicity of **Capricorn** <note>The Earth triplicity: Capricorn, Taurus, and Virgo.</note>, the first ninth part belongs to **Saturn** (ruler of Capricorn), the second also to **Saturn** (ruler of Aquarius), and the third to **Jupiter** (ruler of Pisces). The rest follow according to the natural succession of the signs. 

For example: if a planet or one of the twelve **houses** was at 19 degrees of **Aquarius**, and you divide the sign into nine parts, the 19th degree falls into the sixth division. Since Aquarius is in the triplicity of Libra, you start the count from Libra: 
1. Venus (ruler of Libra)
2. Mars (ruler of Scorpio)
3. Jupiter (ruler of Sagittarius)
4. Saturn (ruler of Capricorn)
5. Saturn (ruler of Aquarius)
6. Jupiter (ruler of Pisces)
Thus, the **nauhabar** <note>original: "nauhabar"; a medieval Latin corruption of the Persian/Sanskrit term for a ninth-part division.</note> (that is, the ninth-part ruler) of 19 degrees Aquarius is **Jupiter**.

### Method for finding the ruler of the decan.

From this comes the **dozogen** <note>original: "dozogen" or "dorongen"; likely a corruption of a Greek or Persian term for "decans" or "thirds."</note>, which is found by dividing the **ascendant** into three parts, each division being 10 degrees. You give the first division to the lord of the ascendant sign itself; the second to the lord of the fifth sign from it; and the third to the lord of the ninth sign. This is because the 1st, 5th, and 9th signs always belong to the same triplicity. 

For example: from the beginning of **Aries** up to its 10th degree, the **dorongen** (that is, the **decan** <term>Decan: A 10-degree division of a sign. Each sign has three decans.</term>) belongs to **Mars**. From the 10th degree to the 20th degree, its decan ruler is the **Sun** (the ruler of Leo, the 5th sign). From the 20th degree to the end of the sign, the decan ruler is **Jupiter** (the ruler of Sagittarius, the 9th sign).

Through this, the "lord of the circle" in births is determined. This is done by looking at the lord of the hour in which the person was born. You assign the ascendant and that lord of the hour to the first year of the person's life. The condition of that lord of the hour—whether it is well-placed or afflicted—will signify the health or illness of the person’s body, just as the ruler of the natal ascendant does. 

Then, you assign the lord of the second hour (counting from the birth hour) to the second house (counting from the ascendant) for the second year of life. Its condition will signify the person's state in that year regarding their substance and wealth, just as the ruler of the house of substance does. You assign the lord of the third hour to the third house for the third year, signifying the state of brothers, sisters, and so on. You give the lord of the fourth hour to the fourth house for the fourth year, signifying the state of the parents and those things represented by the fourth house. You shall do the same for the lords of the hours in succession, assigning each hour's ruler to a house...

<summary>This page explains how to calculate the rulers of zodiac sub-divisions—specifically the ninth-parts (navamshas) and decans—and how to use the lords of the birth hours to predict a person's health and fortune year-by-year.</summary>
<keywords>ninth part, navamsha, decan, triplicity, ascendant, planetary rulers, Aquarius, Aries, yearly predictions, medical astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for divisional astrology, moving from the subdivision of signs (ninth parts) to the lords of the houses and the calculation of planetary periods known as Firdaria, as well as the astronomical concept of a planet's "super-eminence" or Almanar.</meta>

...in the radical houses <term>Radical houses: The twelve divisions of the sky as they appeared at the exact moment of birth (the "root" or radix).</term> and it will be for the lord of the 12th hour. From the hour of birth, the 12th house is measured from the natal ascendant, and the 12th year likewise. And the lord of the 13th hour from the natal ascendant <note>original: "natiuitate radicis ascēdentis"</note> and the 13th year, and the lord of the 14th hour from the house of substance <note>The 2nd House, which governs wealth and possessions.</note> and the 14th year. And the lord of the first hour is named the lord of the ascending hour; and the lord of the 2nd hour is the lord of the hour of the house of substance; and the lord of the 3rd hour is the lord of the hour of the house of brothers <note>The 3rd House, governing siblings and short journeys.</note>; and so on for the others. Similarly, the lord of each of these hours <note>original: "haraz horarū"</note> is named after the house; and he is also called the lord of the orbit <note>original: "orbis dominus"</note> and he signifies throughout every year just as is signified by the **Alcocoden** <term>Alcocoden: The planet that "gives years," used in medieval astrology to predict a person's life expectancy.</term>: that is, by the lord of the year. And some astrologers place the lord of the orbit—that is, of the natal ascendant—in the first year; and in the 2nd year, the planet which succeeds this one; and in the third year, the planet which succeeds that one, just as was said regarding the lord of the hour.

From this comes the arrangement of the **Alfridaria** <term>Alfridaria: Also known as Firdaria, this is a Persian system where different planets take turns ruling over specific periods of a person's life.</term>: that is, when a birth is by day, the **Sun** will preside over the arrangement of his Firdaria at the beginning of life according to the number of years of its Firdaria, which are ten. After this, the planet which succeeds the Sun, which is **Venus**, whose Firdaria years are eight. And after Venus, the planet which succeeds it, which is **Mercury**; and his years are thirteen. Then the **Moon**; and her Firdaria years are nine. Then **Saturn**, and his Firdaria years are 11. Then **Jupiter**, and his Firdaria years are 12. Then **Mars**, and his Firdaria years are 7. Then the **Head of the Dragon** <term>Head of the Dragon: The North Node of the Moon, considered a point of growth or increase.</term> and his Firdaria years are 3. After this, the **Tail** <term>Tail of the Dragon: The South Node of the Moon, often associated with release or decrease.</term>; and its Firdaria years are two. These years, collected together, make 75 years. After this, the arrangement returns to the Sun, and similarly through to the last of the planets. If, however, the birth is by night, the arrangement begins from the **Moon**, and she will manage her Firdaria years, which are nine; similarly planet after planet as we said before regarding the Sun. And when a planet has managed the years of its Firdaria, it properly manages the first seventh part alone—that is, a seventh part of its Firdaria. Then, in the second seventh, the planet which succeeds it participates with it; after this, the third planet which succeeds the second participates in the third seventh; and so on until the planet which was before it participates in the final seventh of its Firdaria years. And each one has a judgment when it participates with another over the birth.

From this comes the **Almanar** <term>Almanar: Likely from the Arabic for "lighthouse" or "signpost," referring here to a planet's "super-eminence" or its position in its orbital cycle.</term>, that is, the super-eminence by which it is said that a planet goes "over" a planet. This is so that you may look at the mean motion of a planet and its equated <note>The "equated" or "true" position is the planet's actual location in the sky, as opposed to its average or "mean" position.</note> place; and if its equated place is less than its mean motion, it will be ascending from the middle of its circle up to the summit of the circle. And if its equated place is more than its mean motion, it will be descending from the middle of its circle down to its lowest point. And if its equated place is equal to its mean motion, it will be in the middle of its circle. Then you shall subtract the place of the planet from its mean motion after you have determined this most certainly—that is, the equated place—or conversely, subtracting the smaller from the larger of them, and you shall multiply what remains by seven and divide by 22. And whatever comes out of the division will be for every planet according to the quantity of its ascension or descension. Concerning **Venus** and **Mercury**, we take what is between both places—that is, what is between the places...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for divisional astrology, transitioning from the calculation of planetary periods (Alfridaria) to the specific astronomical conditions of the Sun, Venus, and Mercury, and the "burnt" hours of Albuzic.</meta>

with the Sun, Venus, and Mercury, we act as described above: because through this we know which
of them is stronger in the circle of the apogee <note>original: "augis" — the point in a planet's orbit where it is farthest from the Earth.</note>: but when either Venus or Mercury
is oriental <note>Rising in the east before the Sun.</note> and its adjusted position is less than the position of the Sun, you shall take the 
remainder which is between the position of the Sun and its own position: and you shall do with it just as 
you did, as was said, regarding the higher planets through multiplication and division. ¶ And 
the significance of the planets is nearer and stronger when they pass over one another,
that is, when one passes over another in conjunction. ¶ However, in opposition and in 
the square aspect <note>original: "quarto aspectu" — an astrological angle of 90 degrees, usually considered tense or challenging.</note> their significance will be less apparent and weaker. ¶ And yet,
when one of them is ascending and the other descending, then the ascending one 
goes above the descending one. ¶ But when both are descending: 
the one which is of the lesser descent goes above the one which is of the greater or 
more extensive descent. ¶ If, however, both are ascending, the one which is of the greater 
ascension goes above the one which is of the lesser ascension. ¶ It is also said 
in another way, that is, so that you may know the latitude of both planets: because the 
northern one among them goes above the southern <note>original: "meridianum"</note> one. And if both are northern, 
the one which is of greater latitude goes above the one which is of lesser 
latitude. ¶ But if both are southern, the one which is of lesser latitude 
goes above the one which is of greater latitude; and for this reason, it is said that the Moon goes 
above Jupiter in these two operations. Similarly, if either of the two 
planets is in the middle of the circle having no latitude, then the ascending northern one 
goes above the one that lacks latitude, and the one without latitude itself goes 
above the descending southern one. And from this, the "Opening of the Gates" <term>Opening of the Gates: A specific planetary configuration believed to cause significant changes in the weather or to open spiritual pathways.</term> is said to occur 
when a lower planet is joined to a higher planet and, along with this, their 
houses are opposite.

**E** <note>The initial 'E' begins the section on Albuzic.</note> From this comes **Albuzic** <term>Albuzic: A term derived from Arabic astrology referring to specific time-intervals or the 'emergence' of influence after a lunar conjunction.</term> and it is a matter that the Indians <note>The author likely refers to the Indian astrological traditions that reached the West through Arabic translations.</note> 
observed very strictly. For they 
count 12 hours after the conjunction of the Sun and Moon and apply them 
to the Sun and divide the 12 hours into threes, that is, into three parts, namely in the **Hayz** <term>Hayz: An astrological condition where a planet is in a "preferred" or dignified state based on its gender, the time of day, and its position relative to the horizon.</term> of the Sun. And 
they judge according to all four hours according to the judgment of the lords 
of the triplicity <term>Triplicity: A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water).</term> of the Sun at the hour of conjunction: then they give to Venus after 12 
hours and they divide them again into threes and they judge according to every trinal division 
following the lords of the triplicity of Venus at the hour of conjunction: after this they do 
similarly with Mercury and the other planets by their succession until the 
orbit returns to the Sun after 84 hours; and they do not cease doing this frequently 
until the second conjunction comes. And some say that Albuzic is such that after the 
conjunction there are 12 unequal hours which are called "burnt" <term>Combust: An astrological term for a planet being so close to the Sun that its influence is "burned up" or weakened.</term>: and it is 
not fitting for anyone to begin any work in these: and after these 12 hours, there are 72 unburnt 
hours in which the beginning of works is useful; and after these 72 unburnt hours, 
there are similarly 12 burnt hours until the conjunction which follows. After this, they divide 
these 12 hours into three divisions, that is, into parts. And they said that when anyone 
begins to travel or to wage war in the first four hours, the loss 
of his soul is to be feared; and in the second four hours, the harm to 
his body without the loss of his soul is to be feared; and in the last four hours, the harm 
to his substance and his people and all that he possesses is to be feared.

# The Fifth Distinction: On the Enumeration of All Parts.

<image-desc>Ornate woodcut initial 'E' featuring interlaced strapwork and decorative foliage within a square frame.</image-desc>And because we have completed the explanations of those things which follow from the names used by the astrologers, let us now carry out the enumeration of all the **parts** <note>original: "partium" — these are the "Arabic Parts" or "Lots," calculated points in a birth chart based on the distance between two planets added to the Ascendant.</note>. And when we have noted the location of the parts, take the distance from one place to another and project this from the **Ascendant** <note>The degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note> or from another place: we say that you should add the degrees of the Ascendant to the distance found between both places, and project it from the Ascendant or from the beginning of the sign from which you are projecting. An example of this is the calculation for the **Part of Fortune** <note>original: "partis fortune" — a point representing prosperity and worldly success.</note>, because you take whatever distance in degrees lies between the Sun and the Moon and add those to the degrees of the sign of the Ascendant. Whatever the total is, you project it in 30-degree increments, beginning the count from the start of the sign of the Ascendant; where the number ends, there is the Part of Fortune. At night, it is the opposite: take the distance from the Moon to the Sun, add the degrees of the Ascendant, and project this by 30-degree increments, starting from the beginning of the sign of the Ascendant. ¶ Here, therefore, let us begin the parts of the twelve houses following the Part of Fortune, and let us begin with the Ascendant.

<image-desc>Woodcut initial 'P' with a white letter on a dark background decorated with floral patterns.</image-desc>The **First House** contains the **Part of Life** <note>original: "pars vite"</note>; in the day, this is calculated from Jupiter to Saturn, and in the night, the opposite, and it is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of the Hyleg** <note>original: "Pars hyles" — from the Persian/Arabic term for the "giver of life" or "physical body."</note> is taken from the degree of the conjunction or prevention <note>The New Moon or Full Moon immediately preceding the birth.</note> which occurred before the birth, measured to the degree of the Moon, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Future Things**—that is, the **Part of the Sun** <note>Also known as the Part of Spirit, representing the soul and intellect.</note>—is taken in the day from the Moon to the Sun, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Love and Concord**—that is, the **Part of Venus**—is taken in the day from the Part of Fortune to the Part of Future Things, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Stability and Trust** of the Ascendant's duration for the newborn is the same as the Part of Concord and Love. ¶ The **Part of Courage and Boldness**—that is, the **Part of Mars**—is taken in the day from Mars to the degree of the Part of Fortune, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant.

<image-desc>Woodcut initial 'S' with white letter and floral ornament on a dark background.</image-desc>The **Second House** is the **Part of Substance** <note>original: "pars substātie" — relating to wealth and possessions.</note>; it is taken in both day and night from the Lord of the House of Substance <note>The planet that rules the sign on the cusp of the second house.</note> to the exact degree and minute of the House of Substance, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Poverty and Smallness of Wit**—that is, the **Part of Mercury**—is taken in the day from the Part of Future Things to the Part of Fortune, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Happiness, Triumph, and Victory**—that is, the **Part of Jupiter**—is taken in the day from the Part of Future Things to Jupiter, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant.

<image-desc>Woodcut initial 'T' with white letter and floral ornament on a dark background.</image-desc>The **Third House** is the **Part of Brothers** <note>original: "pars fratrū"</note>; it is taken in the day from Saturn to Jupiter, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of the Goodwill of Brothers** is taken in the day from the Sun to Saturn, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant. If, however, Saturn should be "under the rays" <note>original: "sub radijs" — meaning the planet is so close to the Sun that its influence is hidden or weakened.</note>, it is taken in the day from the Sun to Jupiter, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant.

<summary>This page provides technical instructions for calculating the "Arabic Parts" or "Lots," which are mathematical points in a horoscope representing specific life themes like wealth, life, and courage. It defines how to calculate these points by measuring distances between planets and projecting them from the Ascendant for the first three astrological houses.</summary>

The **Fourth House** <note>In astrology, the Fourth House represents one's home, roots, and family, particularly the father.</note> is the **Part of Fathers**. In the day, it is taken from the Sun to Saturn; in the night, the reverse; and it is projected from the **Ascendant** <note>The degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>. ¶ The **Part of the Death of the Father** is taken in the day from Saturn to Jupiter, and in the night the reverse; and it is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Years** <note>Likely referring to the length of life or significant cycles.</note> is taken in the day from the **Lord of the House of the Sun** <note>The planet that rules the zodiac sign where the Sun is located.</note> to Saturn, and in the night the reverse; and it is projected from the Ascendant. And if the Sun is in its own house, or in any of the houses of Saturn, take it in the day from the Sun to Saturn, and in the night the reverse; project it from the Ascendant and do not consider whether Saturn is visible or **under the rays** <note>original: "sub radijs" — a planet is "under the rays" when it is so close to the Sun that it is hidden by its light.</note>. ¶ The **Part of Inheritances and Possessions** is taken in the day and night from Saturn to the Moon, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of the Nobility of the Person Born**—and of that concerning which there is doubt as to whether he is the son of the father he is attributed to or of another—is taken in the day from the degree of the Sun to the degree of its **exaltation** <note>A specific sign where a planet’s influence is traditionally considered to be at its strongest.</note>, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ But if the Sun is in the degree of its exaltation during the day, or the Moon is in the degree of its exaltation at night, the Part will be in the degree of the Ascendant, and the meaning will be that of the degree of the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of the End of Things** <note>Refers to the final outcome or completion of a project or life stage.</note> is taken in the day and night from Saturn up to the **Lord of the House of Conjunction** <note>The planet ruling the sign where the last New Moon occurred before birth.</note> or of **prevention** <note>original: "peruentionis" — likely referring to the preceding Full Moon (syzygy).</note>, and one adds the degrees of the Ascendant on top of this and projects it from the Ascendant.

The **Fifth House** <note>Traditionally the house of children, pleasure, and creativity.</note> is the **Part of Children**; it is taken in the day from Jupiter to Saturn, and by night the reverse, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part which signifies the time in which a child should be made** <note>i.e., conceived or born</note>, and the number of them—whether male or female—and what the woman will give birth to, is taken in the day and night from Mars to Jupiter, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Knowledge of Male and Female Children** is taken in the day and night from the Moon to Jupiter, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part through which the child is known** (concerning whom an inquiry is made as to whether it is male or female) is taken in the day from the Lord of the House of the Moon to the Moon, and in the night the reverse; it is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part signifying a child when Jupiter reaches it**—that is, the degree of the Part signifying a child—is taken in the day and night from Mars to Jupiter and projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Love and Joy** is taken in the day and night from Venus to Saturn, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Knowledge of the Existence of Children** is taken in the day and night from the Moon to Venus and is projected from the Ascendant.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'S' with a serpent-like form twisted into the shape of the letter.</image-desc>
The **Sixth House** <note>Traditionally the house of illness, labor, and service.</note> is the **Part of Infirmity** and **azemena** <note>original: "azemena" — a term from Arabic astrology referring to certain degrees of the zodiac that signify permanent physical defects or "inseparable weakness" of the limbs.</note>, that is, of the inseparable weakness of any of the limbs; it is taken in the day from Saturn to Mars, in the night the reverse, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Servants** is taken in the day from Mercury to the Moon, and in the night the reverse, and is projected from the Ascendant. And **Alédezgod** <note>Likely Al-Andarzaghar, a 7th-century Persian astrologer influential in the transmission of these techniques.</note> said it is taken in the day from Mercury to the Part of Fortune, and in the night the reverse. And he said: it is necessary that we use both parts together.

The **Seventh House** <note>The house of marriage, partnerships, and open enemies.</note> is the **Part of the Betrothal of Men or Marriage**; and it is taken in the day and night from the Sun to Venus, and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ And this part is similar to the Part of Commands and Rumors. And the **Part of the Betrothal of Women or Marriage** is taken from **Hermes** <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of astrology.</note> in the day and night from Venus to Saturn, and is projected from the Ascendant. And this part corresponds to the Part of the Cultivation of the Earth. ¶ Likewise, the Part of the Betrothal of Women according to **Velium** <note>Likely Vettius Valens, a famous 2nd-century Greek astrologer whose works were heavily cited in the medieval period.</note>...

The **Ninth House** is the **Part of Pilgrimages** <note>representing long-distance travel, religion, and philosophy</note>. It is taken by day and night from the Lord of the Ninth House to the degree and minute of the Ninth House: and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Travel by Water** is taken by day from Saturn to the 15th degree of the sign of Cancer, and in the night the reverse, etc. But if Saturn should be at the 15th degree of Cancer, the degree of the Part will be the degree of the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Religion** is taken by day from the Moon to Mercury, and in the night the reverse: and is projected from the Ascendant.

The **Tenth House** is the **Part of Rule and Kingdom**—and what work the person born might do—and it is taken by day and night from Saturn to the Moon and is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of the King and Kingdom** is taken by day from Mars to the Moon: in the night the reverse: and is projected from the Ascendant. The **Eleventh House** is the **Part of Friends** and is taken by day and night from the Moon to Mercury: and is projected from the Ascendant. The **Twelfth House** is the **Part of Enemies** according to **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary sage to whom many foundational astrological texts were attributed.</note> and is taken by day and night from the Lord of the House of Enemies to the degree and minute of the House of Enemies: and it is projected from the Ascendant. And according to certain of the ancients, it is taken by day and night from Saturn to Mars, etc. And these are the Parts of the twelve houses, etc. ¶ These are other Parts which the masters of the judgments of the stars use greatly. Among these is the **Part which signifies the Falsehood of Rumors**; when it falls in a **tortuous sign** <note>Signs that rise slowly or at an angle, often associated with difficulty.</note> or a **moveable sign** <note>Signs like Aries or Cancer that mark the change of seasons.</note>, or if the Lord of the House is **retrograde** <note>Appearing to move backward through the zodiac.</note> or hindered, or if the "malefics" <note>Planets like Mars or Saturn, thought to bring challenges.</note> aspect it or are joined to it. And it signifies the **Truth of Rumors** if it falls with the "fortunes" <note>Beneficial planets like Jupiter or Venus.</note> or in their houses or terms, or if they aspect it, or if it is in a **direct sign**: and it is taken by day from Mercury...

It also introduces specialized Lots for determining the truth or falsehood of rumors based on the sign and planetary aspects.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the list of the "Arabic Parts" or "Lots," moving from personal psychological traits and war to the broader field of Mundane Astrology—the study of world events, the rise and fall of kings, and the duration of empires. It builds on the previous page's focus on calculating mathematical points in the zodiac based on planetary distances.</meta>

...to Venus: at night the reverse, and it is projected, etc. And according to **Abu Ma'shar** <note>original: "albumasar," the influential 9th-century Persian astrologer whose work was a cornerstone of Latin medieval astrology.</note>, by day from Mercury to the Moon, and at night the reverse, etc. ¶ The **Part of Reason and Depth of Sense** is taken by day from Saturn to the Moon, and at night the reverse, etc. ¶ The **Part of Wisdom and Discipline** is taken by day and night from Saturn to Jupiter, and it is projected, etc. ¶ The **Part of War and Battles** is taken by day and night from Saturn to the Moon: and it is projected from the **Ascendant** <note>The degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment.</note>. ¶ The **Part of Peace and Concord of Armies** is taken by day and night from the Moon to Mercury, and it is projected from the Ascendant. ¶ The **Part of Aspecting in the Solar Return** <note>original: "reuolutione anni" — the "revolution of the year" or Solar Return chart, cast for the moment the Sun returns to its exact position at the start of the year.</note> is taken by day from the Moon to Venus, and at night the reverse, and it is projected from the Sun.

->Concerning the Parts of Revolutions in the Affairs of Kingdoms.<-

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'E' featuring floral and foliate motifs.</image-desc>And because, with God’s help, we have introduced these Parts that pertain to particular matters, we shall now follow with the Parts that pertain to the **Revolution of the years of the world** <note>The chart cast for the moment the Sun enters Aries, used to predict world events for the coming year.</note> and the **conjunctions** <note>Specifically the "Great Conjunctions" of Saturn and Jupiter, which were believed to signal major historical shifts.</note> signifying the causes of a kingdom and its stability or duration. Among these is the Part called the **Part of Kingship or Empire**, which is utilized in the world revolution: it is taken from Mars to the Moon and is projected from the Ascendant of the conjunction that signifies a change in the kingdom. It is also taken in another way: namely, from the degree of the Ascendant of the conjunction to the degree of the conjunction itself, and it is projected from the degree of the Ascendant of the revolution. Likewise, it is taken in another way: from the degree of the **Midheaven** <note>original: "medij celi" — the highest point in the sky at a given time, representing authority and career.</note> of the revolution to the Sun, and let it be projected from Jupiter (that is, from the degree where Jupiter is located). ¶ The **Part of the King's Time**—that is, how long it ought to endure—is taken at the hour of the **election of the king** <note>original: "electionis" — the practice of choosing an astrologically auspicious moment to begin a reign or task.</note>, from the Sun to the 15th degree of the sign of Leo, and it is projected from the Moon; then it is taken from the Moon to the 15th degree of the sign of Cancer and projected from the Sun. Another Part concerning the time of the king's election is taken at the hour of the king's election: by day from Jupiter to Saturn, and by night the reverse, and it is projected from the Ascendant of the revolution of the year in which the king rose to power. If Jupiter was in a **mutable sign** <note>original: "signo cōi" (common sign), such as Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces, known for changeability.</note> and it was a daytime revolution, and if Jupiter was **cadent from the angles** <note>Falling away from the four most powerful houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th), indicating weakness.</note>, then it is taken from Saturn to Jupiter: and 30 degrees are added thereto, and it is projected from the Ascendant. If indeed Saturn and Jupiter were opposite each other and were both cadent from the Ascendant, let that which falls between them be halved—that is, take the midpoint of the degrees that are between them—and it is projected from the Ascendant. And if Jupiter was in his **exaltation** <note>The sign where a planet's influence is most harmoniously strengthened; for Jupiter, this is Cancer.</note> and the revolution was at night, it is counted from him to Saturn and projected from the Ascendant.

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'D' featuring floral and foliate motifs.</image-desc>There are two main Parts from which the time of a king's election and his duration are extracted. ¶ The first of these is that you should look at the hour of the king's election [to see] where the **profection** <note>An ancient timing technique where a point moves forward at a set rate, usually 30 degrees per year.</note> of the year has arrived from the **conjunction of the triplicity** <note>The Great Conjunction of Jupiter and Saturn, which changes element or "triplicity" every 200 years, supposedly marking the rise of new religious or political eras.</note> that signified the **sect** <note>original: "sectam" — a religious or political movement.</note>, according to the number by which one year is given for every 30 degrees, and one month for every two and a half degrees. And when you know in which degree or sign this is, keep it: because this is the place from which you will calculate the first Part. And when you wish to adjust it, you will adjust it by the Ascendant of the revolution of the year in which the chosen one himself rose to power. After this, look from whichever planet—Saturn or Jupiter—is **oriental** <note>Rising before the Sun in the morning sky.</note> to the Sun in that same year, up to the degree of the calculation of the first Part that you kept, and project it from the Ascendant of the revolution of the year; and the place where that number arrives is the location of the first Part. ¶ The second Part is found thus: take the conjunction during which the king rose or began his reign, [observing] to which sign or degree the profection of the year has arrived (at the rate of 30 degrees per year and two and a half degrees per month) up to the day on which the king rose; and this shall be the equal place...

<meta>This page continues the instructions for calculating specific "Arabic Parts" (mathematical points in a horoscope), shifting from the personal power of a king to Mundane Astrology—specifically the prediction of market prices, crop yields, and commodity values for the coming year.</meta>

...of the calculation of the second part: preserve it also. After this, look at the planet setting from the Sun, from Saturn and Jupiter, to the place of the equation of the second part which you preserved. Then **project** <note>In astrology, "projecting" a part means measuring a specific distance between two points and then applying that same distance starting from the Ascendant.</note> it from the **Ascendant of the revolution** <note>The "revolution" refers to the Solar Return chart, cast for the moment the Sun enters 0° of Aries, marking the astrological New Year.</note>; and where it reaches will be the location of the second part. These are the parts which signify the strength of the king and the duration of his reign.

Likewise, there are other parts by which astrologers are assisted in the revolutions of the years of the world. Through them, it is known what will be "heavy" in the affairs of the market—meaning what will be expensive—and what will be "light" in price; what will be precious or cheap, and whether there will be much or little of it. To find this, you must look at where the **Part** <note>The "Arabic Part" or "Lot" assigned to a specific commodity, like wheat or honey.</note> falls: that is, in which planet’s house, exaltation, term, or triplicity <note>These are the "dignities" or levels of influence a planet has over a specific sign in the zodiac.</note>. If that planet is **retrograde** <note>Appearing to move backward.</note>, **combust** <note>Weakened by being too close to the Sun.</note>, or in a "bad place" in the chart, that commodity becomes cheap and will be of little value. If, however, it is in a place of strength or in an **angle** <note>The four most powerful houses in a chart (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th).</note>, and especially in the **Midheaven** <note>The highest point in the sky; the 10th house.</note>, then that commodity will be "heavy" and of great price. If the Lord of the house reaches the place of its **detriment** <note>original: "descensionis," the sign opposite to its place of power.</note>, that thing becomes cheap. You must also observe the aspects of the **fortunes** <note>The "benefic" planets, Jupiter and Venus, which bring good luck.</note> and the **malefic** planets <note>Saturn and Mars, which bring hardship.</note> toward them, as well as toward the Moon and its ruler. See who aspects the part itself: if a fortune and the Moon aspect the part, that resource will be multiplied; but if the malefics aspect it, it will suffer loss.

¶ The **Part of the Year’s Condition** is taken from the Moon to Mercury.
¶ The **Part of Water** is taken from the Moon to Venus.
¶ The **Part of Barley** is taken from the Moon to Jupiter.
¶ The **Part of Chickpeas** <note>original: "ciceris," a type of legume.</note> is taken from Venus to the Sun.
¶ The **Part of Lentils** is taken from Mars to Saturn.
¶ The **Part of Beans** is taken from Saturn to Mars.
¶ The **Part of Millet** <note>original: "almusti .i. milij."</note> is taken from Saturn to Mars.
¶ The **Part of Onions** is taken from Saturn to Mars.
¶ The **Part of Wheat** is taken from the Sun to Mars.
¶ The **Part of Nuts** is taken from Mercury to Mars.
¶ The **Part of Sugar** is taken from Mercury to Venus.
¶ The **Part of Dates** is taken from the Sun to Venus.
¶ The **Part of Honey** is taken from the Moon to the Sun.
¶ The **Part of Rice** <note>original: "alroz .i. ris," a type of grain.</note> is taken from Jupiter to Saturn.
¶ The **Part of Olives** is taken from Mercury to the Moon.
¶ The **Part of Grapes** is taken from Saturn to Venus.
¶ The **Part of Cotton** <note>original: "bombicis."</note> is taken from Mercury to Venus.
¶ The **Part of Sesame** <note>original: "alonicem .i. sisami," a white seed similar to flax seed used by physicians to make useful ointments.</note> is taken from Mercury to Jupiter, or from Saturn to Venus.
¶ The **Part of Melons** <note>original: "albuceflex," describing large, yellow melons.</note> is taken from Mercury to Saturn.
¶ The **Part of Sour Foods** is taken from Saturn to Mars.
¶ The **Part of Sweet Foods** is taken from the Sun to Venus.
¶ The **Part of Herb-flavored Foods** <note>Specifically those tasting of celery or similar herbs.</note> is taken from Mars to Saturn.
¶ The **Part of Bitter Foods** is taken from Mercury to Saturn.
¶ The **Part of Sharp Medicines** is taken from Saturn to Jupiter.
¶ The **Part of False Medicines** <note>Counterfeit or ineffective drugs.</note> is taken from Mars to the Moon.
¶ The **Part of Poisonous Medicines** is taken from the **Dragon’s Head** <note>original: "genzahar .i. a capite draconis," the North Node of the Moon.</note> to Saturn.
All these are to be projected from the Ascendant of the yearly revolution.

These are all the Parts that have come down to us. We have included even these most recent Parts, for even if the tradition regarding them is sometimes weak, we would not wish to omit anything that might serve as an introduction to the mastery of the judgments of the stars.
