...and the land of **Salacia** <note>original: "salacia"; likely referring to the coastal region or the Roman goddess of the sea, but the author corrects this to Scalavia, the land of the Slavs</note>. I believe it should be said of **Scalavia** and **Germany**. And those that agree with **Leo** are **Italy**, **Gaul**, **Apulia**, and **Sicily**. And **Haly** <note>Haly Abenragel, a 10th-11th century Arab astrologer</note> says there: that these are the borders of **Rome**. And those that agree with **Sagittarius** are the lands of **Turkey of the Roads** <note>original: "turcia calciata"; likely referring to the "paved" or "shod" regions of Turkey, indicating areas with Roman roads or infrastructure</note> and **Spain**. Regarding the partitioning of other signs with other lands, I care little for it. For neither their names nor the conditions of their people are known to me, nor would it be of any use to me to know. However, when someone knows them and wishes to dwell in them, let him apply the method described in the three aforesaid signs.

**Abraham Ibn Ezra** <note>original: "Abrahã auenezre"; a famous 12th-century Jewish polymath and astrologer</note> says that the **first climate** <term>climate: in ancient geography, these were latitudinal zones of the earth, each ruled by a planet</term> belongs to **Capricorn** and **Aquarius**, and its governor is **Saturn**. The **second climate** is of **Sagittarius** and **Pisces**, and its governor is **Jupiter**. The **third climate** is of **Scorpio** and **Aries**, and its governor is **Mars**. The **fourth climate** is of **Leo**, and its governor is the **Sun**. The **fifth climate** is of **Taurus** and **Libra**, and its governor is **Venus**. The **sixth climate** is of **Gemini** and **Virgo**, and its governor is **Mercury**. The **seventh climate** is of **Cancer**, and the **Moon** governs it. 

**Abraham Ibn Ezra** sets down the conditions of those born under each sign, and this is his opinion. Whoever is born under the **first face** <term>face: also known as a decan, a 10-degree division of a zodiac sign. Each sign has three "faces."</term> of **Aries** will be ruddy and snub-nosed; his belly will be spotted and narrow with thin flesh; he will have a mark above his left foot and on his left elbow; he will have many friends and he himself will hate evil. He who is born under the **second face** will have a darkness joined to the beauty of his face; his body will be temperate, but he will be quick to anger, hiding an evil will in his mind; he is high-hearted, and his enemies will be many. He who is born under the **third face** will be of a ruddy and saffron-yellow color, and he will be solitary. 

Whoever is born under the **first face** of **Taurus** will be of short stature; his eyes will be large, his lips thick, and he will have a mark on his neck; he is hasty of heart; his friends will be many, and he will delight in various kinds of luxuries. One born under the **second face** will have a round face, a wide belly, and beautiful eyes; his soul is willful and things proceed quickly for him; he will be hairy around the shoulder blades and will have a mark on his loins. And whoever is born under the **third face** will be of a beautiful color and a beautiful face; he will have a mark in his left eye and will be a hardworking man, yet he will not be fortunate with women. He who is born at the very end of this sign will naturally lack testicles and be without them, or be a **hermaphrodite** <note>original: "hermofroditus"</note>. 

Whoever is born under the **first face** of **Gemini** will be of a beautiful and decent body, with fine eyes and hair; he will have a mark on his head or in his eyes. His sight is sharp; he is very little prone to anger and will be a laborer; yet he will be very little fortunate with women. And he who is born under the **second face** will be of short stature and dark, with a black mark upon his elbow; his words are beautiful and sweet. One born under the **third face** will have excessively small eyes; he will be suspicious and fickle, speaking irrational words, and he will be a liar.

Whoever is born under the **first face** of **Cancer** will be decent in body and hair; his eyebrows will be narrow, his nostrils long, and his shoulders wide; he will have a mark on his right elbow or his arm; his soul is good, his friends are many, and he will be knowledgeable and ingenious. He who is born under the **second face** will be red in color and of short stature, lacking a beard, and will have a mark in his eyes. He who is born under the **third face** will be fat and of short stature, and will have a multitude of hair in his eyebrows.

Whoever is born under the **first face** of **Leo** will be beautiful in body and face; the color of his countenance will be red mixed with white; his eyes will be mixed; his chest straight; and his legs will suffer disease in the upper part; he will be well-known among people, and simple, and will mingle in the company of kings. He who is born under the **second face** will be beautiful; his chest will be wide, his testicles and legs slender; he will be honored among his family and be high-hearted. One born under the **third face** will be somewhat short of stature...

...[stature], and his color will be white mixed with rosy. His voice will be powerful; he will be a lover of women, he will have a great number of friends, and he will endure many illnesses.

There are three faces per sign.</term> of **Virgo** will be of decent stature; his body will be erect and beautiful. His voice will be powerful, he will be sterile, his soul good, his face beautiful, and he will be a scribe and well-educated. 

He who is born under the **second face** will be of beautiful appearance; his eyes will be small, his nose beautiful, and he will be teachable and simple. Being of a willing heart, he will love to be praised.

He who is born under the **first face** of **Scorpio** will be only slightly beautiful and will have a mark on his head, and his chest will be wide. He will have a mark on his left foot or right hand and is a man of discipline <note>original: "hõ castigationis"; literally a man of correction or refinement</note>, full of good discretion, and hasty in speech. 

One born under the **second face** will have a large head and will be of a moderately beautiful figure. He will have a mark on the knee and back, and will be a man of discipline who multiplies his words <note>original: "verba sua multiplicãs"; meaning he is talkative or verbose</note>. 

He who is born under the **third face** will be of short stature, with twisted eyes, an appetite for eating, and a lover of women. But he who is born at the end of this sign will be either of no sex or of both. 

He who is born under the **first face** of **Sagittarius** will be of beautiful appearance, comely to look at, and of straight stature; he will be diligent in good works, mingling with kings and nobles. 

One born under the **second face** will have a decent body, yet his face will be yellowish with compressed eyebrows, and he will have a mark on his chest. 

One born under the **third face** will be tall and beautiful of face; his eyes will be like a cat's <term>eyes like a cat's: the original Latin "vt murilegiz" literally means "like a mouse-catcher," a common medieval term for a cat</term>, his chest wide, and he will have a mark on his left leg. 

He who is born under the **first face** of **Capricorn** will have a decent body and a wide chest; he will have a black mark on his elbow and will be cunning, teachable, simple, and willing. 

One born under the **second face** will be beautiful; he will have long nostrils and beautiful eyes. His will is bad and he is quick to anger, yet he is a man of knowledge. 

One born under the **third face** will be beautiful of body, yet his face will be the color of a citron <note>a pale, yellowish complexion</note>. He will have a mark on his left arm or on his knee; he is hasty in anger, despising evil or perhaps fighting for an evil cause, a lover of women, a man of discipline, and one who loves his companions. But he who is born at the end of this sign will be a child of adultery, and this degree is of very little value in the births of women. 

He who is born under the **first face** of **Aquarius** will be shapely in body and face, and will have a mark on his chest or left foot; he will be a teachable man who loves his companions. 

One born under the **second face** will be tall; his face will be red, and he will have a mark on his back and under his elbow, and he will be in pain all his days. 

One born under the **third face** will be beautiful of body, short in stature, and rosy-faced; he will have a mark under his elbows and will be a lover of women. And he who is born at the end of this sign will be changeable in his figure and in all his works. 

He who draws his origin from the **first face** of **Pisces**: his body will be white, and his face likewise. His chest will be wide, his beard beautiful, his forehead clear, and his eyes more black than white. And perhaps some limb will be missing from him. He will love to sleep, and will be a glutton and a drunkard, and he will have a mark under his elbow...

<summary>This page details the physical traits, personality quirks, and "marks" (birthmarks or scars) associated with the three decans or "faces" of the zodiac signs from Virgo to Pisces. It reflects the medieval belief in physiognomy—that a person's character and fate are written in their physical appearance.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the description of the "faces" (decans) of the zodiac signs, specifically the final faces of Pisces, and begins a new section on how the planets influence specific parts of the human body when located within those signs. It then introduces the classification of specific degrees within the zodiac.</meta>

...or in the foot. But one born under the **second face** <term>face: a ten-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign; there are three faces per sign</term> will be of short stature. He will be handsome in appearance, with a black beard, and hairy; he will walk in hardship among the sons of men. One born under the **third face** of **Pisces** will be illustrious in appearance; his eyes will be beautiful, but he will be prone to illness.

---

<margin>Previously, the author showed how the signs correspond to the members of the human body.</margin>
## And if indeed the planets.
In this part, he shows us how the **planets** relate to the members of the human body within those same signs. This part can be divided into twelve sections according to the twelve signs; where these sections begin is clear. The first part can be divided into two: first, he proposes the idea, and second, he follows it through starting with **Aries**. He says first that if any planet signifies some infirmity or weakness in the human body, it will have a specific member in each sign in which that infirmity will reside. And because this is so, it is appropriate to treat the pains caused by the planets in the signs. He begins from Aries, proceeding according to the succession of the signs and planets. 

What the author says is plain in the text. To clarify one part, I will provide an example: Suppose that in a certain birth <note>original: "natiuitate"; refers to a natal chart or the moment of birth</note>, **Saturn** signifies a weakness of some member for the newborn, and Saturn is in **Aries**. I say that this weakness or infirmity will be in the chest. And if Saturn is in **Taurus**, that infirmity will be in the belly, and you may understand the same for the other signs. I also propose that if **Mars** in the birth—understand this also for the revolution of the years of the birth <term>revolution: also known as a solar return, a chart calculated for the exact moment the Sun returns to its birth position each year</term>—signifies a wounding, and Mars is in Aries, the wound will be in the head. If in Taurus, the wound will be in the neck; and you may understand the same for the other signs and planets. 

**Ptolemy** <note>Claudius Ptolemy, a 2nd-century Greco-Egyptian mathematician and astronomer whose works formed the basis of medieval astrology</note> maintains that the members in which diseases and impediments fall are taken from the figure of the heavens at the hour of birth or revolution. For he says in the 74th proposition of the *Centiloquium* <note>The "Hundred Sayings," a collection of astrological aphorisms attributed to Ptolemy</note> that when Mars is in the **ascendant** <term>ascendant: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> of someone’s birth, there will be a scar on the face or head of the newborn. **Haly** <note>Likely Haly Abenragel, an 11th-century Arab astrologer</note> says that the head belongs to the ascendant (the first house), and according to this, the neck belongs to the **second house**, the arms to the **third house**, and so on according to the order of the houses, just as was said regarding the signs. Concerning this matter, we shall speak more diffusely, Lord willing, in the chapter on the **sixth house** <note>The astrological "house" traditionally associated with health and illness</note>.

---

<margin>In this part, the author determines certain special properties of the planets which are in various degrees of the circle of signs, and there are six of them.</margin>
## There are also...
For there are in the signs certain **masculine degrees** and certain **feminine** ones. And there are certain **bright degrees**, and some **dark** ones. There are also certain degrees which are called **wells** <term>wells: or "putei," specific degrees that were thought to "sink" or weaken the influence of a planet placed in them</term>. And there are certain degrees called **azemena** <term>azemena: from Arabic, meaning "lame" or "weakened"; degrees associated with permanent physical disabilities or chronic illness</term>. And there are certain degrees called **increasing fortune**. And there are certain degrees called **consorts**. 

According to this, this part can be divided into six sections: since in the first part he enumerates the masculine and feminine degrees. In the second part, he enumerates the bright, dark, and smoky degrees. In the third part, he enumerates the degrees called "wells." In the fourth part, he enumerates the degrees called "azemena." In the fifth part, he enumerates the degrees that increase fortune and the degrees of sublimity. In the sixth part, he enumerates the degrees called "consorts." 

The first part begins at the start. The second begins there: "and in each of these signs." The third begins there: "and in the signs there are degrees." The fourth there: "and they are in the signs..."

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological degrees within the zodiac signs, shifting from the influence of planets on the body to the specific qualities of individual degrees, such as those that are fortunate, dark, or associated with physical illness.</meta>

...sign there are certain degrees. The fifth part is there: "and in the circle." The sixth is there: "and all two degrees."

The first part can be divided into two: because in the first part he begins to list the masculine and feminine degrees literally. In the second part, he provides a table for them. What he says is clear in the text. Afterward, he says there are certain degrees in the circle which are called bright, and some dark, and some smoky, and some which are called empty. And he begins first to list them. And then he provides a table for them, and it is clear. 

After this, he says there are certain degrees in the circle called pits <note>original: "putei"; these were specific degrees thought to "swallow" a planet's influence, leading to misfortune or failure</note>. And he lists them all in the text, and some books have a table made for them. Then he says that there are certain notable degrees called *azemena* <term>azemena: from an Arabic term for "chronically ill"; these degrees were traditionally associated with permanent physical disabilities</term>. And he says that *azemena* is a certain physical debility, such as blindness or deafness or something similar, or the loss of a limb. 

Regarding this, it should be noted what Haly Abenragel <note>An 11th-century Arab astrologer whose work, *The Complete Book on the Judgment of the Stars*, was a primary textbook for medieval European students</note> says in the first chapter of the sixth house of his book on nativities: When the *azemena* part is in the division of the ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note>, and the Moon is joined with it or with the lord of the ascendant, it signifies that the debility will be in that limb of the newborn which corresponds to the division of that sign.

¶ It should also be noted that if the Sun, at someone's birth, is in an *azemena* degree, there will be a debility in the right eye; if it is the Moon, it will be in the left. 

Afterward, he says there are certain degrees in the circle called "increasers of fortune," and he lists them in the text. Regarding this part, it should be noted what Haly says in the commentary on the 229th proposition of the first part of the *Quadripartitum* <note>original: "quadripartiti"; refers to Ptolemy's *Tetrabiblos*, the foundational text of Western astrology</note>: you should know that the degrees increasing fortune are the degrees of the exaltation <term>exaltation: a specific sign and degree where a planet is thought to have its most powerful and harmonious influence</term> of the Sun and of the fortunate planets, such as the 19th of Aries, the 3rd of Taurus, the 15th of Cancer, and the 27th of Pisces. Likewise, they are those in which the "bounds" <term>bounds: also called 'terms'; five irregular subdivisions of a zodiac sign, each ruled by a different planet</term> of the fortunate planets are joined, such as the 15th of Sagittarius and the 15th of Pisces; and they are also the degrees of the bounds of fortune. And also the degrees in which there are fixed stars of a fortunate nature; and conversely, those in which there are stars of an unfortunate nature. 

And afterward he says that any two degrees that are at the same distance from the heads of the movable signs <term>movable signs: also known as cardinal signs (Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn), which mark the start of the four seasons</term> are of the same strength. And they are called "partners" <note>original: "confortes"; degrees that mirror each other across the solstices or equinoxes</note>, such as the 20th degree of Capricorn and the 10th degree of Sagittarius. For these two degrees are equally distant from the head of Capricorn; and the 20th of Libra and the 10th of Virgo are equally distant from the head of Libra. And the 20th of Cancer and the 10th of Gemini are equally distant from the beginning of Cancer; and the 20th degree of Aries and the 10th of Pisces are equally distant from the beginning of Aries. 

¶ Ptolemy says in the 229th proposition of the first part: other astrologers have spoken much about degrees and have divided them into degrees that are bright, slippery <note>original: "lubricos"; likely referring to degrees that are unstable or difficult to interpret</note>, and smoky. And among them are the *azemena*, and among them are the pits. And they spoke of many other things as you will find throughout many books of astronomy. And if they found this through experience, it is good that you work according to it. And perhaps if it happened to them that some planet or star was in a degree, or its ray <note>the light or "aspect" cast by a planet to a specific degree</note>, and it produced good fortune, they concluded that the degree itself had done this. And if indeed this is so, it is fitting that you move from one degree to another. And here let us put an end to our reasoning. 

It appears through this text that Ptolemy does not strongly approve of the "degrees increasing fortune," etc. For he says if this is so, it is fitting that you move from one degree to another; for degrees are called bright or dark because of the fixed stars existing there. And Haly maintains, according to what was previously cited, that degrees are called "increasing fortune" when stars of a fortunate nature are within them. However, it is well known that the fixed stars change their positions with respect to the zodiac of the ninth sphere <note>a reference to the precession of the equinoxes, where the positions of stars slowly shift relative to the zodiac signs over centuries, making fixed degree-lists potentially outdated</note>. From that text of Ptolemy...

<summary>This page discusses the classification of specific zodiac degrees, including "pits," "increasing fortune," and "azemena" degrees associated with physical disabilities. It contrasts traditional degree lists with Ptolemy’s more skeptical view that these effects might stem from shifting fixed stars rather than the degrees themselves.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of specialized astrological degrees within the zodiac, specifically addressing how certain degrees affect a planet's power to predict success, failure, or physical traits. It concludes the section on individual degrees and begins a new structural analysis of the "accidental" properties of the zodiac, such as the twelve houses.</meta>

...it can be argued that judgments ought to be made according to the ninth sphere <note>In medieval cosmology, the ninth sphere was often associated with the tropical zodiac (the signs), while the eighth sphere contained the fixed stars; this refers to the complex debate over whether astrology should follow the stars or the seasons.</note> and not according to the eighth. For from this he argues that it is necessary to change these degrees; it follows that the degrees according to which judgments ought to be made do not change. **Abu Ma'shar** <note>original: "Albumazar"; a 9th-century Persian astronomer and one of the most influential astrologers of the Middle Ages</note> in his *Introduction* sets out these degrees: namely masculine and feminine, bright and dark, and so on. And he says there that if a planet in a birth chart or in an interrogation <note>a "horary" question asked of an astrologer</note> of a man is in a masculine degree, or in a birth or interrogation of a woman is in a feminine degree, its significance will be stronger.

Regarding bright, dark, and smoky degrees, he says that if a planet falls in a bright degree, its significance will be stronger in the indication of honors and signifies beauty. And if it falls in a dark degree, it signifies hardness, slowness, a horrible thing, and something dark and evil. And when it falls in a dusky, shadowy, or smoky degree, or in an empty degree, it signifies something slightly horrible. And if a planet falls in a "pit" degree <note>original: "putei"; specific degrees thought to "swallow" or negate a planet's positive influence</note>, its beauty and appearance depart, and it is weakened in its significance. For even the Fortunes <note>the "benefic" planets, Jupiter and Venus, which bring good luck</note> when they fall into them are weakened in their significance. Truly the Malefics <note>the "malevolent" planets, Saturn and Mars</note>, when they fall into them, their [evil] significance is weakened. And perhaps it will even signify an accidental good fortune because their power to do evil is weakened.

Concerning degrees that increase fortune, he says the ancients thought that there were certain degrees in the circle that increase fortune. And they said that when the planets are not in places signifying the fortune of the newborn, but the Moon or the Lot of Fortune <note>original: "pars fortune"; a specific mathematical point in a horoscope representing prosperity and success</note> is in these degrees, or if the degrees themselves are rising on the horizon, they increase the fortune of the newborn. Even if the planets signify a downfall for him, these degrees move him toward elevation by a certain amount through a specific influence. Concerning degrees of sublimity, he says: Some have said that when the rising sign is one of these degrees, or the Sun is in a daytime birth, or the Moon in a nighttime birth is in certain of them, and it is in the best possible place in the chart—and when the planets of the birth-root <note>the original configuration of the sky at birth</note> signify this—it will lead the newborn to sublimity and a seat of nobility, and he will rule over lands and cities, and possess much wealth.

<margin>
The author previously determined concerning the essence [of the zodiac].
</margin>
**And** because, with God’s help, [having finished with] the essential being of the circle of signs, here he determines its accidental being <note>In medieval philosophy, "essence" refers to what a thing is, while "accidents" refer to how it relates to other things—in this case, how the zodiac signs relate to the Earth and the observer through the system of "houses."</note>. And this is divided into two parts: first, he connects what has been said with what is to be said. Second, he follows with: "for the circle of signs." And that is divided into two parts: first, he determines the accidental division of the circle of signs. Second, he determines the properties and significations following that division, starting at: "the fourth part, which is from the ascendant." This is further divided into two parts: first, he sets out the properties and significations of the quarters. Second, he sets out the significations of the individual houses; and that begins at: "and each of the houses."

First, he says that after completing the essential being of the circle of signs, he now wishes to treat its accidental being. Afterward, he says that the circle is represented every hour by such a figure <note>the square or circular horoscope chart</note> which is divided into four parts, divided by the horizon line and the meridian line. And each of these quarters is divided into three equal parts according to the division of the rising sign in the direct circle. And in this manner, the whole circle is divided into 12 parts which are called "towers" or "houses" <note>original: "turres vel domus"; the twelve segments of a horoscope</note>; the explanation of which is found in the rules of the astronomical tables. Two great circles must be understood passing through the poles of the world: one of which is the meridian circle, and the other a circle passing through the point of the zodiac which is in...

<summary>This page concludes a discussion on how specific zodiac degrees (bright, dark, or "pits") modify planetary influence and begins a new section on the twelve astrological houses, explaining how the sky is divided by the horizon and meridian.</summary>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the zodiac's structure, moving from the specific power of individual degrees to the calculation of the twelve astrological houses and the symbolic qualities of the four quadrants of the sky.</meta>

...the contact of the eastern horizon: and through the opposite point which is in the contact of the western horizon, intersecting the meridian circle at the poles of the world <note>The north and south celestial poles around which the stars appear to rotate</note>. These two circles divide the entire heaven into four parts which are sometimes equal to each other and sometimes unequal; however, for the most part, they are unequal. For if the beginning of Aries or the beginning of Libra is at the ascendant <note>The point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</note>, these quarters are equal. In all other arrangements, however, they are unequal. After this, one should imagine a portion of the equinoctial circle <note>The celestial equator</note> intercepted between the meridian circle and the circle passing through the start of the ascendant to be divided into three equal parts. Similarly, the other portion of the equinoctial circle which is from the ascendant down to the lower part of the meridian circle—that is, to the <term>Angle of the Earth</term> <note>original: "angulum terre"; the Imum Coeli or IC, the lowest point of the chart representing the northern "midnight" position</note>—is understood to be divided into three equal parts. Then, imagine great circles passing through these aforementioned divisions and through the poles of the world. Then the portion of the zodiac intercepted between any two of these primary circles is one "house." This is the position of **Ptolemy** <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century foundational authority on astronomy and astrology</note>, and it is the one commonly held. There is also another concept of the houses held by **Abraham Ibn Ezra** <note>original: "Habrahā auenezre"; a celebrated 12th-century Jewish scholar and astrologer</note>. He imagines circles passing through the divisions of the equinoctial and through the intersection of the meridian circle and the horizon in the northern part. This way is not commonly held; therefore, I do not care to explain it much. Moreover, to find the beginnings of the twelve houses using an **astrolabe** <note>A handheld portable tool used by medieval astronomers to solve problems relating to time and the position of the stars</note> is not difficult; indeed, it is easy. To insert the instruction for it here would be long and superfluous, since it is sufficiently set down in the "Canons of the Astrolabe." Whoever wishes to "equate" the houses <note>Calculate the exact boundaries of the houses</note> by using tables will find complete instruction in the canons of the tables of the *Primum Mobile*. Next, the author says the beginning of this division is the ascendant, the start of which is at the point of the hemisphere on the eastern side. Then follows the second house, then the third, and so on up to the twelfth.

<margin>Here the author sets down the properties and significances of the circle of signs</margin>

# As for the fourth part

following its accidental division: he says that the quarter of the circle which is from the ascendant up to the Midheaven <note>original: "medium celi"; the highest point of the sky</note>—namely the 12th, 11th, and 10th houses—is called the eastern, masculine, "approaching" quarter. It signifies the beginning of life and is called "childish" and "sanguine" <note>The humor associated with blood, sociality, and the season of spring</note>. The second quarter is from the Midheaven to the degree of the west—namely the 9th, 8th, and 7th houses—and is called the southern, feminine quarter "receding" from us. It signifies middle age, which is called the "perfection of youth," and it is called "summery" and "choleric" <note>The humor associated with yellow bile, ambition, and the heat of summer</note>. The third quarter, which is from the degree of the west to the Angle of the Earth—namely the 6th, 5th, and 4th houses—is the western, masculine quarter "approaching" us. It signifies the end of life and is called "autumnal" and "melancholic" <note>The humor associated with black bile, introspection, and the harvest season</note>; it represents late middle age. The fourth part, which is from the Angle of the Earth to the ascendant—namely the 3rd, 2nd, and 1st houses—is the northern, feminine quarter "receding" from us. It signifies what happens to a person after their death regarding their memory among men—namely, whether good or evil is spoken of them. This part is called "senile," "phlegmatic" <note>The humor associated with phlegm, lethargy, and the cold of winter</note>, "deficient," and "wintry." This comparison which the author sets down here is exactly like the one he set down in the essential division of the circle, except that he adds here that they signify the beginning, middle, and end of life, and what happens to a person after death. Afterward, he says that the entire half that is from the Midheaven, going through the ascendant to the fourth house, is called the ascending half. And the other half, which is from the fourth house, going through the western degree up to the Midheaven, is called...

<summary>This page explains the geometrical construction of the twelve astrological houses, contrasting Ptolemy's common method with that of Abraham Ibn Ezra, and details the symbolic associations of the four quadrants of the sky with the stages of human life and the four humors.</summary>
<keywords>house division, Ptolemy, Abraham Ibn Ezra, astrolabe, Angle of the Earth, four humors, sanguine, choleric, melancholic, phlegmatic, stages of life, reputation after death</keywords>

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the celestial houses. The previous page discussed the mathematical division of the sky based on the horizon and meridian; this page moves into the interpretive meanings of those divisions and how they apply to a person's life and the "querent's" (the person asking a question) intentions.</meta>

...is above the earth is called the right; and that which is below the earth is called the left. Then it says that the first houses—namely the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th—are called the **angles** <note>The most powerful houses in a chart, corresponding to the four cardinal directions</note>. And the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th are called **succedents** <note>original: "succedentes"; houses that follow or "succeed" the angles</note> to the angles. The 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th are called **cadents** <note>original: "cadentes"; houses that "fall away" from the angles</note> from the angles. Then he adds a notable point: he says when a planet is in an angle or in a succedent, it is said to "prosper" or be effective; and if it is in the cadents, it is said to "fail" or be weakened.

# And each of these houses.

And each of these houses.
Here the author sets down the specific meanings of each of the twelve houses. And this entire part can be divided into four: for in the first part, he sets down the meanings of the twelve houses regarding the life of men. In the second part, he sets down the meanings of the houses regarding colors. In the third part, he sets down certain rules which can be called **aphorisms** <note>original: "amphorismi"; short, pithy principles of astrological judgment</note>. In the fourth part, he teaches how to choose **significators** <note>The specific planets or points that represent a person or topic in a chart</note>. The first part begins at the start. The second begins where it says: "they signify: and the 12 houses [signify] colors." The third begins: "and it is said in the meaning of the houses." The fourth begins: "and when you wish to know the planet ruling the matter." 

The first part can be divided into twelve sections according to how he sets down the meanings of the twelve houses; where they begin will be clear. First, he says that each house signifies something about the state of human beings. For the first house, which begins from the eastern horizon and is called the **ascendant** <note>original: "ascendens"; the point rising in the east at a specific moment</note>, signifies the bodies of men and life; the beginnings of all works in **interrogations** <note>original: "interrogationibus"; the practice of "horary astrology" where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked</note>, and speeches, and rumors, and other such things. And it signifies the thought of the **querent** <note>original: "querentis"; the person seeking the astrological consultation</note>, namely what the querent has in their mind, and it signifies the beginning of life. 

And the lord of the **triplicity** <note>original: "triplicitatis"; a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element. Each triplicity was ruled by three specific planets acting as "lords."</note> of the first house: the first lord signifies the life and nature of the **native** <note>the person born at the time of the chart</note> or the querent; and his pleasures and desires—what he loves or what he hates—and what good or evil happens to him at the beginning of life. And the second lord of the triplicity of this house signifies life and the body and the virtue or strength of the native and the middle of life. The third lord of the triplicity signifies the same as a companion and signifies, of itself, the end of life. From this text <note>original: "lĩa", abbreviation for "littera"</note> it can be gathered that if the first lord of the triplicity of this house is stronger than the other two, the first part of life will be better; and you should understand the same regarding the other two.

Regarding this house, certain things must be noted. One should note first concerning the intention of the querent what **Ptolemy** says in the 94th proposition of the *Centiloquium* <note>original: "centilogi"; a famous collection of 100 astrological secrets attributed to the astronomer Claudius Ptolemy</note>. The place of the stronger significator in the ascendant represents that which is in the mind of the interrogator. Therefore, when you wish to know the intention of the querent: see which planet has the most strengths in the ascendant at that hour when they come to you with the intention of asking; and see in which of the twelve houses that planet is located. You shall then speak according to the meanings of that house in which it is found. 

For example: I suppose the ascendant is Leo and the Sun has the most strengths in the degree of the ascendant; and I suppose the Sun is in the second house. I would say that his intention is to ask about his **substance** <note>material wealth or possessions</note>, or about profit, or about something similar. And if the Sun had been in the third house, I would have said that he wanted to ask something about the state of a brother or brothers, and so on for the other houses in their own manner. 

And if the person coming to you holds some thing in their hand or has hidden it in another way: if you wish to know the substance and nature of that thing, look at the stronger planet in the ascendant. If it "aspects" <note>forms a geometric relationship with</note> the ascendant, that thing will be of the substance of the sign that is currently ascending. If it does not aspect the ascendant, it will be of the substance of the sign in which that planet is located. If it is an **earthy sign** <note>Taurus, Virgo, or Capricorn</note>: it will be of the nature of earth or some thing that grows from the earth. If it is a **watery sign** <note>Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces</note>: it will be some thing of the nature of water, or which grows in water. If it is **airy** <note>Gemini, Libra, or Aquarius</note>: it will be some thing of the nature of air. If it is a [fiery] sign...

<meta>This page continues the astrological instructions for interpreting a birth chart or a question. While the previous page categorized the twelve houses of the zodiac, this page provides specific diagnostic rules—known as aphorisms—for determining the physical properties of objects, the lifespan of a person, and the survival of newborns.</meta>

...will be fiery or another thing that works through fire. And if you wish to know the color of a thing, look to the **Lord of the Hour** <note>The planet ruling the specific hour the question was asked</note> and judge according to its color. You will speak of the colors of the planets later where the natures of the planets are discussed, the Lord <note>original: "domino"; referring here to God</note> proceeding. And if you wish to know whether a thing is new or old, look to the Moon: if it is above the earth, it will be new; if it is below the earth, it will be old. And if you wish to know whether it is long or short: look to the Lord of the **term** <note>A specific subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> of the degree of the **angle of the earth** <note>The 4th House or the northern angle</note>; for if it is at the **apogee** <note>original: "auge"; the point in a planet's orbit furthest from Earth</note> of its circle, the thing will be long. If it is in the opposite of the apogee, it will be short. If in the middle distances, it will be middling between long and short. This is the opinion of Ptolemy <note>A 2nd-century Greco-Egyptian astronomer/astrologer whose work was the foundation of the field</note> in the 90th proposition of the *Centiloquium*. When the Moon regards the significator of the Ascendant, etc.

Haly Abenragel <note>An 11th-century Arab astrologer whose "Book on the Judgment of the Stars" was a standard textbook in Medieval Europe</note> says: if someone asks you about their life, whether it be long or short: look to the **Lord of the Ascendant** <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> and the Moon. If they are safe from **malefics** <note>original: "infortunis"; planets like Mars or Saturn that were thought to cause harm</note>, they signify a long life. And if they are **combust** <note>original: "combusti"; so close to the Sun that their power is "burned up"</note> or afflicted by malefics, they signify a short life.

¶ It should be noted regarding **nutrition** <note>original: "nutritionem"; referring to whether an infant will survive and be successfully reared</note>, namely whether a child may live or not. Haly Abenragel says when the **Lords of the Triplicity** <note>The three planets that rule the "element" (fire, earth, air, water) of a sign</note> of the **luminary of the time**—by which he means the Lords of the Triplicity of the sign in which the Sun is located if it is a daytime birth, or the sign of the Moon in a nighttime birth—are in the Ascendant, or in the 10th house, or the 11th, or the 5th, it is a sign of good and easy nutrition. And if they are in the 7th house, they signify bad nutrition, full of anxiety and labor. When the degree of the Ascendant and the luminary of the time are "damaged," it signifies that the child will not be nourished unless the Lords of the Triplicity are safe and firm in the **angles** <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>. When the Moon is afflicted by malefics in the Ascendant, it does not signify nutrition. When the Moon is in the 4th house joined bodily with a malefic, or regards a malefic by a **quartile** <note>A 90-degree angle</note> or **opposition** <note>A 180-degree angle</note>, it signifies that the child will not be nourished and its mother is in danger, or perhaps will die.

Abraham Avenezre <note>Abraham Ibn Ezra, a 12th-century Jewish scholar and astrologer</note> says: If the Lord of the Ascendant is combust, the newborn will not live the space of 8 days. The birth chart of a certain boy was brought to me whose Ascendant was Virgo, and Mercury <note>The ruler of Virgo</note> was in Pisces (in its **detriment** <note>A sign where a planet's power is weakened</note>), **retrograde** <note>Appearing to move backward</note>, and combust. And I believed in this saying and I said that the boy would not live for eight days, and he was dead on the sixth day.

Haly Abenragel says: a conjunction of planets does not signify humanity. He means to say that a conjunction of many planets in the Ascendant does not signify survival. And he places here a notable example. He says: the king of our city called me, and one of the women had given birth to a son, and the Ascendant was Libra at 8 degrees, in the term of Mercury. And Jupiter, Venus, Mars, and Mercury were in it. A company of astrologers gathered there, and each of them spoke his opinion, but I remained silent. The king said to me, "What is it with you that you do not speak?" To whom I responded: "Give me a term of three days; for if your son passes the third day, it will be a great miracle." And when the newborn had completed 24 hours, he set himself to speaking; and he spoke and made a sign with his hand, and the king was greatly terrified by it. And he said it was possible that he might speak some prophecy or some miracle. And the king went to the newborn, and we with him, to hear what he would say. And the infant said: "I am born unfortunate, and I am born to judge the loss of the kingdom and the destruction of the **German people** <note>original: "gentis almanam"</note>." Immediately after this, the newborn fell and died.

¶ Regarding those who die before they receive food, Ptolemy says in the *Quadripartitum*: I say generally that when any luminary is in one of the angles, and similarly some malefic has a participation with it in longitude which is with it degree by degree, or regarding it in a figure of two equals <note>possibly referring to a specific aspect like a square or opposition</note>, and a **benefic** <note>original: "fortuna"; a lucky planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> has no participation with it in the figure—and the **dispositor** <note>The planet ruling the sign where the luminaries are located</note> of the place of the luminaries is found in the places of malefic planets—that newborn will not receive food and will die in the hour in which he is born.

<meta>This page continues the specific astrological rules for interpretation, moving from the concerns of the First House (the self and beginnings) to the Second House (wealth and resources). It references several major authorities used in medieval astrology, including Ibn Ezra, John of Seville, and Ptolemy.</meta>

Abraham <note>original: "Abraham"; likely Abraham Ibn Ezra, a 12th-century Jewish polymath and scholar</note> says that when the **Dragon's Tail** <note>original: "cauda draconis"; the South Node of the Moon, traditionally associated with loss or decrease</note> is in the **Ascendant** <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> of a birth chart, that person will be blind; and according to its distance from the degree of the Ascendant, so shall the years be. For example: if it is at the 10th degree from the Ascendant, he will go blind in his 10th year. **Elections** <note>The practice of choosing an auspicious time to start an action</note> belonging to this house concern the beginnings of things, such as starting to build or anything similar. Hyspalensis <note>original: "Hyspalensis"; John of Seville, a prolific 12th-century translator of Arabic astrological texts into Latin</note> says regarding elections of the First House: "If you know someone's birth chart, take care in his election not to place a sign in the Ascendant in which there was an **ill-fortune** <note>original: "infortuna"; a malefic planet like Saturn or Mars</note> at his birth." If, however, you do not know the birth chart, choose according to the nature of the thing for which the election is made. For example: if the election is for beginning to write a book, let **Mercury** <note>The planet associated with communication and writing</note> be strengthened. If for going to war, let **Mars** <note>The planet of war</note> be strengthened. If for building, let a **fixed sign** <note>Signs like Taurus or Leo, associated with stability and endurance</note> be placed in the Ascendant. The same Hyspalensis says: "If you wish to choose an hour for **bloodletting** <note>original: "sanguinis minuendi"; a standard medieval medical treatment</note>, ensure that the Moon is in a **trine or sextile aspect** <note>Harmonious angular relationships of 120 or 60 degrees</note> to Mars. And beware that she [the Moon] is not joined with him [Mars] bodily <note>original: "corporaliter"; meaning a conjunction</note>, nor in a **square or opposition** <note>Tense angular relationships of 90 or 180 degrees</note> to him. And ensure the Moon is in a sign corresponding to the **humor** <note>One of the four bodily fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, or black bile—that were thought to govern health</note> being reduced."

## Second House.

<margin>Here the author provides the significations of the second house. And he says that the sec-</margin>
<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'S' decorated with foliage and strapwork.</image-desc>
The Second House signifies wealth <note>original: "substantiam"</note>, livelihood, helpers, and ministers, and it signifies the end of youth. And **Anduzgār** <note>Likely Andruzagar, an early Persian astrologer whose work influenced later medieval texts</note> said: "Look at which of the three **Lords of the Triplicity** <note>A system of three planets that rule a set of zodiac signs belonging to the same element</note> of the house of wealth is stronger in itself—that is, in the place where it sits in the chart—and regard that as the **significator** <note>The planet that represents a specific person or topic in a chart</note> of wealth." If this planet is in the **Tenth House** <note>The house of career, status, and authority</note>, he will receive wealth from a king. And if it is in the **House of Trust**—that is, in the Eleventh—it will be better. Another reading <note>original: "Alia littera"; literally "another letter" or "another manuscript version"</note> says: "if it is in the House of Faith, it will be greater." I believe the first reading is true. For the House of Trust is the house of the king's wealth. And the first Lord of the Triplicity gives wealth in the beginning of life; if he is fortunate, the **native** <note>The person for whom the birth chart was cast</note> will be rich in the beginning of life. The second gives wealth in the middle of life; if he is fortunate, the native will be rich in the middle of life. The third gives wealth at the end of life; if he is fortunate, the native will be rich at the end of life. And understand the same regarding their misfortunes: that is, if any of them is ill-fortuned, the person will lose wealth in that part of life assigned to it. Hyspalensis says in the rules of this house: "Consider whether the Lord of the Second House is in the Ascendant, or gives its power to the Lord of the Ascendant, and is not in a **fallen house** <note>original: "domo lapsa"; a cadent house, which is traditionally considered weak</note>. If so, he will have profit without labor." And if the opposite occurs, the reverse happens. And if **Jupiter** <note>The "Greater Benefic" planet, associated with prosperity and growth</note> is in an **angle** <note>One of the four most powerful houses: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note> in the birth chart, or is in its **dignity** <note>original: "honore"; a sign where a planet has specific strength</note>, or has power in the Ascendant, or regards the **Part of Fortune** <note>A calculated point in a chart representing success and prosperity</note>; or if it is the Lord of the house of the Sun in a daytime birth, or the Lord of the house of the Moon in a nighttime birth, or the **Lord of the Hour**, then the person will not lack for means in his life, because he—that is, Jupiter—is the Lord of wealth. If the Lord of the Ascendant regards the Lord of wealth, or its house, or the Part of Fortune with an **aspect of enmity** <note>A square or opposition</note>, he will destroy his wealth with his own hands. If the Lord of the Ascendant is in the Second House and is ill-fortuned, he will give away his wealth of his own will. But if an ill-fortuned planet is there that is not the Lord of the Ascendant, his wealth will be taken from him by force or by theft. If **Capricorn** is ascending, the person will be greedy and a miser, because the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Second House are the same—namely, **Saturn** <note>The planet of restriction and scarcity; in many systems, Saturn rules both Capricorn and the following sign, Aquarius, which would make it the ruler of both the 1st and 2nd houses here</note>. Ptolemy says in the rules of this house: "It is fitting for us to investigate matters pertaining to the profit of wealth solely through the Part of Fortune, which we know is always found by the distance between the Sun and the Moon, projected from the Ascendant in births by day and in births by night." According to this, it should be noted that the Part of Fortune, according to Ptolemy, is projected in this manner: let the true position of the Sun be subtracted from the true position of the Moon, and what remains...

<meta>This page continues the astrological instructions for interpreting a birth chart, focusing on the calculation and significance of the "Part of Fortune" and shifting into the specific concerns of the Second House, which governs wealth, possessions, and livelihood.</meta>

f iij

It is calculated from the degree of the **Ascendant** <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> and where the count ends, there lies the **Part of Fortune** <note>original: "pars fortune"; a mathematical point in a chart used to predict prosperity and well-being</note>. Ptolemy <note>original: "Ptolemeus"; the 2nd-century scholar whose work formed the basis of medieval astronomy</note> commands that this be done in this way always, whether the birth occurred by day or by night. God willing, this will be discussed more extensively in the explanation of the "fifth distinction," for it has its proper place there.

Afterwards, Ptolemy says the judgment of this matter is of this manner: I mean to say, take the planets that are the governors of the sign. Haly <note>original: "Haly"; likely Haly Abenragel, an 11th-century Arab astrologer</note> says there that the rule by which we know the riches of the newborn is to take the planets governing the place of the Part of Fortune and favor the one who holds more **dignities** <note>original: "dignitates"; the specific strengths a planet gains by being in certain signs or degrees</note> there. 

Then Ptolemy says that when the governors of Fortune are strong and powerful, the newborn will possess great riches, especially if the **two luminaries** <note>original: "duo luminaria"; the Sun and the Moon</note> testify there with matching testimony—that is, by a **trine** or **sextile** aspect <note>Geometric angles of 120 or 60 degrees, considered harmonious and helpful</note>.

Ptolemy [says], you should know that **Saturn** signifies that the newborn's riches will come from women, or through working the land, or by the seas. And **Jupiter** signifies that the riches will come through stewardship <note>original: "senescalia"; the office of a high official or estate manager</note> or through the gifts of good and religious men. And **Mars** signifies that the riches will come from producing soldiers or governing men of arms. And **Venus** signifies that the riches will come through the gifts of friends and women. And **Mercury** signifies that the riches will come through the sciences and commerce.

Haly Abenragel says we must first look at the **fixed stars** <note>original: "stelle fixe"; stars that do not move relative to each other, unlike "wandering" planets</note> that are of the first and second magnitude <note>The brightest and most significant stars</note>. And if we find any of them in the degree of the Ascendant, or in the degree of the **Tenth House**, or in the degree of the **Seventh**, or in the degree of the **Fourth** <note>These four points are known as the "Angles" and are the most powerful areas of a chart</note>, or in the degree of the Sun if the birth was by day, or in the degree of the Moon if the birth was by night—and the best of all these places is the degree of the Ascendant, then the degree of the Tenth House, then the degree of the Seventh, then the Fourth—if these aforementioned stars are in two, three, or more of these places, and if the child was born of royal lineage, he will be an exalted king. And if he was not of royal lineage, he will have power and command similar to the power and command of a king, and will arrive at great nobility and high lordship, and will command various cities and people.

And know that the fixed stars of the "first honor" <note>The most prestigious stars</note> lift a person to a high degree and great dignity, and change them from the lowest state to the highest. Ptolemy says the same in the 29th proposition of the *Centiloquium* <note>original: "centiloquij"; a famous collection of 100 astrological aphorisms</note>: the fixed stars give gifts exceeding all measure, but they often end in misfortune. Haly says in his commentary on that same proposition: when these stars stand alone—that is, without the testimony of **fortunate planets** <note>original: "fortunay planetaruʒ"; "benefic" planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>—their death will be the worst of deaths.

Haly Abenragel says when the Part of Fortune is **cadent** <note>original: "cadẽs"; located in a weak or "falling" house of the chart</note> and in a bad place, or if it is "under the Sun's rays" <note>Weakened by being too close to the Sun's light</note>, or in the rays of an **ill-fortune** <note>original: "infortune"; a "malefic" planet like Saturn or Mars</note>, and its lord <note>The planet ruling the sign where the Part is located</note> is in a **peregrine** place <note>original: "loco peregrino"; a place where a planet has no specific dignity or strength</note>, and the lord of the **Second House** <note>The house of wealth and possessions</note> does not **aspect** <note>Look toward or form a geometric connection with</note> the Ascendant, the newborn will be hard-working and poor, and will live a narrow and miserable life. 

Neither the **Sun**, nor **Mars**, nor **Saturn** is good in the Second House in any birth chart, nor will the newborn have good from it; because the Sun destroys the planets and takes away their light, and Mars is the signifier of obstacles, labors, and lack of substance. Saturn signifies poverty and indigence, and worst of all is if the lord of the house of substance <note>The Second House</note> is **combust** <note>original: "cõbust⁹"; so close to the Sun that its influence is "burned up"</note> along with the affliction of the Part of Fortune.

Abraham Ibn Ezra <note>original: "abrahã auenezre"</note> says if you wish to know which of those born will be poor or rich, look at the lord of the Second House; for if it is fortunate, he will be rich; if it is combust, or **retrograde** <note>appearing to move backward in the sky</note>, or in its **fall** <note>original: "casu"; a position of astrological weakness</note>, he will be poor. Just as this is said regarding births, so should it be understood regarding **questions** <note>original: "questionib⁹"; the branch of astrology that answers specific questions based on the time they are asked</note>.

Regarding **elections** <note>The practice of choosing an auspicious time to start an action</note> made in this house, John of Seville <note>original: "hyspalẽsis"</note> says: if you wish to buy any thing for the sake of profit, see that the **Moon** is in a trine or sextile aspect with the Sun; and take care that it is not "under the light" <note>Too close to the sun's overwhelming brightness</note>...

...of the Sun: and it is better that the lord of the **ascendant** <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> gives power to a planet existing in the tenth or eleventh house: provided that the recipient is not **retrograde** <note>Appearing to move backward in the sky, which usually weakens a planet's influence</note>. If **Jupiter** is in the ascendant, the fourth, tenth, eleventh, or second house, it is good. Look also that the **Part of Fortune** <note>A mathematical point in the chart representing prosperity</note> is in a good place: and it is better if the lord of that Part looks upon it, or the Moon, or the Sun.

## The Third House.

<margin>Here he sets down the meanings of the third house: and he says that the third house signifies brothers and sisters, neighbors, kinsmen, and loved ones: and</margin>

It signifies brothers and sisters, neighbors, kinsmen, and loved ones: and it signifies faith and religion, commands and embassies, changes: and short journeys. It also signifies the state of life before death. And Ansduzgaʒ <note>likely Andruzagar, a 7th-century Persian astrologer</note> says that the first lord of the **triplicity** <note>The three planets that rule the zodiac signs of the same element</note> of this third house signifies older brothers. The second signifies middle brothers. The third signifies younger ones: and their status and dignity are judged according to the status of these lords. 

Haly Abenragel <note>An 11th-century Arab astrologer whose work was central to medieval European practice</note> says when the Sun is the lord of the third house from the ascendant and has **combusted** <note>original: "combusserit"; when a planet is so close to the Sun that its power is "burned up" or hidden</note> the lord of the second house or the "Part of Substance" <note>A point used to calculate wealth</note>, it signifies that the newborn will lose his substance because of his brothers: and he will pay for them taxes, losses, and many damages. When the lord of the third house and the "Part of Brothers" or Jupiter are in **common signs** <note>original: "signis communibus"; signs like Gemini or Pisces that suggest multiplicity</note> or signs of many children, and the **fortunes** <note>benefic planets like Venus or Jupiter</note> look upon them with an aspect of friendship <note>such as a sextile or trine aspect</note> and with **reception** <note>A condition where planets are in each other's signs, indicating mutual support</note>: it signifies that the newborn is the firstborn among his brothers: and that he will have powerful and good brothers. 

If the lord of the house of brothers is combust, or the Part of Brothers is combust, or if the Sun is in the house of brothers: or in opposition to Jupiter: these are signs of few brothers and that they will be destroyed and scattered. When the lord of the house of brothers is **unfortunate** <note>Afflicted by "malefic" planets like Saturn or Mars</note> above the earth: it signifies that damage and trouble will come to the brothers who were born before him. And if he has this misfortune while he is below the earth, the evil and damage will come to the brothers who are born after him. And you shall say the same regarding good and fortune when the planet is well-placed in good and strong locations. 

If the lord of the house of brothers is in the ascendant: it signifies the newborn is the firstborn: or he will be alone without ever having brothers or sisters: or when there is no planet between the ascendant and the **Midheaven** <note>original: "medium celi"; the highest point in the sky</note>, it similarly signifies that the newborn is the firstborn. And if any brothers are born after him, they will be lost. And if by chance any of them remains: the firstborn will nevertheless always be greater and better. 

When in any **solar return** <note>original: "reuolutione annorum"; a chart cast for the year ahead on one's birthday</note> the lord of the third house is in the tenth house from the birth ascendant, one of his brothers will die in that year. Similarly, if the lord of the third house of the original birth chart <note>original: "radicis"</note> is in the tenth house of the solar return: this is because the tenth house from the ascendant is the eighth house from the third house <note>The eighth house signifies death; in the "derived house" system, counting eight houses starting from the third house reaches the tenth house of the main chart</note>. 

If someone asks you about the state of his brother, look at the third sign from the ascendant, which is the house of brothers, and the lord of its triplicity and the lord of that house, and which of the fortunes or misfortunes looks upon it and in what place it is: for if you find the lord of the third house in the sixth house <note>The house of illness</note> or **applying** <note>Moving toward a geometric relationship</note> to the lord of the sixth house: or the lord of the sixth in the third: say that his brother is sick. And if you find him in the fifth or the eleventh, say that his brother is not in his place: because he went away from his own place. And if you find the lord of the third house unfortunate, or in the twelfth house, say...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Third House, specifically focusing on the health of siblings and travel conditions, before moving into the detailed significations of the Fourth House, which governs fathers, land, and the end of things.</meta>

...that his brother is in a state of anxiety or infirmity. And if you find the lord of the third house together with Mars, both joined and entering **combustion** <note>original: "combustionē"; a state where a planet is so close to the Sun that its influence is "burned up" or weakened</note> under the rays of the Sun, you should say that he will not escape from the infirmity. And if both were combusted, judge by all means that death follows. And you should inspect in this manner if you are asked about things pertaining to other houses, such as regarding a father, a son, or a wife, etc. Hispalensis <note>John of Seville, a major 12th-century translator of Arabic astrology into Latin</note> says that if you wish to begin a first journey, ensure that a fortunate planet looks upon the third house or its lord, provided that no malefic planet is there, nor in its fourth aspect <note>a square aspect, usually challenging</note> or its opposite aspect. If the Moon is in the third house, it is good. Cancer, Leo, and Capricorn are bad signs for a short journey. The Moon in the **ascendant** <note>the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> or in the fourth house is bad for every **election** <note>the practice of choosing an auspicious time to start an undertaking</note>. One must beware lest the Moon be in the **combust way** <note>original: "via cōbusta"; a specific stretch of the zodiac considered unlucky, from 19° Libra to 3° Scorpio</note>. One must also beware lest the Moon be near the **Head or Tail of the Dragon** <note>the North and South Nodes of the Moon</note> within 12 degrees.

# The Fourth House.

<margin>Here he sets down the meanings of the fourth house. And he says that the fourth house signifies properties, inheritances, and the ends of things; it signifies treasures and all occult and hidden things; and it signifies the end of life. And Andruzagar <note>a 7th-century Persian astrologer</note> says the first lord of the **triplicity** <note>the three planets ruling the signs of the same element</note> of the fourth house signifies fathers. The second signifies cities and lands. The third signifies the end of things and prisons. Haly Abenragel <note>An 11th-century Arab astrologer</note> says: Look at the state of the father from the lords of the triplicity of the fourth house.</margin>

If the first lord of the triplicity of the fourth house is in its own house or in its **exaltation** <note>the zodiac sign where a planet exerts its strongest influence</note>; or in its **haiz** <note>a specific technical condition where a planet is in its preferred gender and solar/nocturnal environment</note> received by the Sun or Jupiter, and is in the high place of the **Midheaven** <note>original: "medij celi"; the highest point of the sky</note> or in the fifth house, it signifies the good status of the father: nobility, high standing, riches, power, long life, and good fortune. This is true if the sign in which this planet was located was masculine; however, if the sign itself was feminine, it signifies for the mother those things we have mentioned, especially if the planet was feminine. What **haiz** is, he will explain later, God willing. And if the second lord of the triplicity of the fourth house is fortunate, the father will possess lands, populate them, labor in them, and will have good utility or profit from this. And if these planets are in a diverse or contrary state, judge the opposite. And if the third lord of the triplicity of the fourth house is strong and fortunate as we said before: the father will be a man who extracts treasures, prunes vineyards, and prepares precious stones. And if he is in a state different from what we said, he will be a stone-cutter or will labor in bad and stinking materials. If the Sun is present in daytime births, or Saturn in nocturnal ones, and the **Lot of the Father** <note>original: "pars pŕis"; a mathematically calculated point in the horoscope</note> likewise aspects the ascendant or its lord from the fourth or opposite aspect, it signifies that the father will abhor the native son, hate him, and see to it that he is killed. And if the Moon and the **Lot of the Mother** aspect the ascendant or its lord, that abhorrence and malevolence toward the son will come from the mother. Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of hermetic wisdom</note> said the fourth house is the "pit of the planets" because it is at the bottom of the circle of the heavens and of infirmity: it is in a **trine** aspect <note>a harmonious 120-degree angle</note> to the 12th house, which is the house of darkness, labors, anxieties, and prisons: and it is the "joy" of the **Greater Malefic** <note>Saturn</note>: and it is in a **sextile** aspect <note>a 60-degree angle</note> to the sixth house, which is the dark house of infirmity and loss, and the "joy" of the **Lesser Malefic** <note>Mars</note>. When any planet is in the fourth house and in its **fall** <note>original: "casu"; the sign where it is weakest</note>, it is like a man suffocated in solitude until he leaves there. Haly Abenragel says when the **Lot of Fortune** <note>original: "pars fortuune"</note> is unfortunate in a masculine sign, judge that the father will die before the mother. If in a feminine sign, the mother will die before the father. The Lot of the Father is taken by day from the Sun to Saturn: and by night...

<meta>This page concludes the calculation of the Lots (Arabic Parts) for parents from the Fourth House and then transitions into the Fifth House, which governs children and pleasures. and conversely, and it is projected from the **ascendant** <note>the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note>. The **Part of the Mother** <note>original: "Pars matris"; a mathematical point in a chart used to find specific information</note> is taken by day from Venus to the Moon, and by night conversely, and is projected from the ascendant. If the lord of the **Part of the Father** is above the earth and the lord of the Part of the Mother is below the earth, judge that the mother will die before the father. And if it should be the opposite, judge the opposite. **Haly Abenragel** <note>Ali ibn Abi al-Rijal, a famous 11th-century Arab astrologer</note> says that Albezen son of Alhezib said: One day when he was with Abolabem and Meraeso was with him, a certain old man came to me and gave me a paper in which was written the figure of a **nativity** <note>a birth chart</note>. And he told me that I should look into that same figure; and I asked him whose figure or birth it was. He responded that it was of a certain son of his. And while I was inspecting it and thinking about it, Abolabem took it from my hand and looked at it for a little while and said: "The first thing we see in this birth is that this child was born of adultery, because he is not the son of the one who says he is his father." I said, "From where do you have this?" and he responded: "This old man says that this birth is of his son; but the father of the child to whom this birth belongs has already been dead for four years, and he died in the same year in which the child was born." And I asked how he had this. And he said: "I looked at the degree of the father and found it in the opposite of the mother, and there was not more than one degree between them. And I found the **Part of the Father** in the 11th house from the ascendant—which is the eighth and the fourth <note>meaning the 8th house (death) relative to the 4th house (father)</note>—and there was one degree between the Part and Saturn, because of which I judged that the father of this child died in the year the child was born. And this old man says that he is his son, therefore the child is born of adultery." And then the old man said: "I am not the father of this child, but he is the son of my son; and my son, his father, died in the same year of this birth, just as this good man said."

## The Fifth House.
<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'Q' featuring a central flower surrounded by stylized foliage inside a square frame.</image-desc>
—signifies children, both male and female. And it signifies delights, and envoys or messengers, and gifts. And what shall be after death, whether of praise or blame. And **Anduzgaz** <note>likely Andruzagar, an early Persian astrologer</note> said that the first **lord of the triplicity** <note>a planet that rules a group of three zodiac signs of the same element</note> of the house of children signifies children and their life. The second designates delights. The third signifies envoys or messengers. **Ptolemy** says regarding the reasons of this house, it is fitting that we look to the planets existing in the place of the **zenith** <note>original: "zenith capitum"; the point directly overhead</note> and to the place which follows it—which is the place of **fortune** <note>the 11th house</note>—and to the place participating with them in the figure. **Haly** <note>likely Ali ibn Ridwan, a commentator on Ptolemy</note> says in his commentary: the place which is the zenith of our heads is the 10th house, and the place which follows it is the 11th house, which is the house of fortune. And the rule by which we shall know regarding children is taken from the planets existing in these places, or existing in places participating with them, such as the ascendant and the 7th house, because each of them participates with the 10th and 11th. And if we do not find a planet in these places, we shall look if we find any in the 4th house or the 5th, and we shall take the signification from there. Ptolemy says we shall judge by the Moon, Venus, and Jupiter in the giving of children. The Sun, Mars, and Saturn in the taking away of them; and we shall make Mercury a participant with whichever of them he shares a figure with. Haly says in the commentary: If there should be any of the **fortunate planets** <note>benefics like Jupiter or Venus</note> in the aforesaid places, it signifies that he will have children. If any of the **unfortunate planets** <note>malefics like Saturn or Mars</note>, he will have no child, and if he should have them, they will be weak and few. And if Mercury has participation with the fortunes, he will be a fortune; if with the infortunes, he will be an infortune. Ptolemy says, and it is proposition 144 of the fourth part...
