...you will be able to know the particulars of children through consideration, when you have well-inspected the planet giving children for any of them, so that you place it in the position of the **ascendant** <note>the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, and you will know the other particulars of the children generally, just as you would through a **nativity** <note>a birth chart</note>. **Haly** <note>Ali ibn Abi al-Rijal, 11th-century court astrologer</note> says in his commentary: Moreover, what **Ptolemy** <note>the 2nd-century foundational authority on astrology</note> wishes to recount in this place is that which happened in my own nativity. I inspected it and found **Jupiter** in the tenth house and in the 28th degree of **Capricorn**, aspecting itself with the **Moon** and **Saturn**; and this signified that I would have children, but that they would die, because Saturn was with Jupiter in the 10th house. And because Jupiter was in a feminine sign and the Moon was similarly placed, it signified that there would be more than one child and that they would more likely be female. And because Jupiter was **oriental** <note>rising before the Sun, considered a more "masculine" or active position</note>, it signified that I would have a male. And thus I had three children: one male and two females, and they all died. The degree of the ascendant of the male's nativity was near the degree of Saturn in my own nativity, and because of this, he died quickly. And the degree of the ascendant of one female was in **Pisces**, in **sextile** <note>an aspect of 60 degrees, usually considered harmonious</note> to the Jupiter of my nativity and in **trine** <note>an aspect of 120 degrees, considered very favorable</note> to the Moon of my nativity; and the **Sun** was the **hylech** <note>term: hylech; the planet or point in a chart that acts as the "giver of life" or determines the lifespan</note> in the sign of Capricorn. And the degree of the ascendant of the other female was in Capricorn. And this is wonderful: for because of this, it can be known that nativities always accord with one another. **Hyspalensis** <note>John of Seville, a prolific 12th-century translator of Arabic astrology into Latin</note> says, wishing to choose the hour for conceiving a male child: let Jupiter be in any **angle** <note>the four most powerful houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note> or let it be oriental to the Sun, and let the **Lord of the Hour** be male, and it is better if the ascending sign and its Lord be masculine. And if the Moon is made fortunate by Jupiter, it will be better. For a female, take feminine signs and feminine planets, and let Jupiter be **occidental** <note>setting after the Sun</note>. **Haly Abenragel** says when any **Fortune** <note>a beneficial planet, usually Jupiter or Venus</note> is in the **fifth house** and the Lord of the fifth house is free from **misfortunes** <note>afflictions from Saturn or Mars</note> and aspects the **Midheaven** <note>the highest point of the chart</note>, the native will have many children and they will live and come to good. And if it should be the opposite, it signifies the opposite. Look in all nativities: if you find the **luminaries** <note>the Sun and Moon</note> unfortunate and having no aspect from the Fortunes, the native will have few children.

¶ Regarding knowing the time in which they will have children, look to the planet which has more **dignities** <note>astrological "rights" or power over a sign, such as being its ruler</note> in the house of children. If you find it in the oriental part, he will have children in his youth. If you find it in the Midheaven, he will have them in the middle of his age. And if in the seventh, he will have them at the beginning of old age. If in the fourth, he will have them toward the end of his life. Similarly, when the year arrives at the sign in which Jupiter or Venus was in the root of the nativity, he will have a child in 그 year. **Haly Abenragel** says if one is asked by a woman whether she is pregnant or not, look if you find both the Lord of the Ascendant and the Moon in the house of children; or if you find the Lord of the house of children in the ascendant free from misfortunes: you may say that the woman is pregnant. And if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the house of children gives its power to a planet existing in an angle; and similarly if the Lord of the Ascendant is **received** <note>a condition where planets occupy each other's home signs</note> and the one receiving it is also received, say that she is pregnant. And if the Lord of the ascendant gives its power to a planet **falling from an angle** <note>occupying a "cadent" house, which is considered weaker</note>, it signifies that she is not pregnant.

# The Sixth House.

<margin>Here he places the significations of the 6th house.</margin>
The Sixth House. <gloss>He says the sixth house signifies illnesses and</gloss>
servants, and it signifies the end of life and what is to be before old age. **Aduzgaz** <note>an unidentified or corrupted name of an Arabic authority</note> said that the first Lord of the **triplicity** <note>term: triplicity; a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water)</note> of this house signifies illnesses and
convalescences or the worsening of illnesses. The second signifies **vernaculars** and servants. ¶ Note the difference between a "vernacular" and a "servant." For a vernacular is the same as a household attendant and is called a "brought-in servant." A servant is one who is of a servile condition. Whence he said as a verse...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Sixth House, moving from the specific lords of the triplicity to the broader application of astrology in medicine, specifically regarding "critical days" or the "Albohara." It maintains the focus on the Sixth House as the domain of illness and servants.</meta>

...indicator. There is a proverb here: **"This is a servant for whom the tavern is not dear."** <note>original Latin: "Est probō hic verna cui nō est cara taberna." This rhyming phrase describes a sober and diligent domestic worker.</note> The third lord of the triplicity of the **sixth house** signifies what of good or evil happens to the subject from servants, as well as their usefulness and labor.

And along with this, he is the indicator of beasts, cattle, and all quadrupeds that are not ridden <note>such as sheep, goats, or pigs, as opposed to horses which belong to the Twelfth House</note>, and he signifies their multitude or scarcity, their strength, and how long they will remain in the subject's possession or when they will depart from his hands. He also signifies prisons and detentions.

**Ptolemy** <note>the 2nd-century authority on astronomy and astrology</note> says in the 60th proposition of the *Centiloquium* <note>a collection of 100 astrological aphorisms</note>: The **Albohara** <note>from the Arabic "al-buhran," meaning the "crisis" or turning point of a disease</note> are truly and certainly the hours in which changes of diseases toward good or evil are swiftly declared, according to the positions of the **Moon** in the angles of a square enclosed by the direct circle. However, the alterations that precede these and indicate them are the true positions of the Moon in the angles of the **almuten** <note>the planet with the most combined dignity and power in a specific chart location</note>. Those which nevertheless precede these are the positions of the Moon in angles having 16 sides. 

And therefore, after the state of the sickness has proceeded according to its equilibrium and nothing external has occurred to disturb the sick person, when we find a **fortune** <note>a "benefic" or lucky planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> among either the fixed or wandering stars [planets], it will signify a prosperous change. If, however, we find a **misfortune** <note>a "malefic" or unlucky planet like Saturn or Mars</note>, it signifies an adverse change—unless that misfortune is contrary to the sickness itself and is in its own **hayz** <note>an astrological condition where a planet is in a sign and "sect" (day or night) that matches its nature, making it more constructive</note>.

The Moon in these angles signifies **acute** <note>short and intense</note> diseases, while the **Sun** signifies prolonged ones. And similarly, every planet acts according to its own nature. **Haly** <note>Ali ibn Abi al-Rijal, an 11th-century commentator</note> in his commentary on the same work says that Ptolemy taught us the cause of **determinable days** <note>critical days of a disease's progression</note>, what they are, and when they determine good or evil, and the changes according to their order. 

The usefulness in this is greater for physicians than for astrologers. This is because many philosophers agree on these determinations, saying that there are "changes" and "battles" between the **Nature** <note>the body's internal healing force</note> of the sick person and the acute disease on the 4th day, 7th day, 14th day, and 21st day. These things are often increased according to what is received by mere hearsay and doctrine without true science, and they do not reason in this except through their past experiences. 

But Ptolemy stated the cause for it and revealed whence it arises. The cause, according to Ptolemy, is that **Nature** is not conquered by the disease at its onset, yet the disease prevents her from carrying out her functions in equilibrium; nor does she fight back against the disease in that hour because the disease prevails. Instead, she waits until the Moon is in a place contrary to where it was at the beginning of the illness; for then the force of the **humors** <note>the four bodily fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—which were believed to govern health</note> moving within the patient will not be as it was at the start. 

For then she is stirred to resist it, just as a prudent plaintiff does not act against a foolish defendant when he wishes to resist him, unless the defendant's strength has been weakened and he is in a place where he lacks helpers. And it excites her—that is, Nature—in the fourth sign from the sign in which the illness began, when the Moon is in it; because from any sign, the fourth is contrary to its nature, and similarly the seventh. This is because these places—that is, the **fourth aspect** <note>a "square," or 90-degree angle</note> and the **opposite** <note>an "opposition," or 180-degree angle</note>—signify enmities and oppositions. 

And the time between the start of the illness and the entry of the Moon into the degree of the square in which it was at the beginning—calculated by the "ascensions of the signs" in the direct circle and the "projection of rays"—is seven days. In the same way, the time between the Moon at the start of the illness and its position in opposition is 14 days; and the time between the start and the second fourth aspect, which is contrary to the start of the illness, is 21 days. 

According to this method proceeds the calculation of the other days. These points will be the angles of the square which the line of the direct circle encloses; and besides those points, other points are indicative of the crisis: specifically, when the Moon reaches the point that divides each arc of the square exactly in half. Within these arcs are the sides of a figure having eight angles <note>an octagon, representing 45-degree angles or "semi-squares"</note>. 

All these points are naturally contrary to the points in which the disease began. Therefore, let the astrologer observe what may be in these points, regarding both fixed stars and wandering stars, whether they are "fortunes" or "misfortunes." If they are fortunes, let him judge that they signify the victory of Nature over the **bile** <note>original: "colera", referring to the choleric humor</note>. If misfortunes...

It also concludes the discussion on the Sixth House's influence on servants and small livestock.</summary>

--- Page 99 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of medical astrology and the "Albohara" (critical days). It transitions from the general timing of crises to the specific influence of planetary aspects, the nature of "misfortunes" acting as remedies, and specific astrological configurations that cause blindness.</meta>

Truly, they signify that the planets will hope to heal, unless there is a **malefic** <note>original: "infortuna," referring to Saturn or Mars</note> contrary to the disease while it is strong in its **Hayz** <term>Hayz: An astrological dignity where a planet is in a sign and house of its own gender and in its preferred time of day (day or night)</term>. For the Moon will not be made "fortunate" simply by being in its own **Hayz**, but rather it will remove the disease because it is contrary to it. A "misfortune" acts against a disease—for example, if the planet is cold and the disease is hot, or if the planet is hot and the disease is cold, and in similar types of opposites. 

He says that the position of the **Sun** in long-lasting <note>chronic</note> diseases is like the position of the **Moon** in acute diseases; the major timing of the latter belongs to the sphere of the Moon, while the former belongs mostly to the sphere of the Sun. **Ptolemy** also hints at a subtle point when he says: "every star according to its character." He taught us that whatever is immoderate within us and appears in characters or behaviors we are not accustomed to, he defines as a "disease." He said this is correct unless something external occurs to disturb the order of the diagnosis. Those who have commented on **Hippocrates'** *Book of Aphorisms* have spoken regarding things that destroy a diagnosis, and therefore it is not necessary for us to repeat them in this book. 

The entire intention of **Ptolemy** and **Haly** <note>Ali ibn Ridwan, the 11th-century commentator on Ptolemy</note> rests on this: the reason why the 7th, 14th, and 21st days are **critical days** <note>original: "dies cretici," the turning points of an illness</note> is because on the seventh day, the Moon frequently arrives at the **square aspect** <note>an angle of 90 degrees</note> from the place it occupied at the beginning of the illness. On the 14th day, the Moon frequently reaches the opposite position <note>180 degrees</note>, and on the 21st day, it reaches the second square aspect <note>270 degrees</note>. 

Therefore, **Haly** says that when an astrologer wishes to judge a **critical day**, he must observe the time when the Moon arrives at the square aspect of the position it held at the start of the illness. If it then joins with benevolent planets, or looks upon them with a favorable aspect, or if it joins or looks upon **fixed stars** that are of a "fortunate" nature, it signifies that the **crisis** will result in recovery. 

If it joins or looks upon **malefics**, it signifies the opposite, unless those malefics are contrary to the "matter" of the disease, and the malefic is also in its **Hayz**; in such a case, it can signify something good, but this happens "by accident" <note>meaning as a secondary effect of the planetary conflict, rather than a direct blessing</note>. For example: if the sickness is **phlegmatic** <note>cold and moist</note> and the Moon looks upon **Mars** <note>hot and dry</note>, it signifies a good outcome. 

When the Moon reaches the "good place" which spans the side of an **octagon** <note>45 degrees</note> in the equator, that will be an **indicative day**. The side of an octagon is 45 degrees, which is half of a square aspect. Although it is not a full "aspect," it has a greater agreement with the aspect than other positions. Therefore, when the Moon is meant to join with the "fortunes" in the square aspect, nature already feels relief when it reaches the halfway point; it begins to rise up against the matter of the disease by "digesting" it, and thus signs of digestion appear in the urine. 

Sometimes a **crisis** occurs on the 4th day—namely, when the Moon finds a "fortunate" planet there. In that case, signs of digestion should precede at the time when the Moon reaches the position of the side of a 16-sided figure, which is half of an octagon <note>22.5 degrees</note>. Let these remarks suffice for the causes of critical days.

**Ptolemy** says in the 70th proposition of the *Centiloquium*: when the **Moon** is in opposition to the **Sun** and mixed with "nebulous" stars <note>star clusters that appear cloudy or dim</note>, it signifies inseparable diseases in the eyes. Similarly, if the Moon is in the west and in an "angle" <note>one of the four main pillars of the chart</note>, and both "evils" <note>Saturn and Mars</note> are in the west ascending after the Moon, and the Sun is in an angle and both evils are ascending before the Sun and opposite to each other, the person born will lose both eyes.

¶ **Haly** says in his commentary on the same: the greatest impediment to the two **Luminaries** <note>the Sun and Moon</note> is when there are two "evils"—that is, **Saturn** and **Mars**—ascending before the Sun and after the Moon, with one of them in opposition to the other. The Sun signifies the right eye, while the Moon signifies the left. It is inevitable that the person born will lose both eyes when...

It also provides specific planetary configurations that the authors believe result in permanent blindness.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of medical astrology, moving from general planetary influences to the specific significations of the Sixth House (illness) and the Seventh House (partnerships/oppositions). It maintains the heavy reliance on the authorities Haly Abenragel and Ptolemy.</meta>

...significator in his birth in this way. Truly, the nebulous stars are these: the Achorae <note>original: "achoraye"; these are star clusters believed to cause dim sight</note>, the head of Gemini, the place where the water falls that Aquarius pours, the "drops of Leo" <note>likely stars in the constellation of Leo associated with moisture or eye trouble</note>, and others that do not shine brightly. 

**Haly Abenragel** <note>An 11th-century Arab astrologer whose work was a primary textbook in Medieval Europe</note> says regarding the reasons of this house <note>the Sixth House, which governs illness</note> that we first wish to speak in this chapter about the circumstances and accidents that occur in the spirit, which are infirmities of the spirit. Afterward, we will speak of those things that happen in the body, which are infirmities of the body. Next, he says: I say that **demoniacs** <note>original: "demoniaci"; in a medieval medical context, this refers to those suffering from epilepsy, psychosis, or severe mental disturbances thought to have spiritual or planetary causes</note> are those who do not have **Mercury** in any aspect with the **Moon** in their births, with neither of them "looking toward" <note>forming an astrological aspect to</note> the Ascendant. Furthermore, the stronger planet in the birth—if it was by day, let it be **Saturn**, and if by night, **Mars**—whichever of them it is, let it be in the **angles** <term>Angles: The four most powerful houses in a chart: the 1st (Ascendant), 4th, 7th, and 10th</term>. This is the nativity of "demons."

When the **Sun** is afflicted by the **malefics** <note>Saturn or Mars</note> or by the Lord of the House of Infirmity <note>the 6th house</note>, and it is above the earth in a daytime birth, or if the **Moon** is in such an affliction below the earth in a nighttime birth, blindness in the **right eye** will happen to the person born. And if this affliction of the Sun occurs below the earth in a nighttime birth, or above the earth for the Moon in a daytime birth, that damage and blindness will be in the **left eye**. When the Sun and the Moon are both afflicted in the **Sixth House**, the person born will, without any doubt, be blinded. 

When **Mercury** is joined with **Saturn**, or in a square or opposite aspect to it; or if it is falling from an angle in a base and unfortunate place; or if it is **combust** <term>Combust: Weakened by being within 8.5 degrees of the Sun</term> or retrograde; or **peregrine** <term>Peregrine: A planet lacking any essential dignity or "home" in its current sign</term> in a **mute sign** <note>Signs like Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces, which were thought to hinder speech</note>, the person born will be afflicted in the tongue or mute because of any of these significations. When Mercury is the Lord of the Sixth House, or in opposition to Saturn, the person born will be deaf. The greatest and strongest indication of muteness is when the Lord of the Ascendant, the Lord of its exaltation, the Lord of its triplicity, Mercury, and the Moon are all in mute signs. 

When the **Sun** is afflicted or damaged below the earth, it signifies an affliction of the **stomach**. When the **Moon** is damaged below the earth, it signifies an affliction of the **lungs**. And if **Mars** is in this condition, it signifies an affliction of the **liver**. And if **Saturn** is in this condition, it signifies an affliction of the **spleen**. And if **Mercury** is in this condition, it signifies an affliction of the **gall**. 

**Ptolemy** says in the 89th proposition of the *Centiloquy* <note>A famous collection of 100 astrological aphorisms</note> that the most detestable sign regarding a sick person is for the significator of the inquiry to be entering "under the rays" <note>hidden by the Sun's light</note> or for the **Part of Fortune** <note>A calculated point in a chart representing health and prosperity</note> to be afflicted. **Haly Abenragel** says regarding inquiries of this house: if you are asked on behalf of a sick person whether they will be healed or not, look at the **Moon**, the **Sun**, and the **Almuten** <term>Almuten: The planet that has the most combined power (dignity) over a specific degree of the zodiac</term> of the Ascendant. If they are free from the malefics and have no aspect with the Lord of the House of Death <note>the 8th house</note>, say that they will escape that infirmity. And if two of them are as we have said, they will likewise escape. 

But if the Moon and the Almuten of the Ascendant—especially if the Moon is the "Lady of the Time" (that is, if the inquiry is made at night)—have an aspect with the Lord of the House of Death, the person cannot be delivered from death. And every time any of the significators **applies to** <term>Applies: Moving toward an exact mathematical aspect with another planet</term> a malefic, the disease will be strengthened until the planet separates from it. The time of death will be when the Almuten of the Ascendant or the Moon joins with the Lord of the House of Death, or with the malefic that afflicted them, or when it applies to a square or opposite aspect with it.

# The Seventh House.

## Here he sets down the significations of the **Seventh House**.
## And he says that the Seventh House signifies
## women, marriages, agreements, and oppositions. Anduzgam <note>original: "Anduzgam," a term of Persian origin likely referring to the "Lot of Marriage" or specific marriage configurations</note>

<summary>This page details how specific planetary placements, particularly involving Mercury and the Moon, signify physical and mental disabilities such as "demonic" possession, blindness, and muteness. It also provides a method for predicting whether a sick person will recover or die based on the relationship between the planets and the House of Death.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Seventh House, which in medieval astrology governs marriage, partnerships, open enemies, and legal disputes. It transitions from the general nature of the house to specific techniques for predicting the timing and quality of a spouse based on the works of Ptolemy and Haly Abenragel.</meta>

It was said that the first lord of the **triplicity** <term>Triplicity: A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water). Each triplicity has three "lords" or planetary rulers that govern different aspects of life.</term> of the **seventh house** signifies women; the second signifies contentions; the third signifies participations and mixtures. **Ptolemy** <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century author of the *Tetrabiblos*, the foundational text for Western astrology</note> says that regarding the logic of this house, it is fitting that we look at the state of the **Moon** at the hour of a man’s birth to judge the marriage of men. First, we look at the Moon: if she was in the two **oriental quarters** <note>The quarters of the chart between the Ascendant and Midheaven, or Descendant and Icum Coeli; here referring to the Moon's phase or position relative to the Sun</note>, the man's marriage will occur in his youth, or he will join with a young girl after he has matured. And if she was in the two **occidental quarters**, the marriage will be delayed, or he will join with an old woman. 

And if the Moon was **under the rays** <term>Under the rays: A planet is "under the rays" (or combust) when it is so close to the Sun that it is hidden by its light, which was usually considered a sign of weakness or secrecy.</term> and was participating with **Saturn**, he will never marry. And if she **applies** <term>Application: When a faster-moving planet approaches an aspect (a specific angle) with a slower planet, indicating an event about to happen.</term> to **Saturn**, he will join with a woman who is hardworking and wicked. And if she applies to **Jupiter**, the woman will be of good restraint and fine character. And if she applies to **Mars**, she will be bold and proud. And if she applies to **Venus**, she will be beautiful, joyful, and of a pleasant welcome. And if she applies to **Mercury**, she will be good, intelligent, and a woman of fine words.

And it is fitting that we look at the marriage of women from the state of the **Sun** at the hour of her birth. Thus, if the Sun was in the two oriental quarters, the marriage of a woman having such a birth will be in her youth, or she will join with a youth after she has matured. And if it was in the two occidental quarters, she will marry late or join with an old man after time has passed. And if the Sun was participating with **Saturn**, the husband will be a man of the law and a lover of labor. And if he had participation with **Jupiter**, he will be of good restraint and noble heart. If with **Mars**, he will be of strong heart but without any love. If with **Venus**, he will be pure. If with **Mercury**, he will be effective in his deeds and a maker of many things.

**Haly Abenragel** <note>Abu l-Hasan 'Ali ibn Abi l-Rijal, an 11th-century court astrologer to the Zirid prince Al-Mu'izz ibn Badis</note> says concerning the **questions** <note>original: "quaestionibus"; this refers to Horary astrology, where a chart is cast for the moment a specific question is asked</note> of this house: If someone should ask you regarding a marriage, whether it will happen or not, look to the **Ascendant**, its lord, the **Moon**, and the planet from which she has just separated; give these as the **significators** <term>Significator: The planet or house representing a specific person or topic in an astrological chart.</term> of the one asking (the querent). Look to the seventh house, its lord, and the planet to which the Moon is applying as the significators of the one being asked about. 

And if the querent is male, join the **Sun** with his significators and let it share in the meaning; and if the querent is a woman, give her **Venus** and let her share in the meaning. Afterward, look at what kind of application the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon has with the Lord of the seventh house, and what kind of application the planet from which the Moon separated has with the planet to which she applies—or Venus with the Sun. Wherefore, if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon applies to the Lord of the seventh house, or the planet from which the Moon separated applies to the planet she is approaching, or if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon is located in the seventh house, it signifies that the querent will have the thing sought, but only with many petitions and prayers. 

And if the application was by a **square** <note>original: "quarto"; an aspect of 90 degrees</note> or **opposition** <note>180 degrees</note>, it signifies that this thing will happen only with delay, labor, and pain. But if the Lord of the seventh house applies to the Lord of the Ascendant, or the planet to which the Moon applies joins the planet from which she separated, or if the Lord of the seventh is in the Ascendant, that thing shall be done easily and with the great will of the woman and her party—especially if that application was by a **trine** <note>original: "tertio"; 120 degrees</note> or **sextile** <note>60 degrees</note>. And if you find no application between the significators, it signifies that the matter will not come to pass. 

If someone asks you if on that night he will fulfill his desire with a woman or not, look to see if you find **Venus** in the question aspecting the Ascendant; say that his question [his desire] will be fulfilled that night. And if the question was whether he had the thing with the woman on the preceding night or not, judge this in the same way, neither more nor less. 

If they ask you regarding any woman whether she is a **virgin** or not, look at the Ascendant, its lord, and the Moon; if you find them in **fixed signs** <note>Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius, or Taurus</note>, say that she is a virgin, safe from all stain and pure. And if they were in **common** <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces</note> or **movable signs** <note>Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn</note>, say that she is a woman [non-virgin] and has or had a husband. And...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Seventh House (marriage and partnerships) before moving into a detailed exploration of the Eighth House, which governs death, inheritance, and fear.</meta>

If a question should arise concerning a girl who says she is a virgin: say that she is corrupted, and that someone has had a matter <note>referring to sexual intercourse</note> with her. If you are asked concerning any woman how many husbands she will have, look from the **Lord of the Tenth** <term>Lord of the Tenth: The planet that rules the sign on the cusp of the tenth house (representing status or public life).</term> to the **degree of Mars**; however many planets you find between them, so many husbands she shall have. And if **Mars** is in the **seventh house**, look from Mars to **Jupiter** in the same manner and judge accordingly, and you will find certainty through God.

<margin>
Here he sets down the meanings of the eighth house. And he says that the eighth house is the house of death and fear, and it signifies the inheritances of the dead which heirs ought to possess after death; it also signifies the end of the years of life after old age.
</margin>

# The Eighth House.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'O' featuring a stylized floral or leafy pattern inside a square border.</image-desc>

The **eighth house** is the house of death and fear, and it signifies the inheritances of the dead which heirs ought to possess after death; it also signifies the end of the years of life after old age. **Anduzgam** <note>likely a reference to a Persian or Arabic astrological source, possibly "Andarzaghar"</note> says the first lord of the **triplicity** <term>Triplicity: A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element; each triplicity is governed by three "lords" or planetary rulers.</term> of this house signifies fear. The second signifies commands and ancient things. The third signifies the inheritances of the dead. **Ptolemy** says it is fitting that you look to the state of the degree of the "interfector" <note>The "killing" or "anaeretic" degree, used to calculate the time and cause of death</note>, seeing which of the planets is there or is looking upon it <note>forming an "aspect" or specific angle to it</note>: and according to the nature of that planet shall be the judgment concerning death. 

Thus, if **Saturn** is the lord of death, it will happen because of long illnesses: specifically because of **consumption** <note>original: "ptisim" (phthisis), a wasting disease like tuberculosis</note> and **rheumatism** <note>original: "reumata", historically meaning an excess of bodily fluids or catarrh</note>, and through a defect of nature, and so forth. And if **Jupiter** is the lord of death, death will be due to **abscesses** <note>original: "apostemata"</note> of the throat and lungs and such things. And if it is **Mars**, death will be due to continuous and acute fevers. And if it is **Venus**, death will happen because of illness of the stomach, liver, and heart, and through the flux of blood, and sores spreading through the body and **fistulas** <term>Fistula: An abnormal, ulcerous passage or opening in the body.</term>. And if it is **Mercury**, death will be because of **epilepsy** <note>original: "epilentiam"</note> and because of folly and the loss of the senses. 

**Haly** <note>Haly Abenragel, an 11th-century Arabic astrologer</note> says in the commentary on the 18th proposition of the fourth part: Ptolemy divided death for us into two manners: one is arriving from illnesses. The other is arriving from a matter in which a physician has nothing to see <note>i.e., external or violent causes</note>: such as one who dies by the sword, a spear, a fall, fire, the bite of a beast or wild animal, or by drowning in water and many other occasions. And when the planets that are the governors are safe from misfortunes and in their own **dignities** <term>Dignity: A sign or position where a planet is particularly strong or "at home."</term> and powerful, and when no contrary planet is elevated above them, then death happens due to illnesses. However, the other kind of death happens when the planets are unfortunate or weak, or in the dignities of others that are contrary to them, or when contrary planets are elevated above them. 

¶ To recount all the manners in which death occurs would be too long here, and whoever wishes for this will find it in the fourth part of Ptolemy’s *Quadripartitum* <note>The *Tetrabiblos*, Ptolemy's four-part work on astrology</note>. Ptolemy says in the 74th proposition of the *Centiloquium* <note>The "Hundred Sayings," a collection of astrological aphorisms attributed to Ptolemy</note>: When **Mars** is bodily joined to the **Head of Algol** <term>Algol: A fixed star in the constellation Perseus, traditionally associated with great misfortune and violence, particularly decapitation.</term> and the **Moon** does not look upon the **Ascendant** <note>the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, and there is no "Fortune" <note>a benefic planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> in the 8th house, and the **Lord of the Anareta** <term>Anareta: The planet that "destroys" or signifies the end of life.</term> of the luminaries is opposite Mars or in its fourth aspect <note>a square aspect, 90 degrees</note>, the head of the native shall be cut off. But if the luminaries are in the middle of the sky, he shall be hanged. And if there are evil planets looking from **Gemini** and **Pisces**, his hands and feet shall be cut off. 

**Haly** says in his commentary that when a "Fortune" <note>a benefic planet</note> is in the eighth house, it prevents a bad death. Indeed, I heard from a certain man who was with the son of Haly, who was very courageous at sea; when he wished to enter a sea that was exceedingly tumultuous with winds tossing the waves, and he had been rebuked for it, he said: "I do not fear death for myself in the sea. For Jupiter was in my **nativity** <term>Nativity: A birth chart or horoscope.</term> in the house of death and in the East, which prevents a bad death for me." And he said, "I saw that man die in his bed." It also happened to me that certain servants showed me the nativity of the son of their lord, and I found the **Sun** in the middle of the sky; and he himself was the Lord of the Anareta and **Plech** <note>An obscure astrological term, likely referring to a specific planetary position or lot</note> and Mars...

<summary>This page concludes marital predictions before detailing the astrological significations of the Eighth House (Death), including planetary causes of illness and indications of violent death versus natural death in bed.</summary>
<keywords>Eighth House, death, Ptolemy, Haly Abenragel, Algol, inheritance, medical astrology, nativity, triplicity lords, violent death</keywords>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the Eighth House (Death) and transitions into the Ninth House (Travel and Religion). It follows a discussion on how to predict the manner and timing of a person's end based on planetary positions.</meta>

...in the **fourth aspect** and **Saturn** in the **angle of the earth** <term>Angle of the earth: The fourth astrological house, also known as the Imum Coeli, representing one's roots, home, and the end of life.</term> with **Pisces** ascending. Because of this, I was terrified and delayed giving my judgment, saying that the "days of nursing" <note>The "days of nutrition" or nursing refers to a period in infancy where the child's survival is not yet certain; astrologers often waited until after this period to cast a full life-expectancy horoscope.</note> must be completed before I could judge. However, while I was very concerned about the boy and investigated his movements and character, I understood nothing that would suggest he should fear the cutting off of his hands and feet or being hanged <note>original: "infurcationem," referring to the gallows</note>, since he was gentle and modest. But when he reached 30 years of age, certain men who were fleeing because they had been accused of fraud against the city governor entered his house. They were caught in that house, and he, along with them, lost his hands and feet and was hanged. And I myself saw him without hands and feet, hanging.

**Ptolemy** says in the 59th proposition of the *Centiloquium* <note>The "Centiloquium" is a famous collection of 100 astrological aphorisms attributed to Ptolemy, though now believed to be by a later author.</note>: when you are asked about an absent person, do not judge him to be dead until you account for "drunkenness" <note>original: "ebrietatem," here implying a state of stupor or unconsciousness that mimics death</note>. Nor should you judge him wounded until you rule out bloodletting <note>original: "sanguis minutionem," a common medical practice</note>. Nor should you judge his wealth acquired until you account for money merely entrusted to him. For in these cases, the judgment for such men is the same. **Haly** <note>Haly Abenragel, a prominent 11th-century Arab astrologer</note> says in his commentary on this same point: he who deals with these signs judges by appearances that are close to the truth; therefore, in the skill of astrological judgment, no distinction is found between one who is sleeping and one who is dead, nor between one who is wounded and one who has been bled, nor between him to whom money is entrusted and him who has truly acquired it.

Indeed, I heard from my father (may God forgive him) that when he was with those who hid from the face of the Emperor, alongside **Abraham**, the son of **Almostbedi**, Abraham’s son **Albašten**, an astrologer, visited him secretly every day. He commanded them to bring a large bronze basin full of water and, placing a stool inside it, he advised him to sit upon it for the greater part of the day. He commanded this so that he might cause the Emperor's astrologers to err regarding the location of those in hiding. When the Emperor asked his astrologers where the man was, they said he was "in the middle of the sea" <note>This anecdote illustrates a "magical" use of astrology and physics to trick remote viewing or horary predictions.</note>.

**Haly Abenragel** says: when **Mars** is in the **eighth house** damaging the Lord of the eighth house, the death of the native shall be by the sword <note>original: "ex ferro," literally "from iron"</note>. And if **Saturn** is in the eighth house damaging the Lord of the eighth house, the death of the native shall be because of prison and punishments. When the **Moon** is in the eighth house with the **Dragon’s Tail** <term>Dragon's Tail: Also known as the South Node (Cauda Draconis), traditionally associated with loss, purging, or misfortune.</term>, it signifies that the death of the native is due to a "departure" <note>original: "secessum," which can mean a bowel movement or dysentery in a medical context</note> or a laxative medicine. When **Mercury** is in the eighth house with the Tail, it signifies that the death of the native will be because of evil deeds, or because of poison, or the works of necromancy.

When the **degree of the west** <note>The Descendant or the cusp of the 7th house</note> and its Lord are both afflicted, they signify a bad death for the native. When the **Sun** is afflicted in a daytime birth, or the **Moon** in a nighttime birth, it signifies the same thing. When the **significators** <term>Significator: A planet or point in a chart that represents a specific person or area of life.</term> are afflicted above the earth, they signify a manifest and public death. And when they are afflicted below the earth, the death will be hidden and secret. When any of the **malefics** <note>Saturn or Mars</note> are in the eighth house or joined to the Lord of the eighth house, it signifies a violent and bad death. And if any of the **benefics** <note>Jupiter or Venus</note> are in the eighth house or joined to the Lord of the eighth house, it signifies a good and beautiful death. And if **Jupiter** or **Venus** is in this house, strong and free from the rays of the malefics, the native will be fortunate and praised, and will live for 73 years, and perhaps more.

## The Ninth House. <insert>◆ Here he sets down the meanings of the ninth house.</insert>
He says that the **ninth house** signifies pilgrimages and long journeys; it also signifies epistles, messengers, rumors, and dreams. **Anduzgaz** <note>Likely Andarzaghar, a pre-Islamic Persian astrologer often cited in Latin translations</note> says that the first lord of the **triplicity** of this house signifies pilgrimages and everything that happens to one on journeys; the second signifies faith and religion; the third signifies wisdom and...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Ninth House—focusing on travel, religion, and dreams—and begins the analysis of the Tenth House, which governs career, authority, and kingship. It follows a previous section on death and the Eighth House.</meta>

...dreams. **Haly Abenragel** <note>Abu l-Hasan 'Ali ibn Abi l-Rijal (d. after 1037), a famous Arab astrologer whose work "The Complete Book on the Judgment of the Stars" was a standard medieval text.</note> says, regarding the conditions of this house, that you should look at the travels of the native <term>Native: The person for whom the birth chart is being cast.</term> first by the Lord of the Triplicity <term>Lord of the Triplicity: A planet that rules a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water).</term> of the house of travel. For when the first Lord of the Triplicity of the house of travel is in a good state, strong, fortunate, and well-received <note>original: "receptus," a technical term for a planet being in a sign ruled by another planet that "welcomes" it.</note>, it signifies that the native will move from one place to another through travels and will be fortunate in those journeys. If he is in a state opposite to what we have described, the native will have obstacles and hardships in his travels and movements; he will be held in low esteem in them, and he will be dragged out and expelled from his land and his place, nor will he have any usefulness or profit in his travels.

And if the second Lord of the Triplicity of the house of travel is in a good and fortunate state and well-received, the native will be of good character, religious, and a guardian of the law. If he is in the opposite state, he will be of bad character and be known as an evil man, performing wicked works and being an unbeliever. 

And if the third Lord of the Triplicity is in a good state, the native will have true dreams: whatever he sees in his dreams will be true, and he will not be deceived. The same **Haly** says you should know that **Mercury** has power and its own specific significance in matters of law and legality, separately from the other planets. And know that when **Mercury** is in the house of **Saturn** or in his aspect <term>Aspect: The specific geometric angle between two planets, thought to influence their power.</term>, the native will be of deep thoughts and firm in his beliefs; he praises the things of the other world more than this one, believes in them, thinks much on them, and loves them, holding that the things of the next world are better than those of this world. He hates games, jesting, and comforts; he is humble and patient in labor and hardship, especially if the "fortunate" planets are falling away <note>original: "fortune cadentes," referring to planets in the 3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th houses where their influence is weakened.</note> and not looking toward the Ascendant. 

And if **Mercury** is in the house of **Jupiter** or in his aspect, the native will be of good name, follow good laws and legality, and will be an observer of the law he follows. And if he is in the house of **Mars** or in his aspect—and if that aspect is a square or an opposition <note>original: "de quarto vel oppositione," referring to 90-degree and 180-degree angles, which were considered stressful or difficult.</note>—the native will be a murderer, easily angered, an evildoer of bad law, and will despise his own law.

# The Tenth House.

<margin>Here he places the meanings of the tenth house. He says that the tenth house...</margin>
<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'D' featuring leafy, stylized plant motifs within a rectangular frame.</image-desc>
The Tenth House is the house of kingship, social elevation, and the exaltation of memory; it concerns the voice in commanding, masteries, and mothers. It also signifies the middle years of life. **Andruzagar** <note>original: "Anduzgaz," a medieval Latin corruption of the name of a Sasanian Persian astrologer.</note> says of the Lords of the Triplicity of the Tenth House: The first signifies the work of the native, his exaltation, and the loftiness of his seat. The second signifies the voice of command and boldness in commanding. The third signifies its stability and durability—that is, how long he will last in his dominion. 

**Ptolemy** says in the 5th proposition of the *Centiloquium*: The ascendants of the enemies of a kingdom are cadent <term>Cadent: From the Latin "cadere" (to fall). These are houses that "fall away" from the powerful corners of a chart, representing weakness or hidden things.</term> from the ascendant of that same kingdom; and the ascendants of those who rule are in its angle. The ascendants of those who serve are in the succedent houses <term>Succedent: Houses that follow the angles, usually representing support or resources.</term> of the angle. Furthermore, the ascendants of cities at the time of their building indicate all things that will happen in them. Those ascendants that occur during the coronation of kings of certain cities pronounce all things that will be done under their reign. And the ascendant of the appearance of any sect in those specific cities demonstrates whatever will happen under that sect in those same cities. 

**Haly** says there in his commentary that "cadent" signs are those where there is no angle, or which come after an angle; there are four: namely, the twelfth, third, sixth, and ninth from the ascendant. And he says it is known to every astrologer that when the ascendant of a birth—or any of its angles—is the same as the ascendant or any of the angles of the beginning of a reign, and if that birth is of someone for whom kingship is fitting, kingship will be granted to him in that realm; and he will have honor in that one, and not in another. For indeed, matters...

<meta>This page concludes the discussion on the Tenth House regarding the fate of kingdoms and moves into a detailed analysis of the Eleventh and Twelfth Houses. It continues the use of citations from Haly Abenragel and Ptolemy to define the astrological influences on social standing, friendship, and hidden enemies.</meta>

...kings <note>The Latin text begins with "ges," likely the end of "Reges" (Kings) from the previous page.</note> of old observed the births of all those born in their kingdoms; and whenever they detected an **ascendant** <term>Ascendant: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth, considered the most important point in a chart.</term> in the house of the kingdom's enemies—either the sixth, third, or ninth house—they would kill that child if his kingdom was destined to be set against their own. And after a few intervening words, he says that the ascendant of any city is the sign which is rising when one begins to lay the first stone. The ascendant of a sect <note>original: "secte," referring here to a religious or philosophical group.</note> is the rising sign at the time when that sect begins to prevail over another sect and those living there have accepted it; and that signifies how long it will endure and everything that will happen to it for as long as it lasts. **Haly Abenragel** says when the sign of the **midheaven** <term>Midheaven: The highest point in the sky at birth, representing career, status, and public life.</term> is one of the fiery signs, especially if it is Leo or Aries, and the Sun is in it, and the **Lot of the King** <note>original: "ps regis," a mathematical point in a chart used to predict a person's authority.</note> is there in an aspect of the "fortunes" <note>original: "fortunaz," referring to the beneficial planets Jupiter and Venus.</note>, the one born will without any doubt be a king.

# The Eleventh House.

<margin>Here he sets out the meanings of the eleventh house. He says that the eleventh house is the house of fortune, trust, and praise; and of friends, ministers, and helpers; and it signifies the latter half of middle life.</margin>

**Anduzagaz** <note>An early Persian or Arabic astrological authority, often cited in medieval Latin translations.</note> said that the first **Lord of the Triplicity** <term>Lord of the Triplicity: A planet that rules a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water).</term> of the house of trust signifies trust itself. The second signifies friends. The third signifies their usefulness or profits. **Ptolemy** says in the 39th proposition of the *Centiloquium* <note>The "Book of one hundred sayings," a famous collection of astrological aphorisms attributed (likely falsely) to Claudius Ptolemy.</note>: that an affliction of the eleventh house and its lord during the enthronement of kings signifies what evil will happen among his counselors and his wealth; and in the same way, an affliction of the second house signifies that the common people will gain very little under that king. **Haly** says in his commentary: Astrologers have agreed upon this, that the tenth house in every arrangement of kings is his own place; the eleventh is that of his helpers and his wealth; the **ascendant** belongs to the common people; and the second house to the wealth of the common people. Therefore, an obstruction in the eleventh house hinders the counselors, and in the second house, it hinders the wealth of the people. 

**Haly Abenragel** says in his reasoning on this house: Look at the first Lord of the Triplicity of this house; if you find him "received" <term>Received: A technical term where one planet is in a sign ruled by another, creating a supportive relationship.</term>, fortunate, and strong, judge that the native will have many friends, gains, usefulness, and good through them. And if he is unfortunate and condemned, the native will be solitary, having no friendship with anyone, and he will withdraw and distance himself from those who seek his love and company. And if the second Lord of the Triplicity of this house is strong, fortunate, and received, the native will be fortunate, honored, and wealthy, abounding in a good state and a good life. And if he is different from this state, he will be hard-working, in want, and in manifest poverty and misery. And if the third Lord of the Triplicity of this house is fortunate, strong, and in a good state, the native will have children who remain after him in a good state, with wealth and his own fortune. And if he is unfortunate, weak, falling, and in a bad state, the native will have no son to remain after him, nor an heir of his generation. And know that the best state possible in the matter of friends is for the **Lord of the Ascendant** to be received by the Lord of the Eleventh House, with that same Lord of the Eleventh House being a "fortune" <note>A lucky planet like Jupiter or Venus.</note> and in a good state; for when it is thus, he will have love and agreement with his companions and friends.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'D' depicting a man in a scholar's cap and robes seated at a desk or lectern.</image-desc>
# The Twelfth House.

<margin>Here he sets out the meanings of the 12th house. And he says that the 12th house signifies enemies, labors, sadness, as well as envies, thefts, and schemes, and the beasts that are ridden; and it signifies the end [of life].</margin>

<summary>This page concludes the analysis of how planetary positions at birth or a city's founding predict the future of kingdoms and sects, then details the Eleventh House as the domain of friends, advisors, and social fortune. It begins the Twelfth House, which traditionally governs hidden enemies, suffering, and the end of life.</summary>
<keywords>Eleventh House, Twelfth House, Haly Abenragel, Ptolemy, Centiloquium, Triplicity, Ascendant, Midheaven, kingship, social fortune, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the end of the discussion on the Eleventh House (concerning pregnancy and luck) into the Twelfth House (concerning enemies and suffering), before providing a general summary of the colors, "joys," and relative strengths of all twelve astrological houses.</meta>

...of life <note>original: "vite"; likely the end of a word like "servitute" (slavery), which was associated with the 12th house, or a continuation of a point about the "length of life."</note>: and what happens to women in pregnancy. **Anduzgaz** <note>Andruzagar, an early Persian or Arab astrologer cited in medieval texts.</note> says that the first lord of the **triplicity** <term>Triplicity: A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water). Each house is governed by three "lords" of its element.</term> of this house signifies enemies; the second signifies labors; the third signifies large beasts; and he concludes his statement by saying: "this is what is signified in the Twelfth House." **Haly Abenragel** says in the judgments of this house: look first to the first lord of the triplicity of this house, and if you find him fortunate, strong, and in a high position, judge that the enemies of the native will support him and do whatever he wishes. But if he is **falling** <term>Falling: A planet in a weak position in the sky where it cannot express its power.</term>, **combust** <term>Combust: A planet so close to the Sun that its influence is "burned up" or weakened.</term>, or **peregrine** <term>Peregrine: A planet that has no essential dignity or strength in its current position.</term>, the native will lean upon his enemies, and they will hinder him; and the evil they believed they were doing to the native will happen to them instead. And if the lord of the triplicity of this house is fortunate, the native will have few sorrows and anxieties. But if he is in a contrary state, he will have many impieties and sorrows, and labors, hindrances, and great losses will happen to him in that which he holds in his body. And if the third lord of the triplicity of this house is fortunate, the native will be a **dialectician** <note>A skilled logical debater.</note> and of good eloquence, and happy in his reasons and causes; and he will prosper over anyone opposing him. But if he is in a contrary state, he will be of weak reason and hindered speech; he will not have justice against his adversaries and those who do him wrong.

## The Twelve Houses Also Signify Colors

Here he places the significations—that is, of the twelve houses—as far as colors are concerned. And he says that the 1st and 7th houses are white; the 2nd and 12th are green; the 3rd and 11th are yellow <note>original: "crocee," meaning saffron-colored.</note>; the 4th and 10th are red; the 5th and 9th are the color of honey; and the 6th and 8th are black. Afterwards, he says that each of the planets has in one of these houses a certain accidental power called a **joy** <term>Joy: A specific house where a planet is said to be particularly comfortable or "happy," enhancing its influence.</term>. For **Mercury** rejoices in the **Ascendant** <note>The 1st House.</note>; the **Moon** rejoices in the 3rd house; **Venus** in the 5th; **Mars** in the 6th; the **Sun** in the 9th; **Jupiter** in the 11th; and **Saturn** in the 12th. Then he says that the **angles** <term>Angles: The four most powerful houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th) which represent the cardinal directions of a chart.</term> signify strength and perfection. Those **falling** <term>Cadent: The houses (3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th) that "fall away" from the angles, generally considered weaker.</term> from the angles signify weakness and loss, except that the 9th and 3rd houses signify matters that are fit and revealed with fame; and this is because they are the "joys" of the **luminaries** <note>The Sun and Moon.</note>, as was just said: the Sun rejoices in the 9th and the Moon in the 3rd. And the 12th and 6th houses signify hidden things and the worthlessness of matters. Afterwards, he says that the angles—namely the 1st, 10th, 7th, and 4th houses—and the lords of those angles signify greatness of honor and fortune, and distance from downfall and misfortune. And the presence of the lords of the angles in houses falling from the angles signifies misfortune, disgrace, and downfall. And the 11th house, which follows the 10th, and its lord signify middling fortune—that is, it signifies fortune, but not as great as the 10th. And the 5th house, which follows the 4th, signifies middling fortune through gifts and the outcomes of children. And this house <note>The 6th or 11th, though likely referring to the 11th's role in support.</note> and its lord signify middling fortune by way of servants and helpers. And the 8th house and its lord signify middling fortune from the substance inherited from the dead and from hidden things. Afterwards, he describes the meanings of the lords of the angles when they are present in those very angles. And he says that the presence of the lord of the Ascendant in the **Ascendant** <note>The 1st House.</note> signifies one's fortune through oneself and one's own acquisition. And if he is in the 7th, it signifies acquisition through confrontations <note>original: "preventiones," also meaning coming before others or reaching out.</note> and wives. And if he is in the 10th, it signifies acquisition through masteries <note>Expert skills or high-ranking professions.</note>. And if he is in the 4th, it signifies acquisition through inheritances and by way of fathers, and through the production of waters, the population of lands, and from ancient and fundamental things <note>Another version <insert>original: "alia littera"</insert> has "from foundational things."</note>. Indeed, the presence of the lord of the 10th in the 10th house signifies fortune through the king and a great kingdom, and through high masteries. And if he is in the 7th, it signifies a kingdom through victory in disputes and through the outcomes of wives. And if he is in the 4th, it signifies a kingdom through the outcomes of the king’s ministers, the cultivation of lands, the building of cities, the dividing of rivers, the guarding of cities, and from ancient things. And if he is in the Ascendant, it signifies a kingdom through ingenuity and closeness to the king arising from the affairs of the common people.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of "house lords" (the planets that rule specific astrological houses). Having finished the descriptions of individual houses, the author now explains how the position of these lords in different parts of the chart creates specific outcomes, before moving on to a guide for choosing the "significator"—the specific planet that represents the subject of a query.</meta>

<sig>g ii</sig>

The presence of the lord of the seventh house <note>The 7th House typically governs marriage, partnerships, and open enemies.</note> in the seventh house signifies fortune through negotiations and through contracts and commendations—that is, deposits <note>original: "depositiones"; likely referring to goods or money held in trust</note>—and through women and through planters. And if it should be in the fourth, it signifies fortune through contracts and negotiations and through the affairs of fathers, inheritances, and the cultivation of the land. And if it should be in the ascendant <note>The 1st House, representing the self.</note>, it signifies fortune through contracts and negotiations and through the affairs of medicine and astronomy and through spiritual works and ingenious devices and other similar things. And if it should be present in the tenth, it signifies fortune through contracts and negotiations and wives and through the affairs of the kingdom.

The presence indeed of the lord of the fourth house <note>The 4th House governs the home, ancestors, and "the end of things."</note> in the fourth house signifies fortune from produce and through the affairs of fathers and through ancient things. And if it should be present in the ascendant, it signifies fortune from the cultivation of the land and produce, through the depth of counsel and ingenuity; and if it should be present in the tenth house, it signifies profit from the cultivation of the land and produce through the affairs of kings and masteries. And if it should be present in the 7th house, it signifies fortune from the cultivation of the land and produce and from the part of women and through the affairs of wives and planters and through negotiations.

Next, he says that these things we have mentioned signify the lords of the angles <term>Angles: The four most powerful houses in a chart: the 1st (Ascendant), 4th (Imum Coeli), 7th (Descendant), and 10th (Midheaven).</term> when they are present in those very angles. And just as has been said concerning the angles, so you should understand regarding the other houses in their own way, according to the nature of each house; and he says that he introduced the lords of the angles for this reason: so that they might serve as an example for the other houses.

<image-desc>A large historiated woodcut initial 'E' depicting a figure (likely a scholar) seated at a desk within an ornate rectangular border.</image-desc>

¶ And when you wish to know the ruling planet. Here the author teaches how to choose the **significator** <term>Significator: In astrology, the specific planet chosen to represent the person asking a question or the object they are asking about.</term>: and this can be divided into two parts. In the first part, he does what has been said. In the second part, he provides an example. For instance: the opinion of the author is this, that in identifying the significator of a matter, we ought to look to two things. First, we ought to look to the house in which the nature of that thing resides for which we seek a significator. Second, we ought to look to the planet that signifies that thing by its own nature. For example: if a question is made concerning substance <note>Wealth or personal possessions.</note>, we ought to look to the second house, which is the house of substance; and along with this, we ought to look to **Jupiter**, which signifies substance by its own proper nature. And if the question is about a woman, we ought to look to the seventh house, which is the house of women, and along with this, we ought to look to **Venus**, which by its own nature is the significator of women; and so for others according to the proper natures of the houses and the planets.

It is, however, to be understood that the planet we choose based on the house should be held as the principal significator. And the planet that signifies the nature of the thing [by itself] we ought to make a participant in the signification. This is therefore the meaning of the text: when you wish to know the ruling planet or the significator of a matter, look at which of the planets has the most **fortitudes** <term>Fortitudes: Also called "essential dignities," these are points of strength a planet gains based on its exact position in the zodiac.</term> in the house of the matter being sought, out of the fortitudes mentioned in the chapter on the fortitudes and dignities of planets. And that one which excels in the number of fortitudes is the ruler or significator. And it is understood that in interrogations <note>Horary astrology, where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked.</note>, the ascendant or first house is always attributed to the seeker, and the planet exceeding in the number of fortitudes in the house of the matter being questioned is the significator of the thing sought.

The author provides an example in the text. He says if you have been asked about substance, and you wish to know which of the planets is its ruler or significator, and the second house (which is the house of substance) is at the 5th degree of **Aries**: because Aries is the house of **Mars**, Mars has five fortitudes there. And it is the exaltation of the **Sun**, and therefore the Sun has four fortitudes by reason of that. And it is also the Sun’s tripli... <note>original: "tripli-"; the text cuts off, likely referring to the Sun's "triplicity" power in fire signs like Aries.</note>

<summary>This page explains how the "Lords" of the 4th and 7th houses provide different fortunes depending on where they are located. It then introduces the method for identifying a "significator" for an astrological question by weighing the technical strengths (fortitudes) of planets in a specific house.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the technical calculation of planetary dignities (strengths) and introduces the concept of "accidental" power before concluding the first major section of the book. It then transitions from theory to a practical example: a "horary" question about a missing person.</meta>

[...by its triplicity] <note>The text continues from the previous page's calculation of "strengths."</note> and by reason of that it has three strengths there; and it is the **term** <note>A specific division of a zodiac sign</note> of **Jupiter**, and by reason of that it has two strengths there; and the **face** <note>A 10-degree division of a sign, also called a decan</note> of **Mars**, and by reason of that it has one strength there.

The **Sun** therefore has 7 strengths there, **Mars** six, and **Jupiter** two: it is clear, therefore, that the Sun has more strengths there. And such a planet—namely, the one having the most strengths—is called the <term>almuten</term> <note>original: "almutam"; from Arabic "al-mubtazz," meaning the "victor" or the planet with the most influence over a specific point</note>, that is, the victor. Therefore, the Sun in such a chart would be the **significator of substance** <note>The planet representing the essence or wealth of the subject</note>, and the same method is to be used for the others.

# Concerning Also the Accidental Powers of the Planets.

<margin>This part follows in this way: Previously the author determined the essential dignities of the planets. Here he determines their accidental dignities, and proceeds very briefly, saying:</margin>

There is one accidental dignity of the planets which is called <term>hayz</term> <note>from Arabic "hayyiz," referring to a planet being in its preferred "domain" or "faction"</note>, and it is this: that a **diurnal** planet <note>Sun, Jupiter, Saturn</note> be above the earth by day and below the earth by night. And a **nocturnal** planet <note>Moon, Venus, Mars</note> be above the earth by night and below the earth by day. And also, if it is a masculine planet, that it be in a masculine sign, and a feminine planet in a feminine sign, then the planet is said to be in its *hayz*—that is, in its likeness. And its strength will then be like the strength of a man in his own place of perfection, profit, and fortune.

And thus is completed the explanation of the **first distinction** <note>The first major chapter or section of the book</note> of the **Introduction of Alchabitius** <note>Abu al-Saqr al-Qabisi, a famous 10th-century Arab astrologer whose work was a standard textbook in Medieval Europe</note> to the mastery of the judgments of the stars.

¶ A certain man inquired about an absent person, whether he was dead or alive; and the **ascendant** <note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question</note> of the inquiry was the 20th degree of **Leo**, and such a figure <note>astrological chart</note> occurred:

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological judgment from the previous section, transitioning from a specific case study (a horary question about a missing person) into a general textbook-style explanation of planetary natures and "Firdaria" (planetary periods of life).</meta>

<page-type>text</page-type>
<sig>g iij</sig>

I examined this question and I assigned the Ascendant <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the inquiry, representing the person being asked about</note> and its ruler, as well as the Moon, to the absent person for whom the question was made. I looked and found the ruler of the Ascendant in the Midheaven <note>The highest point of the southern sky in a chart</note>; because of this, it seemed at first glance that he might be living in prosperity. However, because I found Saturn in the eastern angle <note>The First House</note> near the angle within two degrees, I specifically doubted his life—especially because in that same place, Saturn afflicted the Part of Fortune <note>An Arabic Part or mathematical point in a chart representing well-being and luck</note>. Next, I looked to the House of Death <note>The Eighth House</note> and found Venus to be stronger there; that is, in the House of Death, she possessed more dignities, namely exaltation and triplicity <note>Technical terms for a planet being in a position of strength within a specific zodiac sign</note>. I looked at her place in the chart and how she related to the Ascendant, the ruler of the Ascendant, and the Moon. I found her aspecting the Ascendant with a square aspect <note>original: "aspectu quarto" — an unfavorable 90-degree angle</note>, and Saturn was existing there in the same aspect, which made me doubt his life even more.

Then I looked at the Moon and found her "oppressed" under the rays of the Sun <note>original: "sub radijs solis" — combustion, a condition where a planet is too close to the sun to be seen, considered a great weakness</note>; this was one sign of death. I also found the Moon separated from Venus and going toward a conjunction with the ruler of the Ascendant, and she transferred the nature of the ruler of the House of Death to the ruler of the Ascendant. This was a more certain sign of death. From these factors, I concluded that the man for whom the question was made was dead; and so it was found to be. And because Saturn, located in the Ascendant, was the ruler of the seventh house (the significator of the wife of the man in question), and Venus (the significatrix of death) aspected him, it signified that perhaps the killer had committed adultery with the wife of the man for whom the question was made.

<margin>This is the second distinction of this book.</margin>

# Second Distinction on the natures of the seven planets.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'D' featuring a central leaf motif and floral patterns within a square frame.</image-desc>

**D**istinction in which the author determines the natures of the seven planets. It can be divided into four parts: first, he determines the natures and properties of the seven planets. Second, he determines the order or lordship of the planets in conceptions. Third, the nature of the Head and Tail of the Dragon <note>The Lunar Nodes, where the moon's orbit crosses the ecliptic</note>. Fourth, he determines the order of the planets as rulers of days and hours. The second part begins at the words: "concerning the order of the planets." The third at: "the head of the dragon." The fourth at: "if, however, there should be any day." The first part is divided into seven parts: first, he sets down the nature of Saturn; second, Jupiter; third, Mars; fourth, the Sun; fifth, Venus; sixth, Mercury; and seventh, the Moon. Where these parts begin is clear in the text. First, he sets down the nature of Saturn and what it signifies regarding objects, human character, social sects, and so on; all of which appear clearly in the text up to that part concerning the years of the <term>Firdaria</term> <note>original: "fridarie" — a system of planetary periods governing different stages of a person's life</note>.

Regarding this part, it must be understood that when the text says "the years of the Firdaria of Saturn are 11," it refers to the years of governance in the life of the native <note>The person whose birth chart is being read</note>. For the sake of clarity, it should be known that in a daytime birth, the Firdaria begins with the Sun, which governs for 10 years. Afterward, Venus governs according to the number of her years. Then Mercury, and so on, following the order of the planets until the end of life. If, however, the birth was at night, the governance begins with the Moon, which governs according to the number of her years. After the Moon, Saturn governs for the number of his years, and so on according to the order of the planets. There is a specific chapter on this matter in the fourth distinction, where it will be discussed more extensively, God willing. When, however, it says in the text: "the greatest years..."
